- [anatomical, likely] Anatomical: the rhomboids and middle trapezius are the primary scapular retractors; the lower trapezius and latissimus dorsi (via its scapular attachment and humeral pull) depress the scapula. — Functional anatomy consensus (e.g. Neumann DA. Kinesiology of the Musculoskeletal System, 3rd ed., 2017, ch. 5).

Notes: Both current mappings are essentially right. The mid-back shrug moved from 0.8/0.2 to 0.6/0.4 because retraction (not depression) is a shared middle-trapezius/rhomboid task; the scapular pull-up keeps 0.8/0.2 and lats 0.5 because depression is lower-trapezius work. No delt_rear credit: the arms do not move at the glenohumeral joint.

## biceps

### Supinated curl, arm at side (barbell / EZ / dumbbell / cable / machine)

`supinated_curl_neutral_shoulder` · 15 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Elbow flexion with a supinated (or partly supinated, EZ) grip and the upper arm hanging roughly vertical at the side of the torso. Biceps brachii is the primary mover with brachialis as the constant deep synergist; brachioradialis contributes as a secondary elbow flexor; wrist flexors only hold the wrist isometrically.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| biceps | 1 {biceps 0.75, brachialis 0.25} | Biceps brachii is the dominant flexor when the forearm is supinated (highest biceps excitation of all grips; brachialis activity is lowest in supination). Brachialis still works in every curl, so it keeps a quarter share. |
| forearms | 0.25 {forearm_flexors 0.2, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.8} | Brachioradialis is a genuine elbow flexor that is active in all grips (slightly higher in supination in two EMG studies) and deserves colour, but it is not the target; wrist flexors work only isometrically, so they get a small share of a small credit rather than a credit of their own. |

Exercises: Barbell Curl, Close-Grip EZ Bar Curl, Close-Grip EZ-Bar Curl with Band, Close-Grip Standing Barbell Curl, Drag Curl, Dumbbell Alternate Bicep Curl, Dumbbell Bicep Curl, EZ-Bar Curl, Lying Cable Curl, Seated Dumbbell Curl, Seated Dumbbell Inner Biceps Curl, Standing Biceps Cable Curl, Standing Inner-Biceps Curl, Standing One-Arm Cable Curl, Wide-Grip Standing Barbell Curl.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Bilateral barbell curl in bodybuilders: biceps brachii excitation was 19% higher supinated vs pronated and 12% higher vs neutral; brachioradialis excitation was slightly higher (5-6%) with the supinated grip than with pronated or neutral. — Coratella G, Tornatore G, Longo S, Toninelli N, Padovan R, Esposito F, Cè E. Biceps Brachii and Brachioradialis Excitation in Biceps Curl Exercise: Different Handgrips, Different Synergy. Sports (Basel). 2023;11(3):64. PMID 36976950.
- [emg, verified] Brachialis and brachioradialis EMG falls as the forearm supinates while biceps EMG rises; biceps contraction level is higher with the forearm supine, the reverse for brachialis and brachioradialis. — Naito A, Sun YJ, Yajima M, Fukamachi H, Ushikoshi K. Electromyographic study of the elbow flexors and extensors in a motion of forearm pronation/supination while maintaining elbow flexion in humans. Tohoku J Exp Med. 1998;186(4):267-77. PMID 10328159.
- [emg, verified] Brachioradialis is EMG-active throughout resisted elbow flexion in neutral, pronated and supinated forearm positions, peaking near end-range flexion, and slightly higher in supination; it is one of the main elbow flexors, not a mere stabiliser. — Caufriez B, Dugailly PM, Brassinne E, Schuind F. The Role of the Muscle Brachioradialis in Elbow Flexion: An Electromyographic Study. J Hand Surg Asian Pac Vol. 2018;23(1):102-110. PMID 29409427.
- [emg, verified] EZ-bar, straight-bar and dumbbell curls activate both biceps brachii and brachioradialis; EZ and barbell produce slightly higher EMG than dumbbell, but differences between EZ and straight bar are small - implement does not change which muscles are credited. — Marcolin G, Panizzolo FA, Petrone N, Moro T, Grigoletto D, Piccolo D, Paoli A. Differences in electromyographic activity of biceps brachii and brachioradialis while performing three variants of curl. PeerJ. 2018;6:e5165. PMID 30013836.
- [emg, verified] After 4 sets of dumbbell curls to failure, biceps brachii swelled more (18.7%) than brachialis (12.3%), supporting a biceps-biased region split for the supinated curl. — Leitão BFM, Albarello JCS, Halmenschlager GH, de Salles BF, Matta TT. Exercise selection plays an important role in inducing non-uniform acute responses on elbow flexors muscle thickness. J Bodyw Mov Ther. 2024;40:1097-1101. PMID 39593419.
- [emg, verified] Brachialis can be recorded separately from biceps with surface EMG and predicts flexion force as well as biceps during pure flexion, i.e. it is a co-primary flexor in every curl. — Staudenmann D, Taube W. Brachialis muscle activity can be assessed with surface electromyography. J Electromyogr Kinesiol. 2015;25(2):199-204. PMID 25468488.
- [anatomical, verified] The short head of the biceps is relatively more efficient at elbow flexion; the long head is the more efficient supinator when the forearm is supinated. Grip width has no verified EMG or hypertrophy evidence for head-selective loading, so close/wide grips keep the family mapping. — Jarrett CD, Weir DM, Stuffmann ES, Jain S, Miller MC, Schmidt CC. Anatomic and biomechanical analysis of the short and long head components of the distal biceps tendon. J Shoulder Elbow Surg. 2012;21(7):942-8. PMID 21813298.

Notes: Kept biceps 1.0 as current. Changed region split from 0.8/0.2 to 0.75/0.25 (brachialis is a co-primary flexor in all grips) and made the forearm credit consistent across the family: current rows are inconsistent (some 0.5 at 50/50, some none). Forearm credit is now 0.25 biased to brachioradialis because the wrist flexors only stabilise isometrically. Drag_Curl adds shoulder extension through the rep (long head at longer length) but recruits the same muscles, so no override. Lying_Cable_Curl (supine on the floor, low pulley at the feet) keeps the shoulder near neutral and belongs here rather than with incline curls. Wide-, close- and 'inner-biceps' variants: no override, see evidence on grip width.

### Incline / supine dumbbell curl (shoulder extended, long-length)

`incline_curl_shoulder_extended` · 5 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Supinated curl with the upper arm behind the torso line (incline bench 45-60 degrees or lying supine with arms hanging). Puts the biarticular biceps, especially the long head, at a longer length and shifts growth proximally; brachialis is unaffected by shoulder angle.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| biceps | 1 {biceps 0.8, brachialis 0.2} | Shoulder extension lengthens only the biarticular biceps, so emphasis tilts a little further toward biceps brachii than the standing curl; brachialis credit stays small but non-zero. |
| forearms | 0.25 {forearm_flexors 0.2, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.8} | Same brachioradialis synergist role as any supinated curl; wrist flexors only isometric. |

Exercises: Alternate Incline Dumbbell Curl, Flexor Incline Dumbbell Curls, Incline Dumbbell Curl, Incline Inner Biceps Curl, Lying Supine Dumbbell Curl.

Evidence:

- [hypertrophy, verified] 8 weeks of incline curls produced greater proximal elbow-flexor thickness gains than preacher curls, while preacher curls produced greater distal gains; total elbow flexor growth occurred with both. — Kassiano W, Costa B, Kunevaliki G, et al. Distinct muscle growth and strength adaptations after preacher and incline biceps curls. Int J Sports Med. 2025;46(5):334-343. PMID 39809454.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Training elbow flexors at extended joint angles (0-50 degrees, long length) increased biceps+brachialis thickness 8.9% vs 3.4% for flexed-angle training over 5 weeks. — Sato S, Yoshida R, Kiyono R, et al. Elbow Joint Angles in Elbow Flexor Unilateral Resistance Exercise Training Determine Its Effects on Muscle Strength and Thickness of Trained and Non-trained Arms. Front Physiol. 2021;12:734509. PMID 34616309.
- [hypertrophy, verified] When resistance profile and elbow ROM were matched, cable curls at maximal shoulder extension vs neutral shoulder produced equivalent elbow-flexor hypertrophy (7-9%) - shoulder angle changes emphasis, not which muscles are credited. — Larsen S, Sandvik Kristiansen B, Osteras Sandberg N, et al. The effects of shoulder extension angle on elbow flexor hypertrophy in the cable curl exercise. Front Physiol. 2026;17:1750722. PMID 41988507.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Incline dumbbell curl training (3 weeks LL-BFR) increased elbow flexor CSA at 45%, 65% and 85% humerus length - confirms it is a whole-elbow-flexor exercise. — Vendruscolo LDS, Brendon H, Larrain VH, Aihara AY, de Salles Painelli V. Protein supplementation optimizes muscle strength and hypertrophic responses induced by low-load training with blood flow restriction in resistance-trained individuals. Clin Nutr ESPEN. 2025;67:206-216. PMID 40107357.
- [emg, verified] Incline dumbbell curl and standard dumbbell curl produced similar biceps long-head EMG patterns across the full ROM (up to ~95% max RMS), unlike the preacher curl. — Oliveira LF, Matta TT, Alves DS, Garcia MAC, Vieira TMM. Effect of the shoulder position on the biceps brachii EMG in different dumbbell curls. J Sports Sci Med. 2009;8(1):24-9. PMID 24150552.

Notes: Current biceps 0.8/0.2 is kept - it is right for this family. Added the same small forearm credit as the other supinated curls for consistency (current rows have it on Alternate_Incline but not on Incline_Dumbbell_Curl). Lying_Supine_Dumbbell_Curl (arms hanging off a flat bench) is biomechanically the extreme incline curl. 'Flexor' and 'Inner biceps' name variants have no evidence for a different mapping.

### Preacher / spider / concentration / machine curl (shoulder flexed, arm braced)

`braced_arm_curl_preacher_spider_concentration` · 14 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Supinated curl with the upper arm fixed in front of the torso (preacher pad, prone incline bench, elbow on thigh, machine arm pad). Shoulder flexion shortens the biarticular biceps, bias shifts toward the distal elbow flexors (brachialis and distal biceps); tension peaks in the stretched/mid range.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| biceps | 1 {biceps 0.7, brachialis 0.3} | Preacher-style training grows the distal elbow flexors more than incline curls and produced a slightly larger brachialis than biceps response in a matched cable study, so brachialis share rises to 0.3 while biceps remains the primary. |
| forearms | 0.25 {forearm_flexors 0.2, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.8} | Brachioradialis synergist as in every supinated curl; wrist flexors isometric only. |

Exercises: Barbell Curls Lying Against An Incline, Cable Preacher Curl, Concentration Curls, Dumbbell Prone Incline Curl, Lying High Bench Barbell Curl, Machine Bicep Curl, Machine Preacher Curls, One Arm Dumbbell Preacher Curl, Preacher Curl, Seated Close-Grip Concentration Barbell Curl, Spider Curl, Standing Concentration Curl, Standing One-Arm Dumbbell Curl Over Incline Bench, Two-Arm Dumbbell Preacher Curl.

Evidence:

- [hypertrophy, verified] Preacher curls produced greater distal elbow-flexor thickness gains than incline curls over 8 weeks (mean difference 0.10 cm), incline greater proximal gains. — Kassiano W, Costa B, Kunevaliki G, et al. Distinct muscle growth and strength adaptations after preacher and incline biceps curls. Int J Sports Med. 2025;46(5):334-343. PMID 39809454.
- [hypertrophy, verified] 10 weeks of cable preacher curl (shoulder ~50 degrees flexed) increased biceps brachii thickness 6-7% and brachialis 10%; Bayesian (shoulder extended) curl 9% biceps and 8% brachialis; no significant between-condition difference in total growth. — Attarieh P, Nunes JP, Khani S, et al. Comparison Between Shoulder Flexed and Extended Positions in Elbow Flexion Resistance Training on Regional Hypertrophy and Maximum Strength: Preacher versus Bayesian Cable Curls. Eur J Sport Sci. 2025;25(4):e12279. PMID 40082069.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Seated dumbbell preacher curl training for 12 weeks increased elbow flexor CSA 8% (1 set) to 13% (3 sets) by MRI - preacher curls are a full-credit elbow flexor exercise. — Sooneste H, Tanimoto M, Kakigi R, Saga N, Katamoto S. Effects of training volume on strength and hypertrophy in young men. J Strength Cond Res. 2013;27(1):8-13. PMID 23249767.
- [hypertrophy, verified] In the preacher curl, training the initial (stretched) 0-68 degree range grew distal biceps CSA more than the final range and gave larger full-ROM strength gains. — Pedrosa GF, Simoes MG, Figueiredo MOC, et al. Training in the Initial Range of Motion Promotes Greater Muscle Adaptations Than at Final in the Arm Curl. Sports (Basel). 2023;11(2):39. PMID 36828324.
- [emg, verified] Dumbbell preacher curl elicited high biceps long-head EMG (~80% max RMS) only over a short range near the start of the movement, versus the full-ROM activity of incline and standing curls - consistent with a relatively smaller biceps-long-head share. — Oliveira LF, Matta TT, Alves DS, Garcia MAC, Vieira TMM. Effect of the shoulder position on the biceps brachii EMG in different dumbbell curls. J Sports Sci Med. 2009;8(1):24-9. PMID 24150552.
- [consensus, unverified] The widely quoted ACE-commissioned concentration-curl EMG comparison (concentration curl highest biceps activation of eight curl variants) is a non-peer-reviewed report; it supports keeping the concentration curl at full biceps credit but does not justify a distinct mapping. — Young S, Porcari JP, Camic C, Kovacs A, Foster C. ACE-sponsored research: Best biceps exercises. ACE ProSource / American Council on Exercise, 2014.

Notes: Biceps 1.0 kept; region split moved from 0.8/0.2 to 0.7/0.3 on the strength of two 2025 hypertrophy trials that show a distal/brachialis tilt for shoulder-flexed curls. Concentration curls (elbow braced on the thigh or arm hanging while bent over) and spider/prone-incline curls are biomechanically the same shoulder-flexed, fixed-humerus pattern and are grouped here. Barbell_Curls_Lying_Against_An_Incline and Lying_High_Bench_Barbell_Curl are prone (chest on the bench) with the arms hanging forward, i.e. spider curls, not incline curls. Machine_Bicep_Curl (seated arm-pad machine) is placed here; if the app's machine is a free-arm cable machine, move it to the standing family - the mapping difference is only the 0.05 brachialis share. Standing_Concentration_Curl and Concentration_Curls lose the current forearms 0.5 at 50/50 in favour of the family's 0.25 brachioradialis-biased credit.

### Hammer / neutral-grip curl (dumbbell, rope, cross-body, incline, preacher)

`hammer_neutral_grip_curl` · 6 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Elbow flexion with the forearm in neutral (thumbs up). Biceps loses part of its mechanical advantage, so brachialis and brachioradialis carry a larger share of the flexion torque; biceps still contributes substantially.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| biceps | 1 {biceps 0.5, brachialis 0.5} | Biceps excitation drops ~12% vs supinated while brachialis activity rises in non-supinated positions; an even split reflects the shared load. |
| forearms | 0.5 {forearm_flexors 0.2, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.8} | Brachioradialis is at its mechanically strongest in neutral and is the reason lifters choose hammer curls for forearm size; standard half credit, biased to the brachioradialis region. |

Exercises: Alternate Hammer Curl, Cable Hammer Curls - Rope Attachment, Cross Body Hammer Curl, Hammer Curls, Incline Hammer Curls, Preacher Hammer Dumbbell Curl.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Neutral-grip barbell curl produced 12% lower biceps brachii excitation than the supinated grip in bodybuilders; brachioradialis remained highly active in all grips. — Coratella G, Tornatore G, Longo S, Toninelli N, Padovan R, Esposito F, Cè E. Biceps Brachii and Brachioradialis Excitation in Biceps Curl Exercise: Different Handgrips, Different Synergy. Sports (Basel). 2023;11(3):64. PMID 36976950.
- [emg, verified] Brachialis and brachioradialis EMG increases as the forearm moves out of supination while biceps EMG decreases (reciprocal synergy among the flexors). — Naito A, Yajima M, Fukamachi H, Ushikoshi K, Sun YJ, Shimizu Y. Electromyographic (EMG) study of the elbow flexors during supination and pronation of the forearm. Tohoku J Exp Med. 1995;175(4):285-8. PMID 7570586.
- [emg, verified] Brachioradialis contribution to elbow flexion increases when the biceps is at a biomechanical disadvantage (pronated hand); the biceps did not fully compensate across hand positions. — Kleiber T, Kunz L, Disselhorst-Klug C. Muscular coordination of biceps brachii and brachioradialis in elbow flexion with respect to hand position. Front Physiol. 2015;6:215. PMID 26300781.
- [emg, verified] Brachioradialis is a main elbow flexor in the neutral forearm position, active through the full resisted flexion arc. — Caufriez B, Dugailly PM, Brassinne E, Schuind F. The Role of the Muscle Brachioradialis in Elbow Flexion: An Electromyographic Study. J Hand Surg Asian Pac Vol. 2018;23(1):102-110. PMID 29409427.
- [consensus, unverified] No longitudinal study has compared biceps vs brachialis vs brachioradialis hypertrophy after neutral-grip vs supinated curls; the 50/50 split and the 0.5 forearm credit are EMG-plus-anatomy estimates. — Practitioner consensus (hammer curl = brachialis/brachioradialis emphasis), e.g. Schoenfeld BJ. Science and Development of Muscle Hypertrophy, 2nd ed. Human Kinetics, 2021.

Notes: Current biceps 0.5/0.5 is kept - it matches the EMG picture. The forearm credit is made consistent (current: Hammer_Curls, Cable_Hammer_Curls and Incline_Hammer_Curls have none; the others have 0.5 at 50/50) and re-biased to the brachioradialis region because that is the muscle actually doing flexion work; wrist flexors only grip. Cross_Body_Hammer_Curl adds slight shoulder rotation but no different muscle credit. Incline_Hammer_Curls and Preacher_Hammer_Dumbbell_Curl: shoulder angle changes biceps length but the neutral grip is the dominant factor, so no override.

### Reverse (pronated-grip) curl (barbell, EZ preacher, cable, plate, dumbbell)

`reverse_pronated_curl` · 5 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Elbow flexion with a fully pronated grip. Biceps is at its greatest mechanical disadvantage; brachialis and brachioradialis become the primary flexors and the wrist extensors work hard to keep the wrist from collapsing into flexion under the bar.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| biceps | 1 {biceps 0.35, brachialis 0.65} | Brachialis is the workhorse when the forearm is pronated; biceps excitation is ~19% lower than supinated but still substantial, so the group keeps full credit with a brachialis-biased split. |
| forearms | 0.75 {forearm_flexors 0.1, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.9} | Brachioradialis contribution is significantly higher in the pronated hand position and the wrist extensors are loaded throughout; strong-synergist credit, nearly all to the brachioradialis/extensor region. |

Exercises: Reverse Barbell Curl, Reverse Barbell Preacher Curls, Reverse Cable Curl, Reverse Plate Curls, Standing Dumbbell Reverse Curl.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Brachioradialis contribution to elbow flexion is significantly greater in the pronated hand position than in supinated or neutral positions. — Kleiber T, Kunz L, Disselhorst-Klug C. Muscular coordination of biceps brachii and brachioradialis in elbow flexion with respect to hand position. Front Physiol. 2015;6:215. PMID 26300781.
- [emg, verified] Pronated-grip barbell curl produced 19% lower biceps brachii excitation than the supinated grip, with anterior deltoid stabilisation increasing. — Coratella G, Tornatore G, Longo S, Toninelli N, Padovan R, Esposito F, Cè E. Biceps Brachii and Brachioradialis Excitation in Biceps Curl Exercise: Different Handgrips, Different Synergy. Sports (Basel). 2023;11(3):64. PMID 36976950.
- [emg, verified] Brachialis contraction level and gain are higher with the forearm prone than supine; biceps the reverse. — Naito A, Sun YJ, Yajima M, Fukamachi H, Ushikoshi K. Electromyographic study of the elbow flexors and extensors in a motion of forearm pronation/supination while maintaining elbow flexion in humans. Tohoku J Exp Med. 1998;186(4):267-77. PMID 10328159.
- [anatomical, verified] In the pronated forearm the short head of the biceps has the larger supination moment arm and the biceps as a whole is a less efficient flexor - anatomical basis for the reduced biceps share. — Jarrett CD, Weir DM, Stuffmann ES, Jain S, Miller MC, Schmidt CC. Anatomic and biomechanical analysis of the short and long head components of the distal biceps tendon. J Shoulder Elbow Surg. 2012;21(7):942-8. PMID 21813298.
- [consensus, unverified] No longitudinal hypertrophy study isolates reverse curls; the brachialis/brachioradialis emphasis rests on EMG and anatomy plus long-standing practitioner use of reverse curls as a brachioradialis exercise. — Practitioner consensus, e.g. Schoenfeld BJ. Science and Development of Muscle Hypertrophy, 2nd ed. Human Kinetics, 2021.

Notes: Corrects three current rows: Reverse_Barbell_Preacher_Curls (biceps 0.8/0.2), Reverse_Cable_Curl and Reverse_Plate_Curls (biceps 0.8/0.2 and forearms 50/50) are anatomically wrong for a pronated grip - they should match Reverse_Barbell_Curl / Standing_Dumbbell_Reverse_Curl. Forearm credit raised from 0.5 to 0.75 because this is the variant users log specifically for forearms and brachioradialis becomes a primary mover. Reverse_Barbell_Preacher_Curls could carry brachialis 0.7 by the preacher logic, but the difference is within noise, so no override.

### Zottman curl (supinated concentric, pronated eccentric)

`zottman_curl` · 2 exercises · confidence low, unverified

Curl up with a supinated grip, rotate to pronated at the top, lower pronated. Concentric loads biceps as a supinated curl; eccentric loads brachialis, brachioradialis and wrist extensors as a reverse curl. Credit is a blend of the two families.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| biceps | 1 {biceps 0.55, brachialis 0.45} | Half the rep is a supinated curl (biceps-dominant) and half a pronated eccentric (brachialis-dominant); blend of the two family splits. |
| forearms | 0.5 {forearm_flexors 0.15, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.85} | The pronated eccentric loads brachioradialis and wrist extensors hard (eccentric phase carries a large share of hypertrophic stimulus), so forearm credit sits between the supinated (0.25) and reverse (0.75) families. |

Exercises: Zottman Curl, Zottman Preacher Curl.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Supinated grip maximises biceps excitation; pronated grip reduces it ~19% and shifts load to brachioradialis - the two halves of a Zottman rep. — Coratella G, Tornatore G, Longo S, Toninelli N, Padovan R, Esposito F, Cè E. Biceps Brachii and Brachioradialis Excitation in Biceps Curl Exercise: Different Handgrips, Different Synergy. Sports (Basel). 2023;11(3):64. PMID 36976950.
- [emg, verified] Brachioradialis contribution is significantly higher with the pronated hand, i.e. during the Zottman lowering phase. — Kleiber T, Kunz L, Disselhorst-Klug C. Muscular coordination of biceps brachii and brachioradialis in elbow flexion with respect to hand position. Front Physiol. 2015;6:215. PMID 26300781.
- [emg, verified] Reciprocal biceps vs brachialis/brachioradialis activity as the forearm rotates between supination and pronation under load. — Naito A, Yajima M, Fukamachi H, Ushikoshi K, Sun YJ, Shimizu Y. Electromyographic (EMG) study of the elbow flexors during supination and pronation of the forearm. Tohoku J Exp Med. 1995;175(4):285-8. PMID 7570586.
- [anatomical, likely] No study has measured the Zottman curl directly; mapping is an interpolation of the supinated and pronated families. — Anatomical/biomechanical reasoning from the grip studies above.

Notes: Current 0.5/0.5 with forearms 0.5 at 50/50 is close; tightened the split slightly toward biceps (the concentric, where most lifters fail, is supinated) and re-biased the forearm distribution to the brachioradialis/extensor region since that is what the pronated eccentric loads. Zottman_Preacher_Curl keeps the family mapping.

Curl performed with the upper arm held at or above shoulder height (double-biceps pose position, or lying under a high pulley). - [hypertrophy, verified] Shoulder-flexed (preacher) cable curls produced slightly larger brachialis (10%) than biceps (6-7%) thickness gains, supporting a brachialis tilt when the biceps is shortened at the shoulder. — Attarieh P, Nunes JP, Khani S, et al. Comparison Between Shoulder Flexed and Extended Positions in Elbow Flexion Resistance Training on Regional Hypertrophy and Maximum Strength: Preacher versus Bayesian Cable Curls. Eur J Sport Sci. 2025;25(4):e12279. PMID 40082069.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Preacher (shoulder flexed) curls grew the distal elbow flexors more and the proximal region less than incline curls. — Kassiano W, Costa B, Kunevaliki G, et al. Distinct muscle growth and strength adaptations after preacher and incline biceps curls. Int J Sports Med. 2025;46(5):334-343. PMID 39809454.

Notes: Current 0.8/0.2 changed to 0.7/0.3 to match the other shoulder-flexed family. No deltoid credit: holding the arms up is isometric stabilisation (the brief excludes it). Lying_Close-Grip_Bar_Curl_On_High_Pulley (supine, pulling the bar toward the forehead from a high pulley) has the shoulder flexed ~90 degrees and belongs here rather than with lying incline curls.

## chest

### Flat bilateral press (barbell / Smith / machine / cable)

`flat_barbell_bench_press` · 11 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Supine or seated horizontal press with a fixed-width bilateral implement: shoulder horizontal adduction + flexion from ~90 deg abduction with elbow extension. Torso horizontal, bar path to mid/lower sternum.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| chest | 1 {chest_upper 0.25, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.25} | Anchor row. At 0 deg all three pec portions sit at ~27% MVIC (Rodriguez-Ridao 2020) and the flat press grows the 3rd/5th intercostal sites more than the 2nd (Chaves 2020), so a sternal-centred split with equal upper/lower tails is right. |
| delt_front | 0.5 | Anchor row. AD ~26% MVIC vs pec ~27% at 0 deg (Rodriguez-Ridao 2020); AD CSA +15% vs PM +20% after 10 wk bench-only training (Lanza 2024). |
| triceps | 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} | Anchor row. Bench-only training grew the lateral head but not the long head (Brandao 2020); triceps MTH rises after bench training but later and less than pec (Ogasawara 2012; Lanza 2024 TB +12% vs PM +20%). |

Exercises: Barbell Bench Press - Medium Grip, Barbell Guillotine Bench Press, Bench Press - With Bands, Cable Chest Press, Chain Press, Leverage Chest Press, Machine Bench Press, Neck Press, Smith Machine Bench Press, Standing Cable Chest Press, Wide-Grip Barbell Bench Press.

Evidence:

- [hypertrophy, verified] 10 weeks of bench-press-only training increased CSA of pectoralis major (~20%), anterior deltoid (~15%), triceps brachii (~12%) and pec minor; PM grew more than TB. — Lanza MB, Prado GC, Lacerda LT, et al. Muscle hypertrophy response across four muscles involved in the bench press exercise: Randomized 10 weeks training intervention. J Bodyw Mov Ther. 2024;40:1417-1422. PMID 39593465.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Barbell bench press alone produced significant lateral-head triceps CSA gains but no significant long-head gains; lying triceps extension was needed for the long head. — Brandao L, de Salles Painelli V, Lasevicius T, et al. Varying the Order of Combinations of Single- and Multi-Joint Exercises Differentially Affects Resistance Training Adaptations. J Strength Cond Res. 2020;34(5):1254-1263. PMID 32149887.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Pectoralis major thickness increased from week 1 of bench training; triceps thickness increased later (week 5); biceps unchanged. — Ogasawara R, Thiebaud RS, Loenneke JP, Loftin M, Abe T. Time course for arm and chest muscle thickness changes following bench press training. Interv Med Appl Sci. 2012;4(4):217-220. PMID 24265879.
- [emg, verified] Flat (0 deg) bench: upper, middle and lower pec portions all ~27% MVIC, AD ~26%, triceps ~15%; middle and lower portions peak at 0 deg. — Rodriguez-Ridao S, Antequera-Vique JA, Martin-Fuentes I, Muyor JM. Effect of Five Bench Inclinations on the Electromyographic Activity of the Pectoralis Major, Anterior Deltoid, and Triceps Brachii during the Bench Press Exercise. Int J Environ Res Public Health. 2020;17(19):7339. PMID 33049982.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Horizontal bench training for 8 weeks grew the 3rd and 5th intercostal sites (+6.1 / +5.4 mm) more than the 2nd intercostal site (+3.8 mm). — Chaves SFN, Rocha-Junior VA, Encarnacao IGA, et al. Effects of Horizontal and Incline Bench Press on Neuromuscular Adaptations in Untrained Young Men. Int J Exerc Sci. 2020;13(6):859-872. PMID 32922646.
- [emg, verified] Wide grip lowered triceps EMG vs medium/narrow; narrow grip raised triceps, AD and clavicular-head EMG; wider grip raised abdominal-head pec EMG and shoulder joint moments. — Mausehund L, Werkhausen A, Bartsch J, Krosshaug T. Understanding Bench Press Biomechanics-The Necessity of Measuring Lateral Barbell Forces. J Strength Cond Res. 2022;36(10):2685-2695. PMID 33555823.
- [emg, verified] Wide grip displayed lower triceps activation than medium and narrow grips in both novice and trained men; pec activity similar across widths. — Saeterbakken AH, Stien N, Pedersen H, et al. The Effect of Grip Width on Muscle Strength and Electromyographic Activity in Bench Press among Novice- and Resistance-Trained Men. Int J Environ Res Public Health. 2021;18(12):6444. PMID 34198674.
- [emg, verified] Moving from wide to narrower grips increased triceps activity and decreased sternocostal pec activity; supinated grip increased clavicular pec and biceps activity. — Lehman GJ. The influence of grip width and forearm pronation/supination on upper-body myoelectric activity during the flat bench press. J Strength Cond Res. 2005;19(3):587-591. PMID 16095407.
- [emg, likely] Wide pronated grip at 0 deg maximised activation of all three pec heads; close pronated grip favoured the clavicular head and triceps long head. — Roy X, Arseneault K, Sercia P. The effect of 12 variations of the bench press exercise on the EMG activity of three heads of the pectoralis major. Int J Strength Cond. 2021;1(1). doi:10.47206/ijsc.v1i1.39.
- [emg, verified] Smith machine and barbell presses elicited greater triceps activity than dumbbell press; chest press machine excited the clavicular head less than free-weight variants. — Farias DA, Willardson JM, Paz GA, Bezerra ES, Miranda H. Maximal Strength Performance and Muscle Activation for the Bench Press and Triceps Extension Exercises Adopting Dumbbell, Barbell, and Machine Modalities Over Multiple Sets. J Strength Cond Res. 2017;31(7):1879-1887. PMID 27669189.
- [emg, verified] Seated chest press (neutral or wide prone grip) and lying bench press showed similar pec, AD (~24% MVIC) and triceps activity; only clavicular head was slightly higher with a neutral machine grip. — Muyor JM, Rodriguez-Ridao D, Oliva-Lozano JM. Comparison of Muscle Activity between the Horizontal Bench Press and the Seated Chest Press Exercises Using Several Grips. J Hum Kinet. 2023;87:23-34. PMID 37229415.
- [emg, verified] Standing cable press is limited by trunk stability; pec major and erector EMG were larger in the bench press, while abdominal/latissimus activity rose in the standing single-arm press. — Santana JC, Vera-Garcia FJ, McGill SM. A kinetic and electromyographic comparison of the standing cable press and bench press. J Strength Cond Res. 2007;21(4):1271-1277. PMID 18076235.
- [emg, verified] Systematic review: triceps and pectoralis major show similar, high activity in the flat bench press; anterior deltoid is the third prime mover. — Stastny P, Golas A, Blazek D, et al. A systematic review of surface electromyography analyses of the bench press movement task. PLoS One. 2017;12(2):e0171632. PMID 28170449.

Notes: Bands/chains change the strength curve, not the mover pattern; Smith/leverage/cable presses stay on the anchor row (Farias 2017, Muyor 2023). Standing cable press: trunk work is isometric bracing and is not credited (Santana 2007 shows lower pec EMG, higher trunk EMG). Guillotine and Neck Press (same movement in this DB) are wide-grip, flared-elbow presses to the neck: the practitioner claim of more upper chest has no EMG support, so the flat split is kept, but triceps drop to 0.25 like other wide-grip presses. Current mapping kept unchanged for every exercise except wide-grip and neck-press variants.

### Flat dumbbell / kettlebell press and floor press

`flat_dumbbell_press` · 7 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Horizontal press with independent implements (dumbbell or kettlebell), supine on bench or floor, bilateral, alternating or unilateral. Floor press stops the eccentric at ~90 deg elbow when the upper arm meets the floor.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| chest | 1 {chest_upper 0.25, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.25} | Same movement as the barbell anchor; dumbbell press pec activation is equal to or higher than barbell (Welsch 2005; Farias 2017). |
| delt_front | 0.5 | AD not different between barbell and dumbbell press (Welsch 2005). Calibrated to the flat anchor. |
| triceps | 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} | Dumbbell press shows somewhat lower triceps EMG than barbell/Smith (Farias 2017) but still well above half of pec; keep the anchor 0.5. Lateral/medial bias as in the barbell anchor (Brandao 2020). |

Exercises: Alternating Floor Press, Dumbbell Bench Press, Dumbbell Bench Press with Neutral Grip, Extended Range One-Arm Kettlebell Floor Press, Leg-Over Floor Press, One Arm Dumbbell Bench Press, One-Arm Kettlebell Floor Press.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Dumbbell Bench Press with Neutral Grip**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.3, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.2}; delt_front 0.5; triceps 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} — Neutral grip tucks the elbows (more shoulder flexion, less abduction) which favours the clavicular head slightly (Muyor 2023; Lehman 2005 narrow/supinated pattern).
- **One Arm Dumbbell Bench Press**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.25, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.25}; delt_front 0.5; triceps 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7}; obliques 0.25 — Unilateral load: external/internal oblique EMG significantly higher than bilateral (Patterson 2015; Saeterbakken 2020).
- **Alternating Floor Press**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.25, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.25}; delt_front 0.5; triceps 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7}; obliques 0.25 — Alternating arms create an asymmetric load; oblique credit 0.25 replaces the current abs 0.5, for which no evidence exists in a supine floor press.
- **Extended Range One-Arm Kettlebell Floor Press**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.25, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.25}; delt_front 0.5; triceps 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7}; obliques 0.25 — Unilateral load (Patterson 2015; Saeterbakken 2020). 'Extended range' (torso on a pad so the elbow passes below the shoulder) restores the stretched bottom position; region split unchanged.
- **One-Arm Kettlebell Floor Press**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.25, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.25}; delt_front 0.5; triceps 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7}; obliques 0.25 — Unilateral load (Patterson 2015; Saeterbakken 2020).
- **Leg-Over Floor Press**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.25, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.25}; delt_front 0.5; triceps 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7}; obliques 0.25 — Unilateral kettlebell press with the contralateral leg crossed over, an anti-rotation setup; oblique credit 0.25 (Patterson 2015).

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Pectoralis major and anterior deltoid activation were not different between barbell bench press, dumbbell bench press and dumbbell fly at 6RM. — Welsch EA, Bird M, Mayhew JL. Electromyographic activity of the pectoralis major and anterior deltoid muscles during three upper-body lifts. J Strength Cond Res. 2005;19(2):449-452. PMID 15903389.
- [emg, verified] Dumbbell bench press elicited greater pec major activity than barbell, lower triceps activity than barbell/Smith, and higher biceps (stabiliser) activity. — Farias DA, Willardson JM, Paz GA, Bezerra ES, Miranda H. Maximal Strength Performance and Muscle Activation for the Bench Press and Triceps Extension Exercises Adopting Dumbbell, Barbell, and Machine Modalities Over Multiple Sets. J Strength Cond Res. 2017;31(7):1879-1887. PMID 27669189.
- [emg, verified] Neutral-grip pressing produced slightly higher clavicular-head activity (~30% vs ~25% MVIC) than a 150% biacromial pronated grip; AD and triceps unchanged. — Muyor JM, Rodriguez-Ridao D, Oliva-Lozano JM. Comparison of Muscle Activity between the Horizontal Bench Press and the Seated Chest Press Exercises Using Several Grips. J Hum Kinet. 2023;87:23-34. PMID 37229415.
- [emg, verified] Unilateral (one-dumbbell) chest press produced significantly greater external and internal oblique activation than holding two dumbbells; pec activation unchanged; ROM larger with one dumbbell. — Patterson JM, Vigotsky AD, Oppenheimer NE, Feser EH. Differences in unilateral chest press muscle activation and kinematics on a stable versus unstable surface while holding one versus two dumbbells. PeerJ. 2015;3:e1365. PMID 26528421.
- [emg, verified] Asymmetric bench-press loads increased external oblique activation on both sides versus symmetric loading. — Saeterbakken AH, Solstad TEJ, Behm DG, et al. Muscle activity in asymmetric bench press among resistance-trained individuals. Eur J Appl Physiol. 2020;120(11):2517-2524. PMID 32856145.
- [emg, verified] Dumbbell training produced task-specific strength gains and stability demand but the prime movers remain pec, AD and triceps across Smith, dumbbell and Swiss-ball presses. — Saeterbakken AH, Andersen V, Behm DG, et al. Resistance-training exercises with different stability requirements: time course of task specificity. Eur J Appl Physiol. 2016;116(11-12):2247-2256. PMID 27671996.
- [anatomical, unverified] Floor press shortens the eccentric (upper arm stops on the floor), removing the stretched-pec bottom range; the movement is a reduced-ROM flat press with the same prime movers. — Anatomical / biomechanical reasoning (no dedicated floor-press EMG study located on PubMed).

Notes: Current mapping for bilateral dumbbell press is already the flat anchor and is kept. Changes: (1) unilateral and alternating variants get obliques 0.25 (Patterson 2015, Saeterbakken 2020 show real oblique work; kept at the minor-stabiliser level because there is no hypertrophy data); (2) Alternating_Floor_Press loses its abs 0.5 (supine on the floor, no rectus demand shown) in favour of obliques 0.25; (3) neutral grip gets a small upper tilt per Muyor 2023 and the tucked-elbow line of pull. Floor press keeps full chest credit; the ROM cut is noted but the brief has no ROM modifier.

### Incline press (30-45 deg)

`incline_press` · 7 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Press with the torso inclined 30-45 deg: shoulder flexion dominates over horizontal adduction, loading the clavicular head and anterior deltoid; elbow extension.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| chest | 1 {chest_upper 0.6, chest_mid 0.35, chest_lower 0.05} | Anchor row. Incline training grew the 2nd intercostal (clavicular) site 0.5-0.6 cm more than horizontal training (Chaves 2020); 45 deg incline shifts excitation and acute CSA change to the clavicular head (Albarello 2022; Cabral 2021; Trebs 2010). |
| delt_front | 0.5 | Anchor row. AD rises with inclination (~33% MVIC at 30-60 deg vs pec-upper ~30%; Rodriguez-Ridao 2020). |
| triceps | 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} | Anchor gives triceps 0.5 with no split; triceps EMG is identical across inclinations (Rodriguez-Ridao 2020) so the flat lateral/medial split is carried over. |

Exercises: Barbell Incline Bench Press - Medium Grip, Hammer Grip Incline DB Bench Press, Incline Cable Chest Press, Incline Dumbbell Bench With Palms Facing In, Incline Dumbbell Press, Leverage Incline Chest Press, Smith Machine Incline Bench Press.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Hammer Grip Incline DB Bench Press**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.65, chest_mid 0.3, chest_lower 0.05}; delt_front 0.5; triceps 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} — Neutral grip tucks the elbows and adds shoulder flexion, further favouring the clavicular head (Muyor 2023; Lehman 2005).
- **Incline Dumbbell Bench With Palms Facing In**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.65, chest_mid 0.3, chest_lower 0.05}; delt_front 0.5; triceps 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} — Same movement as the hammer-grip incline press.

Evidence:

- [hypertrophy, verified] 8 weeks of incline bench press grew pec thickness at the 2nd intercostal space more than horizontal (+0.62 cm) or combined (+0.50 cm) training; 3rd and 5th intercostal sites grew similarly in all groups. — Chaves SFN, Rocha-Junior VA, Encarnacao IGA, et al. Effects of Horizontal and Incline Bench Press on Neuromuscular Adaptations in Untrained Young Men. Int J Exerc Sci. 2020;13(6):859-872. PMID 32922646.
- [emg, verified] 45 deg incline press: clavicular-head RMS greater than sternocostal for every set, and greater acute CSA increase in the clavicular head; flat press showed the opposite. — Albarello JCS, Cabral HV, Leitao BFM, Halmenschlager GH, Lima LFF, de Oliveira LF. Non-uniform excitation of pectoralis major induced by changes in bench press inclination leads to uneven variations in the cross-sectional area measured by panoramic ultrasonography. J Electromyogr Kinesiol. 2022;67:102722. PMID 36334406.
- [emg, verified] High-density EMG: flat press excites more caudal (sternocostal) pec regions, 45 deg incline shifts excitation cranially toward the clavicular region. — Cabral HV, Albarello JCS, Ribeiro NS, Almeida MFA, Vieira TM, de Oliveira LF. Non-uniform excitation of the pectoralis major muscle during flat and inclined bench press exercises. Scand J Med Sci Sports. 2022;32(2):297-305. PMID 34644424.
- [emg, verified] Upper-pec EMG peaked at 30 deg; middle and lower portions were highest at 0 deg; AD highest at 60 deg; triceps unchanged across 0-60 deg. — Rodriguez-Ridao S, Antequera-Vique JA, Martin-Fuentes I, Muyor JM. Effect of Five Bench Inclinations on the Electromyographic Activity of the Pectoralis Major, Anterior Deltoid, and Triceps Brachii during the Bench Press Exercise. Int J Environ Res Public Health. 2020;17(19):7339. PMID 33049982.
- [emg, verified] Clavicular head activation greater at 44 and 56 deg than 0 deg; sternocostal head greater at 0 deg than 28/44/56 deg; anterior deltoid greater at 44/56 deg. — Trebs AA, Brandenburg JP, Pitney WA. An electromyography analysis of 3 muscles surrounding the shoulder joint during the performance of a chest press exercise at several angles. J Strength Cond Res. 2010;24(7):1925-1930. PMID 20512064.
- [emg, verified] Lower pec EMG at 45 deg (72% MVIC) was lower than at 0, -15 and 30 deg (~87-100% MVIC); upper pec showed higher EMG at 30/45 deg only in the mid-concentric window. — Lauver JD, Cayot TE, Scheuermann BW. Influence of bench angle on upper extremity muscular activation during bench press exercise. Eur J Sport Sci. 2016;16(3):309-316. PMID 25799093.
- [emg, verified] In competitive bodybuilders the clavicular head was most excited in the 45 deg incline press and the sternocostal head least; chest-press machine excited the clavicular head least. — Coratella G, Tornatore G, Longo S, Esposito F, Ce E. Specific prime movers' excitation during free-weight bench press variations and chest press machine in competitive bodybuilders. Eur J Sport Sci. 2020;20(5):571-579. PMID 31397215.
- [emg, verified] Neutral (hammer) grip raised clavicular-head activity slightly relative to a wide pronated grip. — Muyor JM, Rodriguez-Ridao D, Oliva-Lozano JM. Comparison of Muscle Activity between the Horizontal Bench Press and the Seated Chest Press Exercises Using Several Grips. J Hum Kinet. 2023;87:23-34. PMID 37229415.

Notes: Every current incline entry already matches the anchor and is kept. Barbell, Smith, leverage and cable incline presses share the pattern (Coratella 2020 shows machines excite the clavicular head somewhat less, not enough to change credit). Hammer/palms-in grips get a small extra upper tilt (Muyor 2023).

### Decline press (-15 to -30 deg)

`decline_press` · 6 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Press with the head below the hips: pressing direction relative to the torso moves caudally, loading the sternocostal/abdominal fibres; less shoulder flexion so less anterior deltoid.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| chest | 1 {chest_upper 0.15, chest_mid 0.4, chest_lower 0.45} | Lower pec more active in decline than incline and equal to flat (Glass & Armstrong 1997; Lauver 2016 -15 deg lower pec 100% MVIC); abdominal head highest with wide pronated decline (Roy 2021). Upper pec at -15 deg was not lower than flat in Lauver 2016 and only modestly lower in Barnett 1995, so upper is set to 0.15 rather than the current 0.10. |
| delt_front | 0.25 | Anterior deltoid activity increases with inclination and is lowest on the decline bench (Barnett 1995; Rodriguez-Ridao 2020 trend), so one step below the flat anchor's 0.5. |
| triceps | 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} | Triceps long head more active in decline and flat than incline (Barnett 1995); triceps overall unchanged with angle (Rodriguez-Ridao 2020). Anchor split kept. |

Exercises: Decline Barbell Bench Press, Decline Dumbbell Bench Press, Decline Smith Press, Leverage Decline Chest Press, Smith Machine Decline Press, Wide-Grip Decline Barbell Bench Press.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Wide-Grip Decline Barbell Bench Press**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.1, chest_mid 0.4, chest_lower 0.5}; delt_front 0.25; triceps 0.25 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} — Wide pronated grip at -15 deg is the condition that maximised abdominal-head EMG (Roy 2021) and wide grips reduce triceps EMG (Saeterbakken 2021; Mausehund 2022; Lehman 2005).

Evidence:

- [emg, likely] Lower pec (5th intercostal) activation was significantly greater during -15 deg decline than +30 deg incline press in both phases; upper pec did not differ. — Glass SC, Armstrong T. Electromyographical activity of the pectoralis muscle during incline and decline bench presses. J Strength Cond Res. 1997;11(3):163-167. doi:10.1519/00124278-199708000-00006.
- [emg, likely] Sternocostal head more active on the horizontal than decline bench; clavicular head less active on decline; anterior deltoid tended to increase with trunk inclination; triceps long head more active in decline and flat than incline. — Barnett C, Kippers V, Turner P. Effects of variations of the bench press exercise on the EMG activity of five shoulder muscles. J Strength Cond Res. 1995;9(4):222-227. doi:10.1519/00124278-199511000-00003.
- [emg, verified] At -15 deg, lower pec EMG (100% MVIC) equalled flat and exceeded 45 deg incline; upper pec at -15 deg (96%) was not different from flat (98%). — Lauver JD, Cayot TE, Scheuermann BW. Influence of bench angle on upper extremity muscular activation during bench press exercise. Eur J Sport Sci. 2016;16(3):309-316. PMID 25799093.
- [emg, likely] Abdominal-head EMG was greatest with a wide pronated grip at -15 deg and 0 deg versus all 30 deg incline conditions. — Roy X, Arseneault K, Sercia P. The effect of 12 variations of the bench press exercise on the EMG activity of three heads of the pectoralis major. Int J Strength Cond. 2021;1(1). doi:10.47206/ijsc.v1i1.39.
- [emg, verified] In bodybuilders the sternocostal head was similarly excited in -15 deg decline and flat press and more than in 45 deg incline. — Coratella G, Tornatore G, Longo S, Esposito F, Ce E. Specific prime movers' excitation during free-weight bench press variations and chest press machine in competitive bodybuilders. Eur J Sport Sci. 2020;20(5):571-579. PMID 31397215.
- [emg, verified] Wide grip lowers triceps activity and raises abdominal-head pec activity. — Mausehund L, Werkhausen A, Bartsch J, Krosshaug T. Understanding Bench Press Biomechanics-The Necessity of Measuring Lateral Barbell Forces. J Strength Cond Res. 2022;36(10):2685-2695. PMID 33555823.

Notes: No decline-specific hypertrophy study exists. Changes from current: upper share raised 0.10 -> 0.15 (Lauver 2016 shows the clavicular head is not switched off at -15 deg) and delt_front lowered 0.5 -> 0.25 (Barnett 1995). Wide-grip decline keeps a stronger lower bias and drops triceps to 0.25 like other wide-grip presses.

### Parallel-bar dip, forward lean (chest version)

`chest_dip` · 1 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Closed-chain press from a shoulder-extended, elbow-flexed bottom position with ~35 deg forward trunk lean; shoulder flexion/adduction from extension plus elbow extension. Loads the costal pec fibres at long length.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| chest | 1 {chest_upper 0.05, chest_mid 0.35, chest_lower 0.6} | Bar dip peak pec activation ~65% (non-fatigued) rising to >100% of peak with fatigue (McKenzie 2022); with the forward lean the movement is a steep decline press, so costal/abdominal fibres dominate (anatomical; Roy 2021 decline pattern). |
| triceps | 0.75 {triceps_long 0.4, triceps_lateral_medial 0.6} | Triceps peak activation (~80%) exceeded pec (~65%) and AD (~66%) in the bar dip (McKenzie 2022); the long head works from a shoulder-extended (lengthened) position (Kholinne 2018), so a stronger long-head share than in the bench anchor. |
| delt_front | 0.5 | AD peak ~66% in the bar dip, comparable to pec (McKenzie 2022); anchor-calibrated to the flat press 0.5. |

Exercises: Dips - Chest Version.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Bar dip: peak activation pec major 64.8%, triceps 79.5%, anterior deltoid 66.0% of peak in the non-fatigued state, with ~37 deg anterior trunk lean; pec, triceps, serratus, lower trap and lats all rose with fatigue. — McKenzie A, Crowley-McHattan Z, Meir R, Whitting J, Volschenk W. Fatigue Increases Muscle Activations but Does Not Change Maximal Joint Angles during the Bar Dip. Int J Environ Res Public Health. 2022;19(21):14390. PMID 36361276.
- [emg, likely] Bar and ring dips produced greater pec major, anterior deltoid and triceps activation than bench dips; triceps peak 1.04 mV on the bar. — McKenzie A, Crowley-McHattan Z, Meir R, Whitting J, Volschenk W. Bench, Bar, and Ring Dips: Do Kinematics and Muscle Activity Differ? Int J Environ Res Public Health. 2022;19(20):13211. doi:10.3390/ijerph192013211.
- [emg, verified] Triceps long head produces the greatest force and activation of the three heads when the shoulder is near 0 deg elevation (arm by the side / extended), the medial head takes over at >=90 deg elevation. — Kholinne E, Zulkarnain RF, Sun YC, Lim S, Chun JM, Jeon IH. The different role of each head of the triceps brachii muscle in elbow extension. Acta Orthop Traumatol Turc. 2018;52(3):201-205. PMID 29503079.
- [emg, likely] Abdominal-head EMG is maximal in decline pressing conditions; the forward-lean dip presses in the same caudal direction relative to the torso. — Roy X, Arseneault K, Sercia P. The effect of 12 variations of the bench press exercise on the EMG activity of three heads of the pectoralis major. Int J Strength Cond. 2021;1(1). doi:10.47206/ijsc.v1i1.39.
- [consensus, unverified] ACE-sponsored EMG comparison ranked dips near the top for pec major activation alongside barbell bench, pec deck and crossover (non-peer-reviewed). — Schanke W. Electromyographical analysis of the pectoralis major muscle during various chest exercises. Master's thesis, University of Wisconsin-La Crosse (ACE-sponsored), 2012.

Notes: Change from current: triceps raised 0.5 -> 0.75 with a longer-head share (McKenzie 2022 shows triceps peak >= pec even in the chest-lean dip) and the lower-pec share strengthened. No dip hypertrophy study exists; the triceps result rests on peak EMG in 13-15 trained men.

### Horizontal flye / crossover / pec deck

`flat_flye` · 12 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Single-joint shoulder horizontal adduction at ~90 deg abduction with the elbow held at a fixed angle; supine dumbbell, cable, machine, band or bodyweight-slider versions. Standing high-pulley crossovers add a downward (adduction) component.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| chest | 1 {chest_upper 0.25, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.25} | Pec major is the sole prime mover; pec activation in the flye equals the press in some data (Welsch 2005) and is 8-16% lower in others (Solstad 2020), still primary. Horizontal path at 90 deg abduction loads all heads with a sternal centre, as in the flat anchor. |
| delt_front | 0.25 | Anterior deltoid is a secondary horizontal adductor; its activity in the flye is lower than in the barbell press (Solstad 2020), so one step below the press anchor's 0.5. Welsch 2005 and Soncin 2014 (AD ~66% MVIC in chest fly) show it is not negligible. |

Exercises: Around The Worlds, Bodyweight Flyes, Butterfly, Cable Crossover, Cable Iron Cross, Cross Over - With Bands, Decline Dumbbell Flyes, Dumbbell Flyes, Flat Bench Cable Flyes, One-Arm Flat Bench Dumbbell Flye, Single-Arm Cable Crossover, Svend Press.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Cable Crossover**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.15, chest_mid 0.45, chest_lower 0.4}; delt_front 0.25 — High-pulley crossover: hands travel from above shoulder height down to the waist (adduction + slight extension), the line of pull of the costal/abdominal fibres (Roy 2021 decline pattern; anatomical).
- **Single-Arm Cable Crossover**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.15, chest_mid 0.45, chest_lower 0.4}; delt_front 0.25; obliques 0.25 — Same high-to-low path as the bilateral crossover; unilateral cable adds an anti-rotation demand (Santana 2007 standing single-arm press showed raised oblique activity).
- **Bodyweight Flyes**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.25, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.25}; delt_front 0.25; abs 0.25 — Slider/suspension flye from a plank: elbows locked (no triceps credit), abdominals loaded above ground push-up levels (Maeo 2014) but still a stabiliser, so 0.25 not 0.5.
- **Around The Worlds**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.3, chest_mid 0.45, chest_lower 0.25}; delt_front 0.5 — Arc from hips to overhead sweeps through flexion as well as adduction; the overhead half is shoulder flexion, so anterior deltoid stays at 0.5 and the clavicular share rises slightly.
- **Decline Dumbbell Flyes**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.1, chest_mid 0.4, chest_lower 0.5}; delt_front 0.25 — Decline bench biases the arc toward the costal fibres (Glass & Armstrong 1997; Roy 2021).
- **Svend Press**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.25, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.25}; delt_front 0.5; triceps 0.25 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7}; forearms 0.25 {forearm_flexors 0.7, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.3} — Isometric pec squeeze while pressing plates forward to arm's length at chest height: pec adduction is the intent, anterior deltoid flexes the shoulder to 90 deg, triceps extend under light load, forearm flexors hold the squeeze. Practitioner consensus only; no EMG study located.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Barbell bench press showed higher pec major, anterior deltoid and triceps activation than dumbbell flyes at 6RM (8-81% across phases); biceps was higher in flyes. — Solstad TEJ, Andersen V, Shaw M, Hoel EM, Vonheim A, Saeterbakken AH. A Comparison of Muscle Activation between Barbell Bench Press and Dumbbell Flyes in Resistance-Trained Males. J Sports Sci Med. 2020;19(4):645-651. PMID 33239937.
- [emg, verified] Pec major and anterior deltoid motor-unit activation did not differ between barbell press, dumbbell press and dumbbell fly, but the fly had a shorter relative time of activation. — Welsch EA, Bird M, Mayhew JL. Electromyographic activity of the pectoralis major and anterior deltoid muscles during three upper-body lifts. J Strength Cond Res. 2005;19(2):449-452. PMID 15903389.
- [emg, verified] During the chest fly, sternocostal pec reached ~100% MVIC and anterior deltoid ~66-87% MVIC depending on exercise order. — Soncin R, Pennone J, Guimaraes TM, Mezencio B, Amadio AC, Serrao JC. Influence of exercise order on electromyographic activity during upper body resistance training. J Hum Kinet. 2014;44:203-210. PMID 25713681.
- [consensus, unverified] Pec deck and bent-forward cable crossover produced ~98% and ~93% of barbell-bench pec activation; push-ups ~61-63% (non-peer-reviewed). — Schanke W. Electromyographical analysis of the pectoralis major muscle during various chest exercises. Master's thesis, University of Wisconsin-La Crosse (ACE-sponsored), 2012.
- [emg, likely] Wide pronated grip at -15 deg or 0 deg maximised abdominal-head EMG; the high-to-low crossover reproduces that downward, wide arm path. — Roy X, Arseneault K, Sercia P. The effect of 12 variations of the bench press exercise on the EMG activity of three heads of the pectoralis major. Int J Strength Cond. 2021;1(1). doi:10.47206/ijsc.v1i1.39.
- [emg, verified] Sling/suspension push-up positions raise abdominal muscle activity 15-27% over the ground condition; the slider/suspension flye shares this plank base. — Maeo S, Chou T, Yamamoto M, Kanehisa H. Muscular activities during sling- and ground-based push-up exercise. BMC Res Notes. 2014;7:192. PMID 24678968.
- [anatomical, unverified] No elbow extension occurs in a flye, so triceps receive no dynamic work; pec fibre lines (clavicular fibres pull up-and-in, costal fibres down-and-in) predict emphasis from the direction of the arm path. — Anatomical reasoning (pectoralis major fibre architecture; standard kinesiology).

Notes: Kept: chest 1.0 with the flat split for dumbbell/cable/pec-deck flyes. Changed: delt_front unified at 0.25 (current entries mix 0.25 and 0.5; Solstad 2020 places the flye below the press anchor); Cross_Over_-_With_Bands loses its biceps 0.5 (biceps only co-contracts, Solstad 2020, no hypertrophic case); Bodyweight_Flyes loses triceps 0.5 (elbows are locked, no extension) and keeps abs at 0.25 rather than 0.5 (Maeo 2014). Standing high-pulley crossovers get a lower-fibre tilt; the iron cross (arms at shoulder height) and band crossover stay on the horizontal split. Decline flye sits here with a decline override rather than a one-exercise family. Svend Press is an isometric plate squeeze pressed forward at chest height: chest 1.0 (adduction), delt_front 0.5 (shoulder flexion to 90 deg), triceps 0.25 (elbow extension under light load), forearms 0.25 (squeeze); no literature, consensus only.

### Incline flye / low-to-high crossover

`incline_flye` · 4 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Single-joint shoulder flexion-plus-adduction from a low, abducted position toward the upper chest: incline-bench dumbbell or cable flye and standing low-pulley crossover. Clavicular-head line of pull.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| chest | 1 {chest_upper 0.55, chest_mid 0.35, chest_lower 0.1} | Incline arm paths shift pec excitation and acute CSA change to the clavicular head (Albarello 2022; Trebs 2010); a flye keeps the same torso/arm geometry as the incline press anchor, with the lower tail set slightly above the press anchor because the arc passes through the horizontal plane at the bottom. |
| delt_front | 0.5 | The upward arc is shoulder flexion, the anterior deltoid's primary action; AD rises with inclination in pressing (Rodriguez-Ridao 2020) and the same geometry applies here, so it stays at the incline anchor's 0.5 rather than the flat flye's 0.25. |

Exercises: Incline Cable Flye, Incline Dumbbell Flyes, Incline Dumbbell Flyes - With A Twist, Low Cable Crossover.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] 45 deg incline pressing shifts pec excitation and acute cross-sectional change to the clavicular head. — Albarello JCS, Cabral HV, Leitao BFM, Halmenschlager GH, Lima LFF, de Oliveira LF. Non-uniform excitation of pectoralis major induced by changes in bench press inclination leads to uneven variations in the cross-sectional area measured by panoramic ultrasonography. J Electromyogr Kinesiol. 2022;67:102722. PMID 36334406.
- [emg, verified] Clavicular head EMG greater at 44-56 deg than flat; anterior deltoid also greater at those angles. — Trebs AA, Brandenburg JP, Pitney WA. An electromyography analysis of 3 muscles surrounding the shoulder joint during the performance of a chest press exercise at several angles. J Strength Cond Res. 2010;24(7):1925-1930. PMID 20512064.
- [emg, likely] Supinated grip at 30 deg incline was among the conditions with the highest clavicular-head EMG (relevant to the twisting/supinating incline flye). — Roy X, Arseneault K, Sercia P. The effect of 12 variations of the bench press exercise on the EMG activity of three heads of the pectoralis major. Int J Strength Cond. 2021;1(1). doi:10.47206/ijsc.v1i1.39.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Incline training selectively increased pec thickness at the 2nd intercostal (clavicular) site. — Chaves SFN, Rocha-Junior VA, Encarnacao IGA, et al. Effects of Horizontal and Incline Bench Press on Neuromuscular Adaptations in Untrained Young Men. Int J Exerc Sci. 2020;13(6):859-872. PMID 32922646.
- [emg, verified] Anterior deltoid activation in a flye is below that of a press at matched relative load. — Solstad TEJ, Andersen V, Shaw M, Hoel EM, Vonheim A, Saeterbakken AH. A Comparison of Muscle Activation between Barbell Bench Press and Dumbbell Flyes in Resistance-Trained Males. J Sports Sci Med. 2020;19(4):645-651. PMID 33239937.

Notes: Current entries already carry the incline split and delt_front 0.5; only the lower tail is nudged from 0.05 to 0.10 for the flye arc. Low_Cable_Crossover is moved from the flat split to this family: the low-to-high path is shoulder flexion, the clavicular head's action. No flye-specific hypertrophy data exist; region emphasis is inferred from incline-press EMG/hypertrophy plus fibre geometry.

### Standard push-up (hands at floor level)

`push_up` · 9 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Closed-chain horizontal press against ~64-66% body mass (Ebben 2011; Gouvali 2005; Suprak 2011) with the trunk held rigid; hand width, suspension, single-arm and plyometric variants.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| chest | 1 {chest_upper 0.25, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.25} | Push-up training at matched load produced the same pec thickness gain as bench press (Kikuchi 2017) and matched bench-press EMG (Calatayud 2015); horizontal torso, so the flat split. |
| delt_front | 0.5 | AD ~59% MVC vs pec ~64% in the traditional push-up (Snarr 2013); calibrated to the flat anchor. |
| triceps | 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} | Triceps EMG is at least as high as pec in push-ups (Snarr 2013: 74% vs 64% MVC; Kowalski 2022 review), but triceps thickness grew less than pec in push-up training (Kikuchi 2017), so the bench anchor's 0.5 and lateral/medial bias are kept. |

Exercises: Clock Push-Up, Isometric Wipers, Plyo Kettlebell Pushups, Push Up to Side Plank, Push-Up Wide, Pushups, Pushups (Close and Wide Hand Positions), Single-Arm Push-Up, Suspended Push-Up.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Push-Up Wide**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.2, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.3}; delt_front 0.5; triceps 0.25 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} — Wide base lowers triceps (and slightly pec) EMG versus narrow (Cogley 2005; Marcolin 2015); wide pronated hand spacing favours the sternocostal/abdominal fibres (Lehman 2005; Roy 2021). Current abs 0.5 removed (Lehman 2006).
- **Single-Arm Push-Up**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.25, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.25}; delt_front 0.5; triceps 0.75 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7}; obliques 0.5 — One arm carries ~64% body mass with a tucked elbow (Ebben 2011; narrow-base pattern of Cogley 2005), and the strongly asymmetric load demands anti-rotation from the obliques (Saeterbakken 2020; Patterson 2015).
- **Suspended Push-Up**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.25, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.25}; delt_front 0.5; triceps 0.75 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7}; abs 0.25 — Suspension push-up: triceps 106% and AD 81% MVC vs pec 70% (Snarr 2013); abdominals rise 19-144% over ground push-ups (Maeo 2014).
- **Push Up to Side Plank**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.25, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.25}; delt_front 0.5; triceps 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7}; obliques 0.5 — Each rep ends in a side plank, a deliberate oblique loading phase; kept at the current 0.5.
- **Isometric Wipers**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.25, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.25}; delt_front 0.5; triceps 0.25 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7}; obliques 0.25 — Isometric wide push-up hold with side-to-side weight shift: elbow angle barely changes (triceps 0.25), and the alternating single-arm load adds an anti-rotation demand (obliques 0.25 replaces the current abs 0.5).

Evidence:

- [hypertrophy, verified] 8 weeks of push-ups at a load matched to 40% 1RM bench press increased pec major and triceps thickness and bench 1RM to the same degree as bench press (pec 17.0->20.8 mm bench group; similar in push-up group). — Kikuchi N, Nakazato K. Low-load bench press and push-up induce similar muscle hypertrophy and strength gain. J Exerc Sci Fit. 2017;15(1):37-42. PMID 29541130.
- [emg, verified] 6RM band-resisted push-up and 6RM bench press produced equal pec/AD/triceps EMG and equal strength gains after 5 weeks. — Calatayud J, Borreani S, Colado JC, Martin F, Tella V, Andersen LL. Bench press and push-up at comparable levels of muscle activity results in similar strength gains. J Strength Cond Res. 2015;29(1):246-253. PMID 24983847.
- [emg, verified] Traditional push-up: pec major 63.6%, anterior deltoid 58.9%, triceps 74.3% MVC; suspension push-up raised all three (69.5%, 81.1%, 105.8% MVC). — Snarr RL, Esco MR. Electromyographic comparison of traditional and suspension push-ups. J Hum Kinet. 2013;39:75-83. PMID 24511343.
- [emg, verified] Narrow-base push-ups produced greater pec major and triceps EMG than wide-base push-ups. — Cogley RM, Archambault TA, Fibeger JF, Koverman MM, Youdas JW, Hollman JH. Comparison of muscle activation using various hand positions during the push-up exercise. J Strength Cond Res. 2005;19(3):628-633. PMID 16095413.
- [emg, verified] Triceps (long and lateral heads) and pec major (sternal and clavicular) were most active in the narrow variant; forward/backward-leaning variants loaded abdominal and back muscles most. — Marcolin G, Petrone N, Moro T, Battaglia G, Bianco A, Paoli A. Selective Activation of Shoulder, Trunk, and Arm Muscles: A Comparative Analysis of Different Push-Up Variants. J Athl Train. 2015;50(11):1126-1132. PMID 26488636.
- [emg, verified] Scoping review of 30 studies: triceps and pec major carry the highest EMG amplitude across standard, unstable and suspension push-ups; serratus is highest in push-up plus and incline push-ups. — Kowalski KL, Connelly DM, Jakobi JM, Sadi J. Shoulder electromyography activity during push-up variations: a scoping review. Shoulder Elbow. 2022;14(3):326-340. PMID 35599715.
- [anatomical, verified] Regular push-up ground reaction force ~64% body mass, knee push-up ~49%; feet elevated raised GRF, hands elevated lowered it. — Ebben WP, Wurm B, VanderZanden TL, et al. Kinetic analysis of several variations of push-ups. J Strength Cond Res. 2011;25(10):2891-2894. PMID 21873902.
- [emg, verified] Sling-based dynamic push-ups raised pec major (+29%), triceps, biceps and all three abdominal muscles (+19 to +144%) relative to ground push-ups. — Maeo S, Chou T, Yamamoto M, Kanehisa H. Muscular activities during sling- and ground-based push-up exercise. BMC Res Notes. 2014;7:192. PMID 24678968.
- [emg, verified] Pec major EMG was unchanged by surface instability; rectus abdominis in standard push-ups ~13-19% MVC and rose only with hands on an unstable surface. — Lehman GJ, MacMillan B, MacIntyre I, Chivers M, Fluter M. Shoulder muscle EMG activity during push up variations on and off a Swiss ball. Dyn Med. 2006;5:7. PMID 16762080.
- [emg, verified] Plyometric push-up variants (countermovement, jump, fall) differ in impact force and rate of force development but recruit the same pec/triceps pattern. — Garcia-Masso X, Colado JC, Gonzalez LM, et al. Myoelectric activation and kinetics of different plyometric push-up exercises. J Strength Cond Res. 2011;25(7):2040-2047. PMID 21701289.
- [emg, verified] Asymmetric/unilateral pressing loads raise external oblique activation on both sides. — Saeterbakken AH, Solstad TEJ, Behm DG, et al. Muscle activity in asymmetric bench press among resistance-trained individuals. Eur J Appl Physiol. 2020;120(11):2517-2524. PMID 32856145.

Notes: Standard push-up keeps the current (anchor-identical) mapping. The current abs 0.5 on Push-Up_Wide is removed: rectus activity in a plain push-up is ~13-19% MVC (Lehman 2006) and is isometric bracing, which the brief says not to credit; abs 0.25 is retained only where the literature shows a real step up (suspension: Maeo 2014). Wide hand position drops triceps to 0.25 (Cogley 2005; Marcolin 2015). Single-arm and suspended push-ups raise triceps to 0.75 (Snarr 2013 triceps 106% MVC in suspension; single-arm doubles the per-arm load with a tucked elbow). Push_Up_to_Side_Plank keeps obliques 0.5 because the side plank is the deliberate second half of the movement. Isometric_Wipers (weight shifted arm to arm from a wide push-up hold) is an isometric chest/shoulder hold with minimal elbow travel.

### Incline push-up (hands elevated)

`incline_push_up` · 4 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Push-up with hands on a bench or bar: lighter load (~40-55% body mass) and a pressing direction that is caudal relative to the torso, the decline-press pattern.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| chest | 1 {chest_upper 0.15, chest_mid 0.4, chest_lower 0.45} | Hands elevated tilts the torso so the arm presses downward relative to the trunk, the geometry of the decline press, which favours the costal fibres (Glass & Armstrong 1997; Roy 2021) while the clavicular head is not switched off (Lauver 2016). |
| delt_front | 0.25 | Decline-like geometry reduces the shoulder-flexion component; anterior deltoid lowest in decline pressing (Barnett 1995). One step below the flat anchor, matching the decline_press family. |
| triceps | 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} | Elbow extension against the reduced load; triceps EMG unchanged with bench angle (Rodriguez-Ridao 2020). |

Exercises: Incline Push-Up, Incline Push-Up Medium, Incline Push-Up Reverse Grip, Incline Push-Up Wide.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Incline Push-Up Reverse Grip**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.25, chest_mid 0.4, chest_lower 0.35}; delt_front 0.25; triceps 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} — Supinated hand position raises clavicular-head activity (Lehman 2005), partly offsetting the decline-like geometry.
- **Incline Push-Up Wide**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.1, chest_mid 0.4, chest_lower 0.5}; delt_front 0.25; triceps 0.25 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} — Wide base lowers triceps EMG (Cogley 2005) and wide spacing with a caudal press direction is the abdominal-head condition of Roy 2021.

Evidence:

- [anatomical, verified] Hands elevated on 30-60 cm boxes lowered ground reaction force below all other push-up variants (regular ~64% body mass). — Ebben WP, Wurm B, VanderZanden TL, et al. Kinetic analysis of several variations of push-ups. J Strength Cond Res. 2011;25(10):2891-2894. PMID 21873902.
- [emg, likely] Lower pec activation greater in decline than incline pressing; upper pec unchanged. — Glass SC, Armstrong T. Electromyographical activity of the pectoralis muscle during incline and decline bench presses. J Strength Cond Res. 1997;11(3):163-167. doi:10.1519/00124278-199708000-00006.
- [emg, verified] At -15 deg the lower pec kept 100% MVIC and the upper pec 96% MVIC, i.e. decline geometry loads the costal fibres without unloading the clavicular head. — Lauver JD, Cayot TE, Scheuermann BW. Influence of bench angle on upper extremity muscular activation during bench press exercise. Eur J Sport Sci. 2016;16(3):309-316. PMID 25799093.
- [emg, verified] Supinated (reverse) grip increased clavicular-head pec activity and biceps activity in the flat press. — Lehman GJ. The influence of grip width and forearm pronation/supination on upper-body myoelectric activity during the flat bench press. J Strength Cond Res. 2005;19(3):587-591. PMID 16095407.
- [emg, verified] Wide-base push-ups lower triceps and pec EMG relative to narrow base. — Cogley RM, Archambault TA, Fibeger JF, Koverman MM, Youdas JW, Hollman JH. Comparison of muscle activation using various hand positions during the push-up exercise. J Strength Cond Res. 2005;19(3):628-633. PMID 16095413.
- [emg, verified] Serratus anterior is the muscle most emphasised in incline (hands-elevated) push-ups; there is no serratus group, so no extra credit is assigned. — Kowalski KL, Connelly DM, Jakobi JM, Sadi J. Shoulder electromyography activity during push-up variations: a scoping review. Shoulder Elbow. 2022;14(3):326-340. PMID 35599715.

Notes: Current chest split {.10/.40/.50} is moderated to {.15/.40/.45} to align with the decline_press family (Lauver 2016 shows the clavicular head stays active). delt_front lowered 0.5 -> 0.25 as for decline presses. The abs 0.5 on Medium/Reverse/Wide is removed (isometric bracing at low load, Lehman 2006). Reverse grip shifts emphasis back up (Lehman 2005 supinated pattern); wide hands drop triceps to 0.25.

### Decline push-up (feet elevated)

`decline_push_up` · 3 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Push-up with feet on a box, bench or ball: heavier load (~70-75% body mass) and a cranial pressing direction relative to the torso, equivalent to a 15-30 deg incline press.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| chest | 1 {chest_upper 0.45, chest_mid 0.4, chest_lower 0.15} | A 30-60 cm foot elevation over a ~1 m hand-to-foot span gives a 15-35 deg torso angle, i.e. the 15-30 deg incline zone where upper-pec EMG peaks but middle/lower portions remain substantial (Rodriguez-Ridao 2020: PMUP peak at 30 deg); less extreme than the 45 deg incline anchor, so the upper share is 0.45 rather than 0.60. |
| delt_front | 0.5 | AD rises with inclination and at 30 deg (~33% MVIC) already matches the upper pec (Rodriguez-Ridao 2020); anchor 0.5. |
| triceps | 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} | Triceps EMG unchanged with angle (Rodriguez-Ridao 2020); heavier load than the standard push-up but the same share. |

Exercises: Decline Push-Up, Push-Ups With Feet Elevated, Push-Ups With Feet On An Exercise Ball.

Evidence:

- [anatomical, verified] Feet elevated on 30 or 60 cm boxes produced higher ground reaction force than all other push-up variants. — Ebben WP, Wurm B, VanderZanden TL, et al. Kinetic analysis of several variations of push-ups. J Strength Cond Res. 2011;25(10):2891-2894. PMID 21873902.
- [emg, verified] Upper-pec EMG peaked at 30 deg inclination; AD ~33% MVIC at 30 deg and above; middle/lower pec highest at 0 deg. — Rodriguez-Ridao S, Antequera-Vique JA, Martin-Fuentes I, Muyor JM. Effect of Five Bench Inclinations on the Electromyographic Activity of the Pectoralis Major, Anterior Deltoid, and Triceps Brachii during the Bench Press Exercise. Int J Environ Res Public Health. 2020;17(19):7339. PMID 33049982.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Incline pressing selectively increased clavicular-region pec thickness after 8 weeks. — Chaves SFN, Rocha-Junior VA, Encarnacao IGA, et al. Effects of Horizontal and Incline Bench Press on Neuromuscular Adaptations in Untrained Young Men. Int J Exerc Sci. 2020;13(6):859-872. PMID 32922646.
- [emg, verified] Placing the feet on a Swiss ball instead of a bench changed no muscle's activation (pec, triceps, abdominals), so a feet-on-ball push-up behaves as a feet-elevated push-up. — Lehman GJ, MacMillan B, MacIntyre I, Chivers M, Fluter M. Shoulder muscle EMG activity during push up variations on and off a Swiss ball. Dyn Med. 2006;5:7. PMID 16762080.

Notes: Decline_Push-Up currently uses the 45 deg incline anchor split {.60/.35/.05}; a typical foot elevation produces a 15-35 deg torso angle, so the split is moderated to {.45/.40/.15}. Push-Ups_With_Feet_Elevated and Push-Ups_With_Feet_On_An_Exercise_Ball currently use the flat split; they are the same movement (Lehman 2006 shows the ball adds nothing for feet) and are moved here.

### Pullover (bent-arm and straight-arm)

`pullover` · 5 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Supine shoulder extension from an overhead, stretched position back to vertical with the elbows fixed (bent or straight). Sternocostal pec fibres, latissimus and the long head of triceps extend the humerus; the clavicular head and anterior deltoid are flexors and act only near the top.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| chest | 1 {chest_upper 0.15, chest_mid 0.45, chest_lower 0.4} | Barbell pullover EMG emphasised pec major far more than latissimus (Marchetti & Uchida 2011); the review literature calls the pullover pec-dominant (Di Fonza 2026). Sternocostal/abdominal fibres are the humeral extensors from overhead, the clavicular head is a flexor, hence the lower-biased split. |
| lats | 0.5 | Latissimus is a genuine shoulder extensor here but its EMG was well below pec in the barbell pullover (Marchetti & Uchida 2011); activation depends on joint position (Teixeira 2022). Half credit. |
| triceps | 0.5 {triceps_long 0.7, triceps_lateral_medial 0.3} | With the elbow fixed the long head acts as a shoulder extensor at long muscle length; overhead-position loading of the long head produced 1.5x the hypertrophy of neutral-position work (Maeo 2023). Long-head bias mirrors the overhead-extension anchor. |
| delt_front | 0.25 | Anterior deltoid is an antagonist to the extension but showed moderate-to-strong EMG in pullovers, strongest in the bent-arm version (Electromyogr Clin Neurophysiol 2007); minor credit. |

Exercises: Bent-Arm Barbell Pullover, Bent-Arm Dumbbell Pullover, Front Raise And Pullover, Straight-Arm Dumbbell Pullover, Wide-Grip Decline Barbell Pullover.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Straight-Arm Dumbbell Pullover**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.15, chest_mid 0.45, chest_lower 0.4}; lats 0.5; triceps 0.25 {triceps_long 0.8, triceps_lateral_medial 0.2}; delt_front 0.25 — With the elbow locked the triceps do no elbow work; only the long head contributes as a shoulder extensor, so triceps 0.25 with a stronger long-head share.
- **Front Raise And Pullover**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.15, chest_mid 0.45, chest_lower 0.4}; delt_front 0.75; lats 0.5; triceps 0.25 {triceps_long 0.8, triceps_lateral_medial 0.2} — Combination of a supine straight-arm front raise (anterior deltoid prime mover through 0-90 deg flexion) and a straight-arm pullover; the front-raise half earns delt_front 0.75.
