- **Wide-Grip Decline Barbell Pullover**: chest 1 {chest_upper 0.1, chest_mid 0.4, chest_lower 0.5}; lats 0.5; triceps 0.5 {triceps_long 0.7, triceps_lateral_medial 0.3}; delt_front 0.25 — Decline bench and wide grip push the arc toward the costal/abdominal fibres (Roy 2021 wide-pronated decline pattern); lats added at 0.5, missing from the current row.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] The barbell pullover (30% body weight) emphasised pectoralis major activity more than latissimus dorsi in both concentric and eccentric phases; activation depended on the external lever arm. — Marchetti PH, Uchida MC. Effects of the pullover exercise on the pectoralis major and latissimus dorsi muscles as evaluated by EMG. J Appl Biomech. 2011;27(4):380-384. PMID 21975179.
- [emg, verified] Narrative review: the pullover preferentially activates pectoralis major rather than selectively targeting latissimus dorsi; relative amplitude depends strongly on shoulder joint position. — Di Fonza et al. Electromyographic Analysis of Latissimus Dorsi Activation During Common Resistance Training Exercises: A Narrative Review. J Funct Morphol Kinesiol. 2026;11(3):315. PMID 42647355.
- [emg, verified] Pec major, latissimus and posterior deltoid iEMG in isometric pullover vs pulldown varied with shoulder position (0-180 deg), with a pullover-specific interaction for pec major. — Teixeira FGS, et al. Differences Between Pullover and Pulldown Exercises on Maximal Isometric Force and Myoelectric Activity in Recreationally-Trained Men. Int J Exerc Sci. 2022;15(4). PMID 35992501.
- [emg, verified] In 24 men, clavicular pec activity was weak-to-moderate in both pullover modalities while anterior deltoid was moderate in the straight-arm and strong in the bent-arm pullover. — Electromyographic validation of the deltoideus (anterior portion) and pectoralis major (clavicular portion) muscles in pull-over exercises. Electromyogr Clin Neurophysiol. 2007;47(6). PMID 18051626.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Elbow extension training with the shoulder overhead (long head lengthened) produced ~1.5x greater long-head and whole-triceps hypertrophy than in the neutral arm position. — Maeo S, Wu Y, Huang M, et al. Triceps brachii hypertrophy is substantially greater after elbow extension training performed in the overhead versus neutral arm position. Eur J Sport Sci. 2023;23(7):1240-1250. PMID 35819335.
- [emg, unverified] Comparison of EMG during bench press and barbell pullover reported pec-dominant, lat-minor activation in the pullover (Campos & Silva, Motriz 2014). — Campos YAC, Silva SF. Comparison of electromyographic activity during the bench press and barbell pullover exercises. Motriz Rev Educ Fis. 2014;20(2):200-205.

Notes: Biggest correction in the batch: Bent-Arm_Barbell_Pullover currently has lats 1.0 / chest 0.5, the reverse of every EMG dataset; all pullovers are set chest 1.0 / lats 0.5. Triceps split flipped to long-head bias (the long head is the head that crosses the shoulder). delt_front reduced from 0.5 to 0.25 (antagonist with moderate co-activation). No pullover hypertrophy study exists; the lats share might be higher for the straight-arm variant executed with the arms kept behind the head (Teixeira 2022 position dependence), listed under open questions.

## core

### Crunch (spinal flexion, hips fixed)

`trunk_flexion_crunch` · 17 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Lumbar/thoracic flexion with the pelvis fixed: floor, machine, decline, ball, cable/rope crunches and their twisting hybrids. Rectus abdominis is the prime mover; external/internal obliques co-contract at a lower level; hip flexors are largely unloaded unless the feet are anchored on a decline or the legs cycle (bicycle-type variants).

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| abs | 1 | Rectus abdominis is the prime mover of trunk flexion; curl-ups produce >50% MVC (Konrad 2001) and crunches produce the largest RA thickness change of crunch/sit-up/leg-raise (Gomirato 2023). Current mapping already correct. |
| obliques | 0.25 | EO/IO co-contract at roughly 1/4-1/2 of RA level in a plain crunch (Vera-Garcia 2000: RA 21% vs EO 5% MVC on a stable surface; Escamilla 2010 crunch EO 14-30%). Minor credit so oblique colour reflects crunch volume without over-crediting. |

Exercises: Ab Crunch Machine, Air Bike, Bosu Ball Cable Crunch With Side Bends, Cable Crunch, Cable Seated Crunch, Cross-Body Crunch, Crunch - Hands Overhead, Crunch - Legs On Exercise Ball, Crunches, Decline Crunch, Elbow to Knee, Exercise Ball Crunch, Kneeling Cable Crunch With Alternating Oblique Twists, Rope Crunch, Standing Rope Crunch, Tuck Crunch, Weighted Crunches.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Air Bike**: abs 1; obliques 0.75; hip_flexors 0.5 — Bicycle manoeuvre: rotation adds strong oblique work (ACE/Francis 2001 ranked it #1 RA and #2 obliques of 13 exercises) and the cycling legs load the hip flexors dynamically.
- **Bosu Ball Cable Crunch With Side Bends**: abs 1; obliques 0.75 — Cable crunch plus lateral-flexion component: RA primary, obliques strong synergist (same reasoning as the twisting cable crunch).
- **Cross-Body Crunch**: abs 1; obliques 0.75 — Rotational crunch: trunk-curl-with-twist raises EO relative to a straight curl (Willett 2001); legs stay on the floor so no hip-flexor credit.
- **Decline Crunch**: abs 1; obliques 0.25; hip_flexors 0.25 — Feet anchored on a decline: iliopsoas/rectus femoris contribute once the trunk lifts beyond a pure curl (Andersson 1997: supported legs increase hip-flexor activation). Minor credit only.
- **Elbow to Knee**: abs 1; obliques 0.75; hip_flexors 0.5 — Same movement as the bicycle crunch (alternating elbow to opposite knee with the legs cycling).
- **Exercise Ball Crunch**: abs 1; obliques 0.5 — Crunch on a labile ball roughly doubles EO relative to a floor crunch (Vera-Garcia 2000: EO 5% -> 10% MVC; Sternlicht 2007: RA and EO higher with the ball at the low back).
- **Kneeling Cable Crunch With Alternating Oblique Twists**: abs 1; obliques 0.75 — Loaded crunch with an alternating twist: obliques become a strong synergist, RA remains primary. Current mapping (obliques 1.0 / abs 0.5) inverts the emphasis; the load is still moved mainly by trunk flexion.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Pure spine-flexion exercises such as the curl-up produce isolated abdominal activation above 50% MVC; adding hip flexion (sit-up) increases peak activation. — Konrad P, Schmitz K, Denner A. Neuromuscular evaluation of trunk-training exercises. J Athl Train. 2001;36(2):109-118. PMID 12937449.
- [emg, verified] Crunch produced the largest rectus abdominis thickness change (36% greater than a 90-degree leg raise); recruitment is biased toward the segments closest to the load. — Gomirato AP, Grenier SG. Diagnostic ultrasound shows preferential activation of rectus abdominis segments with exercises targeting upper versus lower segments. Int J Exerc Sci. 2023;16(1):1077-1086. PMID 38288259.
- [emg, verified] Stable-surface curl-up: RA 21% MVC, EO 5% MVC; on a labile ball RA 35% and EO 10%, with the oblique increase proportionally larger. — Vera-Garcia FJ, Grenier SG, McGill SM. Abdominal muscle response during curl-ups on both stable and labile surfaces. Phys Ther. 2000;80(6):564-569. PMID 10842409.
- [emg, verified] Stability-ball crunch (ball at low back) produced significantly higher upper/lower RA and EO EMG than a traditional crunch. — Sternlicht E, Rugg S, Fujii LL, Tomomitsu KF, Seki MM. Electromyographic comparison of a stability ball crunch with a traditional crunch. J Strength Cond Res. 2007;21(2):506-509. PMID 17530978.
- [emg, verified] Trunk curl with a twist and the V-sit produced the greatest external oblique activity among five common abdominal exercises; upper RA activity was similar across curl variants. — Willett GM, Hyde JE, Uhrlaub MB, Wendel CL, Karst GM. Relative activity of abdominal muscles during commonly prescribed strengthening exercises. J Strength Cond Res. 2001;15(4):480-485. PMID 11726260.
- [consensus, likely] Bicycle manoeuvre ranked first for rectus abdominis (about 2.5x crunch EMG) and second for obliques among 13 exercises; crunch on exercise ball ranked third for RA. — Francis P, Davis J. ACE-commissioned study: Best and worst abdominal exercises. ACE FitnessMatters, May/June 2001 (San Diego State University Biomechanics Lab; not peer reviewed).
- [emg, verified] Crunch and bent-knee sit-up produced the lowest latissimus dorsi (7-8% MVIC) and lowest rectus femoris (crunch 6-10% MVIC) of the exercises tested, supporting no lat or hip-flexor credit for plain crunches. — Escamilla RF, Lewis C, Bell D, et al. Core muscle activation during Swiss ball and traditional abdominal exercises. J Orthop Sports Phys Ther. 2010;40(5):265-276. PMID 20436242.

Notes: Kept abs 1.0 everywhere (already correct). Added a small oblique credit (0.25) to plain crunches because EO/IO reliably co-activate; if the team prefers a cleaner map, dropping it to 0 is defensible. Twisting hybrids were re-ordered so RA is the 1.0 and obliques the strong synergist (0.75), the reverse of the current guess. No longitudinal regional-hypertrophy study of crunch training was found; ranking rests on EMG/ultrasound.

### Sit-up (trunk + hip flexion)

`sit_up` · 8 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Full sit-ups from supine to upright: trunk flexion followed by hip flexion. Rectus abdominis and obliques drive the first 30 degrees and stabilise the pelvis, then iliopsoas/rectus femoris carry the hip-flexion phase. Includes V-ups and sit-ups combined with an overhead press.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| abs | 1 | Abdominals show marked activation in both trunk-flexion and hip-flexion sit-ups (Andersson 1997); sit-up peak RA activation exceeds the curl-up (Konrad 2001). Current mapping already correct. |
| hip_flexors | 0.5 | All sit-up forms activate psoas 15-35% MVC versus <10% in a curl-up (Juker 1998); rectus femoris EMG is among the highest in the bent-knee sit-up (Escamilla 2006). Half credit per the brief's secondary rule. Current mapping already correct. |
| obliques | 0.25 | EO/IO stabilise the pelvis and trunk through the range (Andersson 1997; Maeo 2013 sit-up EO higher than bracing). Minor credit. |

Exercises: 3/4 Sit-Up, Frog Sit-Ups, Jackknife Sit-Up, Janda Sit-Up, Otis-Up, Press Sit-Up, Sit-Up, Weighted Sit-Ups - With Bands.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Jackknife Sit-Up**: abs 1; hip_flexors 0.75; obliques 0.25 — V-up: straight legs lift simultaneously with the trunk, so long-lever hip flexion is a major share of the work (Andersson 1997: bilateral leg lifts give the highest iliacus/sartorius activity; Maeo 2013: V-sit gave among the highest RA/EO values).
- **Press Sit-Up**: abs 1; hip_flexors 0.5; delt_front 0.5; triceps 0.25 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} — Light barbell pressed to overhead lockout during the sit-up: anterior deltoid does real work, triceps only lockout. Chest credit removed (the bar is not pressed horizontally against meaningful load).
- **Otis-Up**: abs 1; hip_flexors 0.5; delt_front 0.5; triceps 0.25 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} — Sit-up while pressing a plate overhead; same reasoning as the press sit-up. Chest credit removed.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] All forms of sit-ups activated psoas 15-35% MVC versus <10% for the curl-up. — Juker D, McGill S, Kropf P, Steffen T. Quantitative intramuscular myoelectric activity of lumbar portions of psoas and the abdominal wall during a wide variety of tasks. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 1998;30(2):301-310. PMID 9502361.
- [emg, verified] Hip flexors (iliacus, RF, sartorius) are highly activated only in exercises involving hip flexion; abdominals are markedly active in both trunk-flexion and hip-flexion sit-ups; flexed and supported legs increase hip-flexor activation. — Andersson EA, Nilsson J, Ma Z, Thorstensson A. Abdominal and hip flexor muscle activation during various training exercises. Eur J Appl Physiol Occup Physiol. 1997;75(2):115-123. PMID 9118976.
- [emg, verified] Rectus femoris EMG was highest for the Power Wheel pike/knee-up, reverse crunch inclined 30 degrees and the bent-knee sit-up. — Escamilla RF, Babb E, DeWitt R, et al. Electromyographic analysis of traditional and nontraditional abdominal exercises: implications for rehabilitation and training. Phys Ther. 2006;86(5):656-671. PMID 16649890.
- [emg, verified] V-sits and sit-ups produced higher RA and EO %EMGmax than abdominal bracing and curl-ups. — Maeo S, Takahashi T, Takai Y, Kanehisa H. Trunk muscle activities during abdominal bracing: comparison among muscles and exercises. J Sports Sci Med. 2013;12(3):467-474. PMID 24149153.
- [hypertrophy, likely] Twelve weeks of slow bodyweight training that included sit-ups increased hip-flexion strength in older adults, consistent with a training stimulus to the hip flexors from sit-ups. — Tsuzuku S, Kajioka T, Sakakibara H, Shimaoka K. Slow movement resistance training using body weight improves muscle mass in the elderly: a randomized controlled trial. Scand J Med Sci Sports. 2018;28(4):1339-1344. PMID 29247985.

Notes: Janda sit-up: the hamstring co-contraction cue is intended to inhibit the hip flexors, but there is no EMG evidence that it removes hip-flexor involvement; kept at the family default. 3/4 sit-up stops short of upright, reducing but not eliminating the hip-flexion phase; kept at default rather than inventing a finer step.

### Reverse crunch / lying and seated leg raise / knee tuck

`reverse_crunch_leg_tuck` · 14 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Hip flexion with the trunk supported (supine, decline, seated on a bench, plank on a ball or TRX), ending in a posterior pelvic tilt. Rectus abdominis (lower segments) and obliques stabilise and tilt the pelvis; iliopsoas/rectus femoris/sartorius flex the hips and take a bigger share as the leg lever lengthens.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| abs | 1 | The reverse curl gave the greatest lower-RA activity of five common exercises (Willett 2001); bilateral leg lifts require abdominal activation (Andersson 1997); leg-lifting exercises thicken the distal RA most (Dafkou 2020). Current mapping already correct. |
| hip_flexors | 0.5 | Bent-knee hip flexion with a short lever: iliopsoas/RF are the joint movers but the load is small; psoas activity in supine hip-flexion tasks is well above curl-up levels (Juker 1998). Half credit per the brief. |
| obliques | 0.25 | Reverse curl and V-sit produced the greatest EO activity among common floor exercises (Willett 2001); reverse crunch inclined 30 degrees was among the highest EO/IO exercises (Escamilla 2006). Minor credit at the family level, raised for the harder plank-based variants. |

Exercises: Bent-Knee Hip Raise, Bottoms Up, Cable Reverse Crunch, Cocoons, Decline Reverse Crunch, Exercise Ball Pull-In, Flat Bench Leg Pull-In, Flat Bench Lying Leg Raise, Leg Pull-In, Reverse Crunch, Seated Flat Bench Leg Pull-In, Seated Leg Tucks, Smith Machine Hip Raise, Suspended Reverse Crunch.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Bottoms Up**: abs 1; hip_flexors 0.25 — Legs raised vertical, hips lifted off the floor: same pelvic-lift mechanics as the Smith machine hip raise, unloaded.
- **Cocoons**: abs 1; hip_flexors 0.5; obliques 0.25 — Simultaneous crunch and bent-knee tuck: family default (trunk flexion plus short-lever hip flexion).
- **Decline Reverse Crunch**: abs 1; hip_flexors 0.75; obliques 0.5 — Reverse crunch inclined 30 degrees was among the highest exercises for upper/lower RA, IO and rectus femoris (Escamilla 2006).
- **Exercise Ball Pull-In**: abs 1; hip_flexors 0.75; obliques 0.5; delt_front 0.25 — Swiss-ball jackknife/knee-up from a push-up plank: lower RA is maximised (Duncan 2009), EO/IO and RF are among the highest of any exercise (Escamilla 2006, 2010 knee-up/pike); the supporting shoulders earn minor anterior-deltoid credit (Escamilla 2010 lats 17-25% in knee-up/pike, serratus/anterior delt hold the plank).
- **Flat Bench Leg Pull-In**: abs 1; hip_flexors 0.75; obliques 0.25 — Bench version of the leg pull-in: legs extend to horizontal and tuck; long-lever hip flexion.
- **Flat Bench Lying Leg Raise**: abs 1; hip_flexors 0.75; obliques 0.25 — Straight-leg lever: bilateral straight-leg lifts give the highest iliacus and sartorius activity of all tested exercises (Andersson 1997), so hip flexors become a strong synergist.
- **Leg Pull-In**: abs 1; hip_flexors 0.75; obliques 0.25 — Supine straight-leg extend-and-tuck: long-lever hip flexion on each rep (Andersson 1997).
- **Seated Flat Bench Leg Pull-In**: abs 1; hip_flexors 0.75; obliques 0.25 — Same movement as seated leg tucks (bench-seated knee pull-in with V-hold).
- **Seated Leg Tucks**: abs 1; hip_flexors 0.75; obliques 0.25 — Seated knee tuck: the trunk is held in a semi-V and the work is mostly hip flexion against the leg lever; hip flexors are a strong synergist (Andersson 1997 leg-lift data).
- **Smith Machine Hip Raise**: abs 1; hip_flexors 0.25 — Legs held vertical under a loaded bar; the movement is a posterior pelvic tilt/lumbar flexion lifting the hips, so hip flexors work only isometrically at short length. Current abs-only mapping was nearly right; 0.25 hip flexors for the isometric hold under external load.
- **Suspended Reverse Crunch**: abs 1; hip_flexors 0.75; obliques 0.5; delt_front 0.25 — TRX knee tuck from a plank is mechanically the same as the ball pull-in (hanging knee-up with straps was among the top RA/EO/IO exercises in Escamilla 2006).

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Reverse curl produced the greatest lower rectus abdominis activity; reverse curl and V-sit produced the greatest external oblique activity. — Willett GM, Hyde JE, Uhrlaub MB, Wendel CL, Karst GM. Relative activity of abdominal muscles during commonly prescribed strengthening exercises. J Strength Cond Res. 2001;15(4):480-485. PMID 11726260.
- [emg, verified] Bilateral (not unilateral) leg lifts require abdominal activation; bilateral leg lifts produce higher iliacus and sartorius activity than hip-flexion sit-ups. — Andersson EA, Nilsson J, Ma Z, Thorstensson A. Abdominal and hip flexor muscle activation during various training exercises. Eur J Appl Physiol Occup Physiol. 1997;75(2):115-123. PMID 9118976.
- [emg, verified] Reverse crunch inclined 30 degrees, Power Wheel pike/knee-up and hanging knee-up with straps gave the highest upper/lower RA, IO and lat activity and (with the bent-knee sit-up) the highest rectus femoris activity. — Escamilla RF, Babb E, DeWitt R, et al. Electromyographic analysis of traditional and nontraditional abdominal exercises: implications for rehabilitation and training. Phys Ther. 2006;86(5):656-671. PMID 16649890.
- [emg, verified] Lower rectus abdominis activity exceeded upper RA only in the Swiss-ball jackknife; ball exercises exceeded the floor curl-up. — Duncan M. Muscle activity of the upper and lower rectus abdominis during exercises performed on and off a Swiss ball. J Bodyw Mov Ther. 2009;13(4):364-367. PMID 19761961.
- [emg, verified] Isometric leg lifting and combined trunk-plus-leg lifting increased thickness of the distal rectus abdominis segments more than the proximal segments (extended-field-of-view ultrasound). — Dafkou K, Kellis E, Ellinoudis A, Sahinis C, Galanis N. Assessment of rectus abdominis muscle thickness during isometric trunk and leg lifting exercises using extended field of view (EFOV) ultrasound. Med Ultrason. 2020;22(4):430-437. PMID 32905563.
- [emg, verified] Bilateral leg raise produced significantly greater rectus abdominis activation than the plank. — Park DJ, Park SY. Which trunk exercise most effectively activates abdominal muscles? A comparative study of plank and isometric bilateral leg raise exercises. J Back Musculoskelet Rehabil. 2019;32(5):797-802. PMID 30856100.

Notes: Several exercises that the current table maps as abs-only (Bent-Knee Hip Raise, Cocoons, Leg Pull-In, Seated Leg Tucks, Exercise Ball Pull-In, Flat Bench Leg Pull-In, Seated Flat Bench Leg Pull-In, Smith Machine Hip Raise, Bottoms Up) are hip-flexion movements and now carry hip_flexors credit. hip_flexors is an opt-in group, so users who leave it off see no change.

### Hanging leg/knee raise and captain's chair

`hanging_leg_raise` · 4 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Hip flexion (knees or straight legs) with the torso hanging from a bar or supported on parallel-bar elbow pads, finishing in a posterior pelvic tilt. Highest abdominal activation of any leg-raise variant; hip flexors are strong synergists; grip (bar versions) and shoulder extension (toes-to-bar) add small credits.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| abs | 1 | Hanging knee-up with straps ranked among the highest exercises for upper and lower RA (Escamilla 2006); captain's chair ranked second for RA and first for obliques in the ACE study. Current mapping already correct. |
| hip_flexors | 0.75 | Hanging leg raises are the highest-psoas task short of maximal hip flexion (Juker 1998 hip-flexion efforts; Andersson 1997 bilateral leg lifts). Strong synergist tier. Raised from 0.5. |
| obliques | 0.5 | External oblique EMG was highest for the Power Wheel movements and hanging knee-up with straps (Escamilla 2006); captain's chair ranked first for obliques (ACE 2001). Standard secondary credit. |
| forearms | 0.25 {forearm_flexors 1} | Sustained bar hang loads the finger/wrist flexors isometrically for the whole set; minor credit (anatomical reasoning, no hypertrophy data). Removed for the parallel-bar version. |

Exercises: Gorilla Chin/Crunch, Hanging Leg Raise, Hanging Pike, Knee/Hip Raise On Parallel Bars.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Hanging Pike**: abs 1; hip_flexors 0.75; obliques 0.5; lats 0.25; forearms 0.25 {forearm_flexors 1} — Toes-to-bar: the finish pulls the pelvis toward the bar via shoulder extension, so lats do concentric work (Escamilla 2006 found lat EMG highest in the hanging knee-up/Power Wheel group).
- **Knee/Hip Raise On Parallel Bars**: abs 1; hip_flexors 0.75; obliques 0.5 — Captain's chair: elbows rest on pads, so no grip demand; abdominal and oblique activation is otherwise the same class as the hanging knee-up (ACE 2001 #2 RA / #1 obliques).
- **Gorilla Chin/Crunch**: lats 1; biceps 0.5 {biceps 0.8, brachialis 0.2}; abs 1; hip_flexors 0.5; obliques 0.25; forearms 0.25 {forearm_flexors 1} — A supinated chin-up performed together with a bent-knee raise: the chin-up half makes lats a prime mover (consistent with the Wide-Grip Lat Pulldown anchor: lats 1.0, biceps 0.5) and the knee raise half is a hanging knee-up. Two 1.0 groups is intentional for a genuine two-movement compound.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Hanging knee-up with straps was among the most effective exercises for upper/lower RA, IO, EO and latissimus dorsi, and also produced high rectus femoris activity. — Escamilla RF, Babb E, DeWitt R, et al. Electromyographic analysis of traditional and nontraditional abdominal exercises: implications for rehabilitation and training. Phys Ther. 2006;86(5):656-671. PMID 16649890.
- [consensus, likely] Captain's chair ranked second for rectus abdominis (212% of crunch EMG) and first for obliques among 13 exercises. — Francis P, Davis J. ACE-commissioned study: Best and worst abdominal exercises. ACE FitnessMatters, May/June 2001 (San Diego State University; not peer reviewed).
- [emg, verified] Psoas is most active during maximal hip-flexion efforts and in all sit-up forms (15-35% MVC), far above the curl-up. — Juker D, McGill S, Kropf P, Steffen T. Quantitative intramuscular myoelectric activity of lumbar portions of psoas and the abdominal wall during a wide variety of tasks. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 1998;30(2):301-310. PMID 9502361.
- [emg, verified] Bilateral straight-leg lifts require abdominal activation and produce the highest iliacus and sartorius activity of the tested exercises. — Andersson EA, Nilsson J, Ma Z, Thorstensson A. Abdominal and hip flexor muscle activation during various training exercises. Eur J Appl Physiol Occup Physiol. 1997;75(2):115-123. PMID 9118976.

Notes: No dedicated peer-reviewed EMG of the straight-leg hanging raise was found on PubMed; the mapping extrapolates from the hanging knee-up (Escamilla 2006) and leg-lift data (Andersson 1997).

### Anti-extension: rollouts, fallouts, planks, dead bug, plank crawls

`anti_extension_plank_rollout` · 10 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Resisting lumbar extension while the lever lengthens (ab wheel/barbell rollout, TRX fallout) or holding it statically (plank) or while limbs move (dead bug, spider crawl, mountain climbers). Rectus abdominis and obliques are the primary targets; in rollouts the lats (with pecs and long-head triceps) resist shoulder flexion eccentrically and are the meaningful 'extraneous' muscle.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| abs | 1 | Roll-out gave the highest upper RA (63% MVIC) and near-highest lower RA (53%) of ten exercises (Escamilla 2010); Power Wheel roll-out was among the top RA exercises (Escamilla 2006). Current mapping already correct. |
| obliques | 0.5 | Roll-out EO 46% and IO 46% MVIC, comparable to RA (Escamilla 2010); EO was highest in the Power Wheel group (Escamilla 2006). Standard secondary credit. |
| lats | 0.5 | Latissimus dorsi EMG was highest in the Power Wheel roll-out/pike/knee-up group (Escamilla 2006; 17-25% MVIC for pike/knee-up in 2010): the lats resist shoulder flexion as the wheel rolls out and pull it back. Replaces the current delt_front 0.5, which is anatomically wrong (gravity flexes the shoulder; anterior deltoid is unloaded). |

Exercises: Ab Roller, Barbell Ab Rollout, Barbell Ab Rollout - On Knees, Barbell Rollout from Bench, Butt-Ups, Dead Bug, Plank, Spider Crawl, Suspended Fallout, Wind Sprints.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Ab Roller**: abs 1; obliques 0.5; lats 0.5 — Wheel rollout on knees = family default; delt_front 0.5 replaced by lats 0.5 (Escamilla 2006 lat data).
- **Barbell Ab Rollout**: abs 1; obliques 0.5; lats 0.5; hip_flexors 0.25 — Standing (from the feet) rollout: the hips must be held in flexion against a much longer lever, so iliopsoas works hard isometrically; erectors credit removed (paraspinal EMG <10% MVIC in rollouts, Escamilla 2010).
- **Barbell Ab Rollout - On Knees**: abs 1; obliques 0.5; lats 0.5 — Kneeling rollout = family default; erectors 0.5 removed (lumbar paraspinal EMG low and similar across abdominal exercises, Escamilla 2006/2010).
- **Barbell Rollout from Bench**: abs 1; obliques 0.5; lats 0.5 — Kneeling on a bench with a longer roll: same mechanics as the kneeling rollout. Current glute/hamstring credit removed: the hips extend passively as the bar rolls out and there is no meaningful hip-extension load.
- **Suspended Fallout**: abs 1; obliques 0.5; lats 0.5 — TRX fallout is a standing rollout on straps: identical shoulder-flexion-resisting mechanics. Chest and erector credit removed.
- **Plank**: abs 1; obliques 0.5 — Front plank: RA ~46% and EO substantial (Calatayud 2019; García-Jaén 2020 shows EO/IO rise further with bracing); no arm lever moves, so no lat credit.
- **Dead Bug**: abs 1; obliques 0.25; hip_flexors 0.5 — Supine anti-extension with alternating leg lowering: RA/EO rise with progression level (Kim 2016); the extending leg is lowered and raised by the hip flexors against a long lever.
- **Spider Crawl**: abs 1; obliques 0.5; hip_flexors 0.5; delt_front 0.25 — Moving push-up plank with knee-to-elbow: plank anti-extension plus repeated hip flexion; the supporting shoulder earns a minor anterior-deltoid credit. Chest/triceps credit removed (arms stay locked, no pressing).
- **Wind Sprints**: abs 1; hip_flexors 0.75; obliques 0.25; delt_front 0.25 — Mountain climbers: rapid alternating hip flexion in a plank; the hip flexors do the dynamic work, abs hold the plank.
- **Butt-Ups**: abs 1; hip_flexors 0.5; obliques 0.5; delt_front 0.25 — Forearm-plank pike (hips driven to the ceiling): a floor version of the Swiss-ball pike, which had the highest EO (84% MVIC) and high RA/IO (Escamilla 2010); hip flexors lift the pelvis.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Roll-out and pike produced the highest upper RA (63%/46% MVIC), lower RA (53%/55%), EO (46%/84%) and IO (46%/56%) of ten exercises while lumbar paraspinal EMG stayed under 10% MVIC; lat EMG 17-25% MVIC in pike/knee-up. — Escamilla RF, Lewis C, Bell D, et al. Core muscle activation during Swiss ball and traditional abdominal exercises. J Orthop Sports Phys Ther. 2010;40(5):265-276. PMID 20436242.
- [emg, verified] Upper/lower RA, IO and latissimus dorsi EMG were highest for the Power Wheel (pike, knee-up, roll-out) and hanging knee-up; lumbar paraspinal EMG was low and similar across exercises. — Escamilla RF, Babb E, DeWitt R, et al. Electromyographic analysis of traditional and nontraditional abdominal exercises: implications for rehabilitation and training. Phys Ther. 2006;86(5):656-671. PMID 16649890.
- [emg, verified] Front plank RA median 46% nEMG (48% with brace) and front plank with brace EO 77% nEMG, the highest EO of the exercises tested. — Calatayud J, Escriche-Escuder A, Cruz-Montecinos C, et al. Tolerability and muscle activity of core muscle exercises in chronic low-back pain. Int J Environ Res Public Health. 2019;16(19):3509. PMID 31547140.
- [emg, verified] Adding abdominal hollowing to the prone plank significantly raised EO and IO (and erector) activation while RA was unchanged, confirming meaningful oblique involvement in planks. — García-Jaén M, Cortell-Tormo JM, Hernández-Sánchez S, Tortosa-Martínez J. Influence of abdominal hollowing maneuver on the core musculature activation during the prone plank exercise. Int J Environ Res Public Health. 2020;17(20):7410. PMID 33053717.
- [emg, verified] Across graded curl-up, dead bug, Superman and bird-dog progressions, RA and EO amplitude rose with dead-bug/curl-up intensity levels. — Kim CR, Park DK, Lee ST, Ryu JS. Electromyographic changes in trunk muscles during graded lumbar stabilization exercises. PM R. 2016;8(10):979-989. PMID 27317910.
- [emg, verified] Systematic review: free-weight and rollout-type exercises produce the greatest RA/EO activity; core-stability exercises the greatest IO activity. — Oliva-Lozano JM, Muyor JM. Core muscle activity during physical fitness exercises: a systematic review. Int J Environ Res Public Health. 2020;17(12):4306. PMID 32560185.

Notes: Main correction versus current: rollouts move their arm credit from delt_front to lats (shoulder extensors resist the roll-out; Escamilla measured lats, not deltoid). Erector credit on rollouts removed on direct EMG evidence (<10% MVIC).

### Rotation and anti-rotation: twists, chops, lifts, Pallof, landmine, slams

`trunk_rotation_anti_rotation` · 14 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Transverse-plane trunk work: dynamic rotation (Russian/plate/cable twists, wood chop, cable lift, landmine 180, judo flip, kettlebell figure-8/pass) and isometric anti-rotation (Pallof press). External and internal obliques are the prime movers/targets; rectus abdominis co-contracts; the arms mostly transmit load.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| obliques | 1 | Obliques (EO contralateral, IO ipsilateral) produce trunk rotation and resist it in anti-rotation holds (McGill 1996 twisting tasks; Willett 2001 twist curl highest EO). Current mapping already correct for most twists. |
| abs | 0.5 | Rectus abdominis co-contracts to flex/brace the trunk in every twisting task and in V-sit twists holds the torso up (Maeo 2013 V-sit RA). Standard secondary credit; current mapping already correct. |

Exercises: Cable Judo Flip, Cable Russian Twists, Kettlebell Figure 8, Kettlebell Pass Between The Legs, Landmine 180's, One-Arm Medicine Ball Slam, Pallof Press, Pallof Press With Rotation, Plate Twist, Russian Twist, Seated Barbell Twist, Spell Caster, Standing Cable Lift, Standing Cable Wood Chop.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Cable Judo Flip**: obliques 1; abs 0.5; lats 0.25 — Rotational hip-hinge throw pulling the cable down and across: obliques primary (current abs-only mapping corrected), lats assist the downward pull.
- **Kettlebell Figure 8**: obliques 1; abs 0.5; hamstrings 0.25 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1}; glutes 0.25 {glute_max 0.8, glute_med 0.2} — Low-intensity coordination drill passing the bell around the legs in a held hinge: the rotation loads the obliques, the static hinge gives minor hamstring/glute credit. Front-delt credit removed (arms hang).
- **Kettlebell Pass Between The Legs**: obliques 1; abs 0.5; hamstrings 0.25 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1}; glutes 0.25 {glute_max 0.8, glute_med 0.2} — Same drill as the figure 8 (hand-to-hand pass around the legs in a hinge).
- **Landmine 180's**: obliques 1; abs 0.5; delt_front 0.5 — The arms hold a loaded bar end at shoulder height throughout and lower/raise it through the arc, so anterior deltoid does meaningful work. Glute/erector credit removed: the hips pivot but do not extend against load.
- **One-Arm Medicine Ball Slam**: abs 1; obliques 0.5; lats 0.5; delt_front 0.25 — Overhead slam is primarily violent trunk flexion (abs) plus shoulder extension (lats); the one-arm version adds some rotation (obliques). Front delt lifts the ball overhead between reps.
- **Pallof Press**: obliques 1; abs 0.5 — Isometric anti-rotation: load is set by the lateral band/cable force, not by pressing (Juan-Recio 2025 progression study). Chest, front-delt and triceps credit removed: the press-out is unloaded in the pressing direction.
- **Pallof Press With Rotation**: obliques 1; abs 0.5 — Anti-rotation hold plus controlled rotation: obliques primary; arm-muscle credit removed for the same reason as the plain Pallof press.
- **Plate Twist**: obliques 1; abs 0.5; hip_flexors 0.25 — Weighted Russian twist; same V-sit hold.
- **Russian Twist**: obliques 1; abs 0.5; hip_flexors 0.25 — Performed in a V-sit with feet off the floor: hip flexors hold the position isometrically (Andersson 1997). Erector credit removed: the trunk is flexed, not extended against load.
- **Seated Barbell Twist**: obliques 1 — Unloaded bar-on-shoulders twist: very low external torque, mostly a mobility drill; obliques only, and even that is a small stimulus (anatomical reasoning).
- **Spell Caster**: obliques 1; abs 0.5; delt_front 0.5 — Dumbbells swung across the body at arm's length hip-to-shoulder: anterior deltoid lifts the load each rep. Glute credit removed.
- **Standing Cable Lift**: obliques 1; abs 0.5; delt_front 0.25 — Low-to-high lift: the same diagonal pattern reversed; obliques primary (current abs-1.0 mapping had the emphasis wrong), minor front-delt credit for the upward arm path.
- **Standing Cable Wood Chop**: obliques 1; abs 0.5; delt_front 0.25 — High-to-low chop: arms are kept nearly straight and transmit load; minor front-delt credit for holding the handle out in front (current 0.5 lowered).

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Surface electrodes over the obliques track intramuscular EO/IO activity during standing and sitting twisting tasks; obliques are the muscles loaded by twisting. — McGill S, Juker D, Kropf P. Appropriately placed surface EMG electrodes reflect deep muscle activity (psoas, quadratus lumborum, abdominal wall) in the lumbar spine. J Biomech. 1996;29(11):1503-1507. PMID 8894932.
- [emg, verified] Adding a twist to the trunk curl and the V-sit produced the greatest external oblique activity of five common exercises. — Willett GM, Hyde JE, Uhrlaub MB, Wendel CL, Karst GM. Relative activity of abdominal muscles during commonly prescribed strengthening exercises. J Strength Cond Res. 2001;15(4):480-485. PMID 11726260.
- [emg, verified] V-sits produced among the highest RA and EO %EMGmax of ten static/dynamic trunk exercises, supporting RA co-credit in V-sit twists. — Maeo S, Takahashi T, Takai Y, Kanehisa H. Trunk muscle activities during abdominal bracing: comparison among muscles and exercises. J Sports Sci Med. 2013;12(3):467-474. PMID 24149153.
- [emg, verified] Pallof press difficulty is set by stance/base of support and the lateral force applied, not by the pressing motion; lumbopelvic control demand ranked kneeling < standing < tandem < unstable surface. — Juan-Recio C, Prat-Luri A, Rondón-Espinosa H, Barbado D, Vera-Garcia FJ. Effect of body position and support surface on the postural control challenge during the Pallof press exercise: a smartphone accelerometer-based study. Medicina (Kaunas). 2025;61(2):312. PMID 40005429.
- [anatomical, unverified] No peer-reviewed EMG of the cable wood chop, landmine 180 or kettlebell figure-8 was found; arm and hip credits rest on moment-arm reasoning (which segment moves the load through what range). — Anatomical/biomechanical reasoning by the author of this mapping; practitioner descriptions (BarBend, Bodybuilding.com exercise guides) used only to confirm execution.

Notes: Largest corrections: Pallof presses lose chest/front-delt/triceps (no pressing load), Standing Cable Lift and Cable Judo Flip become oblique-primary, Russian twist loses erector credit. Rotation exercises have weak per-exercise EMG coverage; the family default is safe, the arm credits are judgment calls.

### Lateral flexion: side bends, oblique crunches, side bridge

`lateral_flexion_side_bend` · 10 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Frontal-plane trunk work: standing/seated side bends with a load, side-lying or decline oblique crunches, side jackknife, and the isometric side bridge. Ipsilateral obliques (EO and IO) are the prime movers, with quadratus lumborum and the ipsilateral erector group assisting; rectus abdominis contributes little except in the crunch-type variants.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| obliques | 1 | Lateral-flexion tasks target primarily the external oblique, with high activity in side-lying flexion (Konrad 2001); side bridge EO 69% MVIC (Ekstrom 2007). Current mapping already correct. |
| abs | 0.25 | Rectus abdominis has a small lateral-flexion moment arm and co-contracts modestly in side bends; lowered from the current 0.5 for the standing side-bend variants, kept at 0.5 for crunch-type variants. |
| erectors | 0.25 | Quadratus lumborum (no group of its own) and the ipsilateral erectors are strong lateral flexors: QL was 54% MVC in the horizontal side support and rose with standing lateral-bend load (McGill 1996). Credited to the nearest visible group at the minor tier. |

Exercises: Alternate Heel Touchers, Barbell Side Bend, Decline Oblique Crunch, Dumbbell Side Bend, Oblique Crunches, Oblique Crunches - On The Floor, One-Arm High-Pulley Cable Side Bends, Side Bridge, Side Jackknife, Weighted Ball Side Bend.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Alternate Heel Touchers**: obliques 1; abs 0.5 — Shoulders held off the floor (RA isometric) while reaching side to side (oblique lateral flexion): the dynamic work is oblique, so the current abs-only mapping is corrected.
- **Barbell Side Bend**: obliques 1; abs 0.25; erectors 0.5 — Bar across the shoulders raises the load and its lever above the lumbar spine, so QL/erectors work harder than in a dumbbell-at-the-side bend (McGill 1996 QL rises with compressive load); current erectors 0.5 kept.
- **Oblique Crunches**: obliques 1; abs 0.5 — Side-lying crunch: lateral flexion with a trunk-curl component, no posterior-chain load; current mapping kept.
- **Oblique Crunches - On The Floor**: obliques 1; abs 0.5 — Supine crunch with knees dropped to one side: same as the oblique crunch; current mapping kept.
- **Decline Oblique Crunch**: obliques 1; abs 0.5; hip_flexors 0.25 — Twisting crunch on a decline with the feet anchored: obliques primary, RA secondary, minor hip-flexor credit for the anchored decline (Andersson 1997 supported-leg effect).
- **Side Bridge**: obliques 1; abs 0.5; erectors 0.25; glutes 0.25 {glute_med 1} — Side plank: EO 69% and gluteus medius 74% MVIC (Ekstrom 2007), QL 54% MVC (McGill 1996), rated the best abdominal-wall exercise for activation per unit psoas (Juker 1998). Gluteus medius credit at the minor tier despite being isometric because its activation is above the 45% MVIC strengthening threshold. Front-delt credit removed (passive shoulder support).
- **Side Jackknife**: obliques 1; abs 0.25; glutes 0.25 {glute_med 1} — Side-lying, the top leg abducts while the torso laterally flexes toward it: the leg is lifted by hip abductors (gluteus medius), not the hip flexors, so the current hip_flexors 0.5 moves to glute_med 0.25.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Lateral-flexion tasks primarily targeted the external oblique, which showed high activity in side-lying flexion tasks. — Konrad P, Schmitz K, Denner A. Neuromuscular evaluation of trunk-training exercises. J Athl Train. 2001;36(2):109-118. PMID 12937449.
- [emg, verified] Side-bridge produced external oblique 69% MVIC and gluteus medius 74% MVIC, the only exercise of nine exceeding the 45% MVIC strengthening threshold for those muscles. — Ekstrom RA, Donatelli RA, Carp KC. Electromyographic analysis of core trunk, hip, and thigh muscles during 9 rehabilitation exercises. J Orthop Sports Phys Ther. 2007;37(12):754-762. PMID 18560185.
- [emg, verified] Quadratus lumborum was 54% of maximum in the horizontal side-support posture and 74% during heavy barbell lifts, and increased with compressive load in standing. — McGill S, Juker D, Kropf P. Quantitative intramuscular myoelectric activity of quadratus lumborum during a wide variety of tasks. Clin Biomech (Bristol). 1996;11(3):170-172. PMID 11415616.
- [emg, verified] Among isometric abdominal exercises, the side bridge maximised abdominal-wall activation while minimising psoas activity. — Juker D, McGill S, Kropf P, Steffen T. Quantitative intramuscular myoelectric activity of lumbar portions of psoas and the abdominal wall during a wide variety of tasks. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 1998;30(2):301-310. PMID 9502361.

Notes: No hypertrophy study of side-bend training exists; mapping rests on EMG and moment-arm reasoning.

### Kettlebell windmill and bent press

`windmill_bent_press` · 4 exercises · confidence low, unverified

Overhead-loaded lateral flexion plus hip hinge/rotation: the loaded arm is locked overhead while the trunk side-bends and rotates and the hips hinge back. Obliques (with QL) control the trunk; hamstrings and glutes load eccentrically in the hinge; the overhead arm is an isometric shoulder hold. The bent press adds a press-out/lockout under the bell.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| obliques | 1 | Loaded lateral flexion/rotation of the trunk is the defining action (Konrad 2001 lateral-flexion tasks target EO; McGill 1996 QL/obliques in lateral bending). Current mapping already correct. |
| hamstrings | 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} | The descent is a single-leg-biased hip hinge that lengthens the hamstrings under load; short head does not cross the hip (anatomical). Current 0.8/0.2 split corrected to 1.0/0 as in the Romanian Deadlift anchor. |
| glutes | 0.5 {glute_max 0.7, glute_med 0.3} | Hip extension out of the hinge (glute max) with the pelvis rotated and weight shifted to one leg (glute med frontal-plane control). Anatomical reasoning. |
| erectors | 0.5 | The hinged, side-bent spine is held under an overhead load; erectors/QL resist flexion and lateral flexion (McGill 1996 QL rises with load). Secondary credit. |
| abs | 0.25 | Rectus abdominis braces but is not a mover; lowered from current 0.5. |
| delt_front | 0.25 | Overhead arm is a locked isometric hold; deltoid/rotator cuff stabilise but produce no movement. Minor credit as the visible proxy for overhead stability; lowered from current 0.5. Triceps credit removed for windmills (elbow locked, no extension work). |

Exercises: Advanced Kettlebell Windmill, Bent Press, Double Kettlebell Windmill, Kettlebell Windmill.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Bent Press**: obliques 1; delt_front 0.5; triceps 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7}; erectors 0.5; hamstrings 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1}; glutes 0.5 {glute_max 0.7, glute_med 0.3}; abs 0.25 — The bent press actively presses the bell to lockout while the body bends under it, so anterior deltoid and triceps do real concentric work (kept at 0.5 as in the current row); quad credit removed (knee stays mostly extended on the loaded side; the slight knee bend is unloaded).

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Lateral-flexion tasks primarily target the external oblique; back- and hip-extension components load the erectors only moderately. — Konrad P, Schmitz K, Denner A. Neuromuscular evaluation of trunk-training exercises. J Athl Train. 2001;36(2):109-118. PMID 12937449.
- [emg, verified] Quadratus lumborum and obliques are the loaded muscles in standing lateral bending and increase activation with compressive load. — McGill S, Juker D, Kropf P. Quantitative intramuscular myoelectric activity of quadratus lumborum during a wide variety of tasks. Clin Biomech (Bristol). 1996;11(3):170-172. PMID 11415616.
- [anatomical, unverified] No peer-reviewed EMG study of the kettlebell windmill or bent press exists on PubMed (search 'kettlebell windmill OR bent press electromyography' returned only unrelated records); hip/hamstring/shoulder credits follow from the hinge and overhead-hold mechanics described in practitioner guides. — Author's anatomical reasoning; execution confirmed against BarBend and StrongFirst windmill guides (practitioner sources).

Notes: Low confidence because no direct EMG exists. Changes versus current: hamstring split corrected to hip_ext 1.0 (short head cannot be loaded by a hinge), triceps removed from windmills, delt_front reduced to 0.25, abs reduced to 0.25.

### Balance board

`balance_board_ankle_stability` · 1 exercises · confidence low, unverified

Standing balance on a wobble/rocker board. Ankle plantarflexors (soleus > gastrocnemius), peroneals and tibialis anterior make small, continuous corrective contractions; thigh and hip muscles are minimally loaded. This is a proprioceptive drill with little hypertrophic stimulus for any group.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| calves | 1 {gastrocnemius 0.4, soleus 0.6} | Soleus shows the highest activation of the lower-leg muscles during single-leg stance on unstable platforms (Tan 2024); soleus is the dominant postural plantarflexor, so the split is biased toward it (current 0.7/0.3 reversed). Given 1.0 only because every family needs a primary; absolute stimulus is low. |
| tibialis | 0.5 | Tibialis anterior and peroneus longus co-contract to correct anterior/lateral sway on wobble boards (Silva 2015: peroneus longus significantly higher on the board; Sánchez-Barbadora 2022: high activation on BOSU and wobble board). Peroneals have no group; tibialis is the nearest visible anterior/lateral shin proxy. |

Exercises: Balance Board.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Soleus displayed the highest activation during single-leg stance and single-leg squat on an unstable platform; peroneus longus the highest during the lunge; unstable platforms increased ankle-muscle activation versus flat ground. — Tan F, Harput G, Ulusoy B, Guney-Deniz H. The influence of unstable surfaces on ankle muscle activation during functional exercises. J Bodyw Mov Ther. 2024;40:602-609. PMID 39593651.
- [emg, verified] Single-leg standing on a wobble board significantly increased peroneus longus and biceps femoris activity relative to a firm surface. — Silva PB, Oliveira AS, Mrachacz-Kersting N, Laessoe U, Kersting UG. Strategies for equilibrium maintenance during single leg standing on a wobble board. Gait Posture. 2016;44:149-154. PMID 27004649.
- [emg, verified] BOSU and wobble board produced high EMG in most lower-limb muscles during single-leg stance, with no device-specific difference for peroneus longus. — Sánchez-Barbadora M, Cuerda-Del Pino A, González-Rosalén J, Moreno-Segura N, Escriche-Escuder A, Martín-San Agustín R. Differences in lower limb muscle activation between global and selective instability devices in single-leg stance in healthy active subjects. PeerJ. 2022;10:e13317. PMID 35462768.

Notes: Current hamstring and quad credit removed: activation is postural-level and would mislead users into thinking a balance drill contributes leg volume.

## hinge posterior

### Conventional deadlift (floor pull, hip-width stance)

`conventional_deadlift` · 5 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Bilateral hip-and-knee extension from the floor with a barbell in front of the shins; large lumbar-extensor moment held near-isometrically, hip extension by glute max and biarticular hamstrings, knee extension by the vasti in the first pull, static scapular/grip load. Includes accommodating-resistance (bands, chains, reverse bands) and deficit variants.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| erectors | 1 | Erector spinae is among the most activated muscles in deadlift variants (Martín-Fuentes 2020) and conventional/sumo produce the largest hip and lumbar moments at lockout (Gundersen 2025). Kept as current. |
| glutes | 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} | Conventional deadlift is classed as very high glute max activation (>60% MVIC; Neto 2020) and both hip thrust and squat training transfer to the deadlift (Plotkin 2023); strong synergist = 0.75, matching the squat anchor. Kept as current. |
| hamstrings | 0.75 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} | Biarticular hamstrings extend the hip; knee is extending so the short head is unloaded. Hamstring EMG greatest at low knee-flexion intervals near lockout (Escamilla 2002). Kept as current. |
| quads | 0.5 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9} | Quadriceps activation is consistently greater than glute max and biceps femoris in deadlift EMG (Martín-Fuentes 2020) but the knee moment is well below a squat's; half credit. Kept as current. |
| adductors | 0.25 | Deadlift is tier 1 for adductor magnus (ischiocondylar) force in an EMG-driven model (Collings 2026) and hip adductor EMG rises with knee flexion (Escamilla 2002); minor credit because the inner-thigh adductors (longus/brevis) the user sees coloured are only tier 2-3. Added (was absent). |
| upper_back | 0.5 {traps_mid_lower 0.6, rhomboids 0.4} | Middle trapezius is active throughout the pull (Escamilla 2002); load is near-isometric scapular retraction/depression under very heavy load, which practitioners consistently credit for upper-back size. Kept at 0.5. |
| traps_upper | 0.5 | Upper trapezius EMG greatest in the low-knee-flexion (lockout) intervals (Escamilla 2002); heavy static shrug load. Kept as current. |
| lats | 0.25 | Lats keep the bar against the body via near-isometric shoulder extension; no hypertrophy evidence, so minor credit only. Harmonised: Barbell_Deadlift currently 0.5, the variants 0.25. |
| forearms | 0.5 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2} | Maximal isometric grip on the heaviest lift most users perform; consensus credits deadlifts for grip/forearm development. Kept at 0.5 with a stronger flexor bias than current (.7/.3). |

Exercises: Barbell Deadlift, Deadlift with Bands, Deadlift with Chains, Deficit Deadlift, Reverse Band Deadlift.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Deficit Deadlift**: erectors 1; glutes 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; hamstrings 0.75 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1}; quads 0.75 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; adductors 0.25; upper_back 0.5 {traps_mid_lower 0.6, rhomboids 0.4}; traps_upper 0.5; lats 0.25; forearms 0.5 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2} — Standing on a platform increases starting knee and hip flexion; quadriceps, adductor and glute EMG all peak in the high-knee-flexion intervals (Escamilla 2002), so the vasti earn strong-synergist credit (anatomical reasoning; no deficit-specific hypertrophy data).

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Systematic review of deadlift EMG: erector spinae and quadriceps show greater activation than glute max and biceps femoris; semitendinosus slightly exceeds biceps femoris; RDL shows lower erector activation than the biarticular hamstrings. — Martín-Fuentes I, Oliva-Lozano JM, Muyor JM. Electromyographic activity in deadlift exercise and its variants. A systematic review. PLoS One. 2020;15(2):e0229507. PMID 32107499.
- [emg, verified] 16-muscle EMG of sumo vs conventional deadlift: vastus medialis/lateralis and tibialis anterior greater in sumo, medial gastrocnemius greater in conventional; quads, adductors, glute max, paraspinals and middle trapezius peak in high knee-flexion intervals, hamstrings and upper trapezius near lockout; moderate-to-high quad/hamstring/gastrocnemius co-contraction. — Escamilla RF, Francisco AC, Kayes AV, Speer KP, Moorman CT. An electromyographic analysis of sumo and conventional style deadlifts. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 2002;34(4):682-688. PMID 11932579.
- [emg, verified] Conventional deadlift and hex-bar deadlift are among exercises eliciting very high (>60% MVIC) gluteus maximus activation. — Neto WK, Soares EG, Vieira TL, et al. Gluteus Maximus Activation during Common Strength and Hypertrophy Exercises: A Systematic Review. J Sports Sci Med. 2020;19(1):195-203. PMID 32132843.
- [emg, verified] Conventional and sumo 3RM deadlifts produce larger hip net joint moments near lockout than hex-bar; hex-bar shifts load to the knee extensors. — Gundersen AH, van den Tillaar R, Falch HN, Fredriksen AB, Larsen S. A Biomechanical Comparison Between Conventional, Sumo, and Hex-Bar Deadlifts Among Resistance Trained Women. J Strength Cond Res. 2025;39(3):281-288. PMID 39705135.
- [emg, verified] EMG-assisted musculoskeletal model: deadlift and sumo deadlift are tier 1 for adductor magnus (ischiocondylar) and gracilis force; high adductor magnus forces occur in hip-extension-oriented exercises. — Collings TJ, et al. Hip Adductor Muscle Forces during Strength Training and Rehabilitation Exercises. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 2026 (epub Apr 3). PMID 41931009.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Nine weeks of hip thrust or back squat training produced similar gluteus mCSA growth and transferred similarly to deadlift 3RM, indicating the deadlift shares the glute-max hip-extension demand. — Plotkin DL, Rodas MA, Vigotsky AD, et al. Hip thrust and back squat training elicit similar gluteus muscle hypertrophy and transfer similarly to the deadlift. Front Physiol. 2023;14:1279170. PMID 37877099.

Notes: Calves removed from Barbell_Deadlift (current 0.5): the squat anchor gives no calf credit and the deadlift has less ankle motion; Escamilla's gastrocnemius co-contraction is mostly stabilising. Bands/chains/reverse bands change the strength curve (more lockout load) but not the muscle set. Adductors added at 0.25 on evidence that was not available to the original guess.

### Sumo deadlift (wide stance, toes out)

`sumo_deadlift` · 4 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Wide-stance floor pull with a more upright torso: shorter lumbar moment arm, more knee extension and more hip abduction/external-rotation demand, so the vasti, adductors and gluteals share more of the work than in the conventional pull.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| glutes | 1 {glute_max 0.8, glute_med 0.2} | Hip extension from an abducted, externally rotated position; sumo shares the largest hip lockout moments with conventional (Gundersen 2025). The abducted stance recruits glute med/upper glute max more than a hip-width pull, hence .8/.2. Changed from current (hamstrings 1.0, glutes 0.5). |
| quads | 0.75 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9} | Vastus medialis and lateralis EMG significantly greater in sumo than conventional (Escamilla 2002); more vertical shin and larger knee moment. Raised from 0.5. |
| adductors | 0.75 | Sumo deadlift is tier 1 for adductor magnus and gracilis force (Collings 2026) and the abducted stance puts the adductor mass on stretch; stronger than the squat anchor's 0.5. Raised from 0.5. |
| hamstrings | 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} | Hamstring EMG did not differ between styles (Escamilla 2002) but less hip flexion at the start means a shorter hamstring working range; secondary credit. Lowered from 1.0. |
| erectors | 0.75 | Paraspinal EMG similar between styles (Escamilla 2002) but the upright torso shortens the lumbar moment arm; strong synergist rather than primary. Raised from 0.5. |
| upper_back | 0.5 {traps_mid_lower 0.6, rhomboids 0.4} | Same static scapular load as the conventional pull (middle trapezius active; Escamilla 2002). Kept. |
| traps_upper | 0.5 | Upper trapezius active near lockout (Escamilla 2002). Kept. |
| lats | 0.25 | Near-isometric bar-path control only; minor credit for consistency with the conventional family. |
| forearms | 0.5 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2} | Maximal grip; consistent with the conventional family. |

Exercises: Reverse Band Sumo Deadlift, Sumo Deadlift, Sumo Deadlift with Bands, Sumo Deadlift with Chains.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Vastus medialis, vastus lateralis and tibialis anterior EMG were significantly greater in the sumo deadlift; medial gastrocnemius greater in conventional; hamstring and paraspinal EMG did not differ between styles. — Escamilla RF, Francisco AC, Kayes AV, Speer KP, Moorman CT. An electromyographic analysis of sumo and conventional style deadlifts. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 2002;34(4):682-688. PMID 11932579.
- [emg, verified] Sumo deadlift is tier 1 for adductor magnus (ischiocondylar) and gracilis muscle force in an EMG-assisted neuromusculoskeletal model. — Collings TJ, et al. Hip Adductor Muscle Forces during Strength Training and Rehabilitation Exercises. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 2026 (epub Apr 3). PMID 41931009.
- [emg, verified] Sumo and conventional deadlifts both produce large hip net joint moments near lockout in strength-trained women (3RM), i.e. sumo remains a hip-extensor-dominant lift. — Gundersen AH, van den Tillaar R, Falch HN, Fredriksen AB, Larsen S. A Biomechanical Comparison Between Conventional, Sumo, and Hex-Bar Deadlifts Among Resistance Trained Women. J Strength Cond Res. 2025;39(3):281-288. PMID 39705135.
- [emg, verified] Across deadlift variants, erector spinae and quadriceps activation exceed glute max and biceps femoris activation. — Martín-Fuentes I, Oliva-Lozano JM, Muyor JM. Electromyographic activity in deadlift exercise and its variants. A systematic review. PLoS One. 2020;15(2):e0229507. PMID 32107499.

Notes: Current mapping made hamstrings the sole primary mover, which the EMG does not support (hamstring EMG equal to conventional while quads, adductors and hip moment are higher). Calves dropped (current 0.5 on two variants): plantarflexor range is minimal and the squat anchor gives no calf credit. Accommodating resistance variants share the family mapping.

### Romanian / stiff-legged deadlift

`romanian_stiff_leg_deadlift` · 5 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Hip hinge with near-locked knees and the load descending along the thigh: hamstrings lengthen under load at the hip while the knee stays extended, so the biarticular hamstrings (semis and BF long head) and glute max extend the hip and the erectors hold the spine.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| hamstrings | 1 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} | Fixed anchor row. RDL hamstring EMG 73-115% MVC with ST over BF (Zebis 2013); stiff-leg deadlift activates BFlh and ST almost equally (Hegyi 2018); the short head does not cross the hip and the knee does not flex, so it gets no share. |
| glutes | 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} | Fixed anchor row. RDL glute max activation not different from barbell hip thrust at 1RM (Delgado 2019) and equal to the 45-degree Roman chair (Andersen 2021). |
| erectors | 0.5 | Fixed anchor row. RDL erector activation is lower than biceps femoris/semitendinosus (Martín-Fuentes 2020); largely isometric spinal hold. |

Exercises: Kettlebell One-Legged Deadlift, Romanian Deadlift, Smith Machine Stiff-Legged Deadlift, Stiff-Legged Barbell Deadlift, Stiff-Legged Dumbbell Deadlift.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Kettlebell One-Legged Deadlift**: hamstrings 1 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1}; glutes 0.75 {glute_max 0.7, glute_med 0.3}; erectors 0.25 — Unilateral stance: glute med must control the pelvis in the frontal plane (single-limb deadlift glute med 59% MVIC, Distefano 2009), so the glute share shifts toward glute_med; the light kettlebell load means the lumbar extensors see far less than a barbell RDL, so erectors drop to minor credit.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Romanian deadlift and kettlebell swing target semitendinosus over biceps femoris at very high normalised EMG (73-115% MVC). — Zebis MK, Skotte J, Andersen CH, et al. Kettlebell swing targets semitendinosus and supine leg curl targets biceps femoris: an EMG study with rehabilitation implications. Br J Sports Med. 2013;47(18):1192-1198. PMID 22736206.
- [emg, verified] RDL was as effective as the barbell hip thrust for glute max activation at 1RM and both isolated the hip extensors relative to the back squat; vastus lateralis activity was far lower in RDL than squat. — Delgado J, Drinkwater EJ, Banyard HG, Haff GG, Nosaka K. Comparison Between Back Squat, Romanian Deadlift, and Barbell Hip Thrust for Leg and Hip Muscle Activities During Hip Extension. J Strength Cond Res. 2019;33(10):2595-2601. PMID 31356511.
- [emg, verified] High-density EMG: in the stiff-leg deadlift BFlh and ST activity were near-identical (d=0.19) with small regional differences, unlike the Nordic where ST dominated. — Hegyi A, Péter A, Finni T, Cronin NJ. Region-dependent hamstrings activity in Nordic hamstring exercise and stiff-leg deadlift defined with high-density electromyography. Scand J Med Sci Sports. 2018;28(3):992-1000. PMID 29143379.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Nine weeks of seated leg curl plus stiff-leg deadlift at high load increased semitendinosus volume by ~26% (3D ultrasound); BF long head did not change significantly. — Frouin A, Le Sant G, Barbier L, et al. Individual distribution of muscle hypertrophy among hamstring muscle heads: Adding muscle volume where you need is not so simple. Scand J Med Sci Sports. 2024;34(3):e14608. PMID 38515303.
- [emg, verified] 6RM RDL, 45-degree Roman chair and seated back extension produced equal erector spinae activation; RDL and Roman chair activated glute max 94-140% more than the seated machine. — Andersen V, Pedersen H, Fimland MS, et al. Comparison of Muscle Activity in Three Single-Joint, Hip Extension Exercises in Resistance-Trained Women. J Sports Sci Med. 2021;20(2):181-187. PMID 33948095.
- [emg, verified] Single-limb deadlift activated gluteus medius (59% MVIC) and gluteus maximus (59% MVIC) similarly, well above lateral band walk and clam for glute max. — Distefano LJ, Blackburn JT, Marshall SW, Padua DA. Gluteal muscle activation during common therapeutic exercises. J Orthop Sports Phys Ther. 2009;39(7):532-540. PMID 19574661.

Notes: Current mapping already equals the anchor for the four bilateral variants; kept. Stiff-legged (straighter knee, more lumbar rounding in practice) shares the RDL mapping: the extra spinal flexion is not a reason to raise erector credit beyond the anchor. Smith machine version is identical in muscle set.

### Good morning (bar-on-back hinge)

`good_morning` · 7 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Hip hinge with the load on the upper back: same hip-extensor demand as an RDL but a much longer lever on the lumbar spine, so the erectors take a larger share while the biarticular hamstrings and glute max extend the hip.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| hamstrings | 1 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} | Hamstring IEMG rises with load and the movement is a knee-near-locked hinge (Vigotsky 2015); knee flexion increases slightly with load but the short head is not a hip extensor. Kept as current. |
| erectors | 0.75 | Spinal erector IEMG increases with load (Vigotsky 2015); the bar sits on the shoulders so the lumbar extensor moment arm is far longer than in an RDL, justifying strong-synergist credit above the RDL anchor's 0.5. Raised from 0.5 on barbell versions. |
| glutes | 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} | Bilateral hip extension from moderate hip flexion, same as the RDL anchor. Kept. |

Exercises: Band Good Morning, Band Good Morning (Pull Through), Good Morning, Good Morning off Pins, Hanging Bar Good Morning, Seated Good Mornings, Stiff Leg Barbell Good Morning.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Seated Good Mornings**: erectors 1; hamstrings 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1}; glutes 0.5 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} — Seated with knees at ~90 degrees the hamstrings are slack at the knee and hip range is limited, so the movement becomes mainly lumbar/thoracic extension against the bar; erectors become primary and hip extensors drop to half credit (anatomical reasoning).

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Across 50-90% 1RM good mornings, integrated EMG of hamstrings and spinal erectors tended to increase with load; knee flexion increased and estimated hamstring length decreased with load; lumbar and hip flexion unchanged. — Vigotsky AD, Harper EN, Ryan DR, Contreras B. Effects of load on good morning kinematics and EMG activity. PeerJ. 2015;3:e708. PMID 25653899.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Hip-extension (hinge) training lengthens BFlh fascicles and increases BFlh and semitendinosus volume, more than the Nordic for BFlh. — Bourne MN, Duhig SJ, Timmins RG, et al. Impact of the Nordic hamstring and hip extension exercises on hamstring architecture and morphology: implications for injury prevention. Br J Sports Med. 2017;51(5):469-477. PMID 27660368.
- [emg, verified] RDL, Roman chair and seated back extension produced equal erector spinae activation at 6RM, i.e. loaded hinges load the erectors near their exercise-specific maximum regardless of variant. — Andersen V, Pedersen H, Fimland MS, et al. Comparison of Muscle Activity in Three Single-Joint, Hip Extension Exercises in Resistance-Trained Women. J Sports Sci Med. 2021;20(2):181-187. PMID 33948095.
- [anatomical, likely] Bar-on-back placement lengthens the external moment arm about L5/S1 relative to a bar held in the hands, increasing lumbar extensor demand for the same hip moment. — Standard biomechanical reasoning (lever arm of external load about the lumbar spine); consistent with Vigotsky 2015 kinematics.

Notes: Abs 0.5 removed from the three barbell versions: trunk bracing is isometric and the brief excludes stabiliser credit. Band good mornings (including the 'pull through' variant, which is a band around the neck anchored behind) share the mapping; band tension peaks at lockout, which shifts load away from the stretched position but does not change the muscle set.

### Back extension / hyperextension (45-degree, flat bench, ball, reverse)

`back_extension` · 4 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Trunk or leg raised against gravity with the pelvis (or trunk) fixed: lumbar erectors extend the spine while glute max and the biarticular hamstrings extend the hip. The 45-degree bench version is the '45-degree hip extension' used in the hamstring literature.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| erectors | 1 | Roman chair back extension produced erector activation equal to a 6RM RDL (Andersen 2021); prone back extension loads ES with GM and hamstrings as co-extensors (Plamondon 2004). Kept as current. |
| hamstrings | 0.75 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} | 45-degree hip extension training grew BFlh volume more than the Nordic (Bourne 2017) and activates all three biarticular heads (Messer 2018); Roman chair BF activation exceeded RDL (Andersen 2021). Raised from 0.5; knee is straight so bf_short gets 0. |
| glutes | 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} | Roman chair glute max activation equals RDL and far exceeds the seated machine (Andersen 2021); strong synergist. Raised from 0.5. |

Exercises: Hyperextensions (Back Extensions), Hyperextensions With No Hyperextension Bench, Reverse Hyperextension, Weighted Ball Hyperextension.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Weighted Ball Hyperextension**: erectors 1; glutes 0.5 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; hamstrings 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} — Prone over a ball with feet on the floor the motion is mostly trunk extension over the ball, so the hip extensors assist at half credit. Upper_back 0.5 removed: holding a plate to the chest is not scapular retraction work.
- **Reverse Hyperextension**: glutes 1 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; hamstrings 1 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1}; erectors 0.5 — Trunk is fixed and the legs swing: hip extensors are the movers (higher GM, BF and ST than RDL, Dicus 2023) while the lumbar extensors hold a large but near-isometric moment with little lumbar flexion (Lawrence 2019). Current mapping's calves 0.5 dropped (no ankle work) and hamstring distribution set to hip_ext only (knees stay straight).

Evidence:

- [hypertrophy, verified] 10 weeks of 45-degree hip extension training increased BFlh volume more than Nordic training and control, and increased semitendinosus volume as much as the Nordic. — Bourne MN, Duhig SJ, Timmins RG, et al. Impact of the Nordic hamstring and hip extension exercises on hamstring architecture and morphology: implications for injury prevention. Br J Sports Med. 2017;51(5):469-477. PMID 27660368.
- [emg, verified] fMRI: 45-degree hip extension increased T2 of BFlh, ST and SM more than BFsh and gave a higher BFlh:ST ratio than the Nordic, i.e. it evenly loads the biarticular hamstrings. — Messer DJ, Bourne MN, Williams MD, Al Najjar A, Shield AJ. Hamstring Muscle Use in Women During Hip Extension and the Nordic Hamstring Exercise: A Functional Magnetic Resonance Imaging Study. J Orthop Sports Phys Ther. 2018;48(8):607-612. PMID 29685059.
- [emg, verified] 45-degree Roman chair back extension activated biceps femoris 71-174% more than RDL and seated back extension, glute max equal to RDL, and erector spinae equal across all three. — Andersen V, Pedersen H, Fimland MS, et al. Comparison of Muscle Activity in Three Single-Joint, Hip Extension Exercises in Resistance-Trained Women. J Sports Sci Med. 2021;20(2):181-187. PMID 33948095.
- [emg, verified] Reverse hyperextension vs hyperextension at equal load: HE gave greater integrated biceps femoris and glute max activity, RHE gave greater low-back moment, greater hip ROM, less lumbar flexion and equivalent erector spinae activity. — Lawrence MA, Chin A, Swanson BT. Biomechanical Comparison of the Reverse Hyperextension Machine and the Hyperextension Exercise. J Strength Cond Res. 2019;33(8):2053-2056. PMID 30946266.
- [emg, verified] Compared with the RDL at 50% RM, the open-chain reverse hyperextension increased glute max (+19.5%), biceps femoris (+27.9%) and semitendinosus (+18.2%) activation. — Dicus JR, et al. A Comparison of Muscle Recruitment Across Three Straight-Legged, Hinge-Pattern Resistance Training Exercises. Int J Exerc Sci. 2023;16(1). PMID 37113509.
- [emg, verified] Prone bench back extension recruits erector spinae with glute max and hamstrings; adding a 45 N weight increased hamstring more than erector activation and hip-extensor fatigue limited task performance. — Plamondon A, Trimble K, Larivière C, Desjardins P. Back muscle fatigue during intermittent prone back extension exercise. Scand J Med Sci Sports. 2004;14(4):221-230. PMID 15265144.

Notes: The 45-degree 'hyperextension' is one of the best-evidenced hamstring hypertrophy exercises (Bourne 2017), so hamstring credit is raised to 0.75 rather than the current 0.5. Technique varies (rounded-back = erector emphasis; flat-back hinge = hip-extensor emphasis); the family mapping sits between.

### Cable pull-through and kettlebell swing

`pull_through_swing` · 2 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Hip hinge with the resistance pulled between the legs from behind, finishing in forceful hip extension (glute lockout); the swing is the ballistic version. Line of pull is horizontal so the lumbar extensors are loaded less than in a barbell hinge.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| glutes | 1 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} | Redirected resistance loads the hip extension lockout; pull-through glute max activation did not differ from RDL while erector and hamstring activation fell (Dicus 2023). Kept as current. |
| hamstrings | 0.75 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} | Kettlebell swing hamstring EMG 73-115% MVC, ST-dominant (Zebis 2013; Sahinis 2025); the hip-hinge swing gives the highest hamstring EMG of the swing styles (Del Monte 2020). Raised from 0.5; bf_short 0 because the knee is nearly locked. |
| erectors | 0.5 | Erector activation is lower than in the RDL when resistance is redirected through a pulley (Dicus 2023); in the swing the spine is held isometrically neutral. Kept. |

Exercises: One-Arm Kettlebell Swings, Pull Through.

Per-exercise differences:

- **One-Arm Kettlebell Swings**: glutes 1 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; hamstrings 0.75 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1}; erectors 0.5; delt_front 0.25; forearms 0.25 {forearm_flexors 0.9, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.1} — The arm is a passive lever in a correct swing (hips project the bell), so front delt credit drops from the current 0.5 to minor; one-arm grip on a swinging bell earns minor forearm-flexor credit. Calves 0.5 removed: plantarflexion is not loaded. Obliques (anti-rotation) deliberately not credited per the isometric-stabiliser rule.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Switching from RDL to cable pull-through significantly decreased longissimus (-11%), multifidus (-14%), biceps femoris (-13%) and semitendinosus (-7%) activation with no significant change in glute max. — Dicus JR, et al. A Comparison of Muscle Recruitment Across Three Straight-Legged, Hinge-Pattern Resistance Training Exercises. Int J Exerc Sci. 2023;16(1). PMID 37113509.
- [emg, verified] Kettlebell swing targeted semitendinosus over biceps femoris at very high normalised EMG (73-115% MVC). — Zebis MK, Skotte J, Andersen CH, et al. Kettlebell swing targets semitendinosus and supine leg curl targets biceps femoris: an EMG study with rehabilitation implications. Br J Sports Med. 2013;47(18):1192-1198. PMID 22736206.
- [emg, verified] Hip-hinge style kettlebell swing produced greater medial hamstring and biceps femoris sEMG than squat-style or double-knee-extension swings; medial hamstrings exceeded BF across styles. — Del Monte MJ, Opar DA, Timmins RG, Ross JA, Keogh JWL, Lorenzen C. Hamstring Myoelectrical Activity During Three Different Kettlebell Swing Exercises. J Strength Cond Res. 2020;34(7):1953-1958. PMID 28930870.
- [emg, verified] Meta-analysis: semitendinosus EMG exceeds biceps femoris in kettlebell swings (SMD -0.59) while BF exceeds ST in back extensions. — Sahinis C, et al. Differences in activation amplitude between semitendinosus and biceps femoris during hamstring exercises: A systematic and critical review with meta-analysis. J Sports Sci. 2025. PMID 40192215.

Notes: No longitudinal hypertrophy study exists for either exercise; weights are calibrated to the RDL anchor via the EMG comparisons above. Ballistic swings are usually done for high reps, so the repFactor rule already discounts them.

### Cleans and pulls (barbell, dumbbell, kettlebell)

`olympic_clean_pull` · 11 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Explosive triple extension (hip, knee, ankle) with a shrug and, for cleans, a front-rack catch. Muscle set is a deadlift-like pull plus a violent shrug/plantarflexion; the lower-body work is quad-and-glute dominant rather than hamstring dominant.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| glutes | 1 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} | Glute max is a predominant muscle in hang power clean/pull EMG (Geisler 2023) and hip extension velocity drives the second pull. Changed: current has hamstrings 1.0 and glutes 0.5. |
| quads | 0.75 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9} | Vastus lateralis is one of the highest-activity muscles in cleans and pulls (Geisler 2023); knee re-bend and extension in the second pull plus deadlift-like first pull from the floor. Raised from 0.5. |
| erectors | 0.75 | Erector spinae activity is high and greater in the clean than the pull for beginners (Geisler 2023); holding the torso angle through the first pull is a large lumbar moment. Raised from 0.5. |
| traps_upper | 0.75 | Trapezius EMG is high in both cleans and pulls and greater in pulls at lighter loads (Geisler 2023); the shrug is the defining upper-body action. Raised from 0.5. |
| hamstrings | 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} | Hamstrings extend the hip in the first pull but the second pull is quad/glute driven; secondary. Lowered from 1.0; knee is extending so the short head gets 0. |
| upper_back | 0.5 {traps_mid_lower 0.6, rhomboids 0.4} | Scapular retraction/elevation during the pull and catch; same static load as a deadlift. Kept where present, added to variants lacking it. |
| calves | 0.5 {gastrocnemius 0.7, soleus 0.3} | Ballistic plantarflexion is part of triple extension (unlike squats/deadlifts), so calves earn secondary credit. |
| forearms | 0.5 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2} | Hook/overhand grip on an accelerating load. |
| delt_front | 0.25 | Anterior deltoid works in the front-rack catch and turnover; minor credit. |

Exercises: Alternating Hang Clean, Double Kettlebell Alternating Hang Clean, Dumbbell Clean, Kettlebell Dead Clean, Kettlebell Hang Clean, One-Arm Kettlebell Clean, One-Arm Open Palm Kettlebell Clean, Open Palm Kettlebell Clean, Power Clean, Smith Machine Hang Power Clean, Snatch Pull.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Alternating Hang Clean**: glutes 1 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; quads 0.5 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; erectors 0.5; traps_upper 0.75; hamstrings 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1}; upper_back 0.5 {traps_mid_lower 0.6, rhomboids 0.4}; calves 0.5 {gastrocnemius 0.7, soleus 0.3}; forearms 0.5 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2}; delt_front 0.25 — Hang start removes the floor first pull, so quadriceps and erector demand fall to standard secondary credit; rest as family.
- **Double Kettlebell Alternating Hang Clean**: glutes 1 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; quads 0.5 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; erectors 0.5; traps_upper 0.75; hamstrings 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1}; upper_back 0.5 {traps_mid_lower 0.6, rhomboids 0.4}; calves 0.5 {gastrocnemius 0.7, soleus 0.3}; forearms 0.5 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2}; delt_front 0.25 — Hang start: quads and erectors at 0.5 (no first pull from the floor).
- **Kettlebell Hang Clean**: glutes 1 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; quads 0.5 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; erectors 0.5; traps_upper 0.75; hamstrings 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1}; upper_back 0.5 {traps_mid_lower 0.6, rhomboids 0.4}; calves 0.5 {gastrocnemius 0.7, soleus 0.3}; forearms 0.5 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2}; delt_front 0.25 — Hang start: quads and erectors at 0.5.
- **Smith Machine Hang Power Clean**: glutes 1 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; quads 0.5 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; erectors 0.5; traps_upper 0.75; hamstrings 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1}; upper_back 0.5 {traps_mid_lower 0.6, rhomboids 0.4}; calves 0.5 {gastrocnemius 0.7, soleus 0.3}; forearms 0.5 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2}; delt_front 0.25 — Hang start on a fixed bar path: quads and erectors at 0.5; the guided bar does not change the muscle set.
- **Snatch Pull**: glutes 1 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; quads 0.75 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; erectors 0.75; traps_upper 0.75; hamstrings 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1}; upper_back 0.5 {traps_mid_lower 0.6, rhomboids 0.4}; calves 0.5 {gastrocnemius 0.7, soleus 0.3}; forearms 0.5 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2} — No catch, so no front-delt credit; wide grip and heavy loads make the shrug/trapezius demand at least as high as in the clean (Geisler 2023 found greater trapezius EMG in pulls at submaximal loads).

Evidence:
