- [emg, verified] During hang power clean/snatch and their pulls, EMG of vastus lateralis, glute max, erector spinae and trapezius was recorded; pattern of the predominant muscles is similar between lifts and pulls above 70% 1RM, with trapezius favouring pulls at lighter loads and erector spinae greater in the full lifts. — Geisler S, Havers T, Isenmann E, et al. Effects of Expertise on Muscle Activity during the Hang Power Clean and Hang Power Snatch Compared to Snatch and Clean Pulls - An Explorative Analysis. J Sports Sci Med. 2023;22(4):778-789. PMID 38045750.
- [emg, verified] Deadlift-type pulls from the floor activate erector spinae and quadriceps more than glute max and biceps femoris. — Martín-Fuentes I, Oliva-Lozano JM, Muyor JM. Electromyographic activity in deadlift exercise and its variants. A systematic review. PLoS One. 2020;15(2):e0229507. PMID 32107499.
- [emg, verified] Upper trapezius and gastrocnemius EMG peak in the low-knee-flexion (extension/lockout) phase of a pull, consistent with the shrug and plantarflexion of the second pull. — Escamilla RF, Francisco AC, Kayes AV, Speer KP, Moorman CT. An electromyographic analysis of sumo and conventional style deadlifts. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 2002;34(4):682-688. PMID 11932579.
- [consensus, likely] Weightlifting derivatives are triple-extension movements where the knee re-bends (double knee bend) and extends explosively; the arms act as hooks until the catch. — Practitioner consensus (NSCA Essentials of Strength Training and Conditioning, weightlifting technique chapters).

Notes: These are power exercises, typically 1-5 reps and rarely to failure; the repFactor (<3 reps -> 0.5, <5 -> 0.75) already discounts them and no per-muscle hypertrophy data exist. Biceps 0.5 removed from Alternating_Hang_Clean (arms should not curl the bell). Kettlebell cleans are light, so the set counts but the loading is modest; consider a global implement discount (open question).

### Leg curl (knee-flexion isolation: seated, lying, standing, band, ball/slide, manual)

`leg_curl` · 8 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Knee flexion against resistance with the hip fixed (or, in bridge-type curls, held extended by the glutes). Loads all four hamstring heads including the short head; gastrocnemius assists knee flexion. Hip angle sets biarticular-head length: seated (hip flexed) lengthens them and grows the biarticular heads more than prone.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| hamstrings | 1 {hamstrings_hip_ext 0.6, hamstrings_bf_short 0.4} | Fixed anchor row for Seated_Leg_Curl. Flywheel leg curl produced the largest T2 shifts of any hamstring exercise in BFsh (51%) and ST (65%) (Fernandez-Gonzalo 2016); seated curl training grows the whole hamstring more than prone (Maeo 2021); seated curl + SLDL program grew ST ~26% (Frouin 2024). |

Exercises: Ball Leg Curl, Leg Lift, Lying Leg Curls, Platform Hamstring Slides, Prone Manual Hamstring, Seated Band Hamstring Curl, Seated Leg Curl, Standing Leg Curl.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Lying Leg Curls**: hamstrings 1 {hamstrings_hip_ext 0.5, hamstrings_bf_short 0.5} — Prone (hip extended) shortens the biarticular heads, which grow less than in the seated curl (Maeo 2021) while the short head is unaffected by hip angle; emphasis shifts one step toward bf_short relative to the seated anchor.
- **Standing Leg Curl**: hamstrings 1 {hamstrings_hip_ext 0.5, hamstrings_bf_short 0.5} — Hip neutral (same length state as prone), unilateral; same shift toward the short head as the lying curl.
- **Ball Leg Curl**: hamstrings 1 {hamstrings_hip_ext 0.6, hamstrings_bf_short 0.4}; glutes 0.5 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; calves 0.25 {gastrocnemius 1} — Supine bridge-curl: hips are held extended by glute max through the curl (secondary credit) and the heels drive into the ball so gastrocnemius is loaded as a knee flexor; supine curl is BF-dominant (Zebis 2013). Calves lowered from current 0.5 to minor.
- **Platform Hamstring Slides**: hamstrings 1 {hamstrings_hip_ext 0.6, hamstrings_bf_short 0.4}; glutes 0.5 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; calves 0.25 {gastrocnemius 1} — Single-leg supine slide curl: same mechanics as the ball curl (bridge held by glutes, heel-loaded gastrocnemius).
- **Prone Manual Hamstring**: hamstrings 1 {hamstrings_hip_ext 0.5, hamstrings_bf_short 0.5} — Partner-resisted prone curl: identical joint configuration to the lying machine curl.
- **Leg Lift**: hamstrings 1 {hamstrings_hip_ext 0.5, hamstrings_bf_short 0.5}; glutes 0.25 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} — Standing bodyweight/ankle-weight leg curl with slight hip extension: knee flexion is the loaded action (standing-curl distribution), glute max earns only minor credit for the small hip-extension component. Replaces the current glutes-1.0 guess.

Evidence:

- [hypertrophy, verified] 12 weeks of seated vs prone leg curl (within-subject): seated (long muscle length) produced greater hypertrophy of the biarticular hamstrings and the whole hamstring group than prone; both grew the short head. — Maeo S, Huang M, Wu Y, et al. Greater Hamstrings Muscle Hypertrophy but Similar Damage Protection after Training at Long versus Short Muscle Lengths. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 2021;53(4):825-837. PMID 33009197.
- [emg, verified] fMRI after flywheel leg curl: T2 increased in gracilis (95%), ST (65%), BFsh (51%) and BFlh (14%); the most substantial hamstring use of the four exercises tested. — Fernandez-Gonzalo R, Tesch PA, Linnehan RM, et al. Individual Muscle use in Hamstring Exercises by Soccer Players Assessed using Functional MRI. Int J Sports Med. 2016;37(7):559-564. PMID 27116347.
- [emg, verified] Regional fMRI: flywheel leg curl raised T2 in all regions of BFlh (9-16%), BFsh (41-71%) and ST (60-69%), confirming strong short-head and semitendinosus loading in knee-flexion curls. — Mendez-Villanueva A, Suarez-Arrones L, Rodas G, et al. MRI-Based Regional Muscle Use during Hamstring Strengthening Exercises in Elite Soccer Players. PLoS One. 2016;11(9):e0161356. PMID 27583444.
- [hypertrophy, verified] High-load seated leg curl + stiff-leg deadlift training for 9 weeks selectively increased semitendinosus volume (~26%). — Frouin A, Le Sant G, Barbier L, et al. Individual distribution of muscle hypertrophy among hamstring muscle heads: Adding muscle volume where you need is not so simple. Scand J Med Sci Sports. 2024;34(3):e14608. PMID 38515303.
- [emg, verified] Meta-analysis: biceps femoris EMG exceeds semitendinosus during prone-lying curls (SMD 0.94); supine leg curl targets BF over ST (Zebis 2013). — Sahinis C, et al. Differences in activation amplitude between semitendinosus and biceps femoris during hamstring exercises: A systematic and critical review with meta-analysis. J Sports Sci. 2025. PMID 40192215.
- [emg, verified] Supine leg curl (bridge-type) and hip extension specifically targeted biceps femoris over semitendinosus at 75-87% MVC. — Zebis MK, Skotte J, Andersen CH, et al. Kettlebell swing targets semitendinosus and supine leg curl targets biceps femoris: an EMG study with rehabilitation implications. Br J Sports Med. 2013;47(18):1192-1198. PMID 22736206.

Notes: Seated_Leg_Curl and Seated_Band_Hamstring_Curl keep the anchor exactly. Gastrocnemius contributes to knee flexion in every curl (and grows with flywheel curls per fMRI gracilis/gastroc co-activation reports) but is not credited on the anchor row, so it is only added on the bridge-type curls where the ankle is also loaded. Leg_Lift (free-exercise-db: standing, lift one leg behind you 'as if performing a leg curl') is a bodyweight standing leg curl and belongs here, not with glute kickbacks.

### Glute-ham raise and Nordic curl

`glute_ham_raise_nordic` · 3 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Bodyweight knee flexion with the feet anchored: the lower leg is fixed and the body is the lever, giving a very high (largely eccentric) knee-flexor load. Machine GHR adds a hip-extension phase from the pad; floor/'natural' versions are Nordic curls with the hips held extended isometrically.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| hamstrings | 1 {hamstrings_hip_ext 0.6, hamstrings_bf_short 0.4} | Nordic training grew ST 26%, BFsh 22%, BFlh 9%, SM 6% (Pai 2025) and preferentially recruits ST over BFlh (Bourne 2015/2017; Hegyi 2018); ST is in the hip_ext region and the short head is one small muscle, so .6/.4 mirrors the seated-curl anchor. Kept as current for the machine GHR distribution? No: current is .5/.5 for GHR and .8/.2 for Floor GHR; harmonised to .6/.4. |
| glutes | 0.5 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} | Machine GHR starts with hip extension from a flexed position over the pad before the curl; secondary credit. Kept. |
| calves | 0.25 {gastrocnemius 1} | Feet are plantarflexed into the plate/anchor and gastrocnemius assists knee flexion; minor credit (current 0.5 lowered). |

Exercises: Floor Glute-Ham Raise, Glute Ham Raise, Natural Glute Ham Raise.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Natural Glute Ham Raise**: hamstrings 1 {hamstrings_hip_ext 0.6, hamstrings_bf_short 0.4}; calves 0.25 {gastrocnemius 1} — Nordic curl: hips stay extended isometrically, so no dynamic glute work (stabiliser rule); the whole load is knee flexion.
- **Floor Glute-Ham Raise**: hamstrings 1 {hamstrings_hip_ext 0.6, hamstrings_bf_short 0.4}; calves 0.25 {gastrocnemius 1} — Partner-held Nordic (instructions say do not flex the hips): same as Natural_Glute_Ham_Raise; glute credit removed.

Evidence:

- [hypertrophy, verified] 9 weeks of Nordic training produced non-uniform hamstring hypertrophy (MRI): BFsh +22%, BFlh +9%, ST +26%, SM +6%. — Pai S A, Andrews MH, Gurchiek RD, et al. Hamstring muscle architecture and microstructure changes following Nordic hamstring exercise training and detraining. J Sport Health Sci. 2025;14:101070. PMID 40578759.
- [hypertrophy, verified] 10 weeks of Nordic training increased semitendinosus volume as much as hip extension training but produced less BFlh hypertrophy than hip extension. — Bourne MN, Duhig SJ, Timmins RG, et al. Impact of the Nordic hamstring and hip extension exercises on hamstring architecture and morphology: implications for injury prevention. Br J Sports Med. 2017;51(5):469-477. PMID 27660368.
- [emg, verified] fMRI: during the Nordic, semitendinosus T2 increase was 16-20% greater than BFlh, BFsh and SM; the Nordic preferentially activates ST. — Bourne MN, Opar DA, Williams MD, Al Najjar A, Shield AJ. Muscle activation patterns in the Nordic hamstring exercise: Impact of prior strain injury. Scand J Med Sci Sports. 2016;26(6):666-674. PMID 26059634.
- [emg, verified] High-density EMG: in the Nordic, ST activity was substantially higher than BFlh (d=1.06) with the distal BFlh region most active. — Hegyi A, Péter A, Finni T, Cronin NJ. Region-dependent hamstrings activity in Nordic hamstring exercise and stiff-leg deadlift defined with high-density electromyography. Scand J Med Sci Sports. 2018;28(3):992-1000. PMID 29143379.
- [emg, verified] Eccentric and concentric Nordic and bent-knee bridge gave the lowest BF:medial-hamstring EMG ratios of ten exercises, i.e. knee-flexion-dominant tasks bias the medial hamstrings. — Bourne MN, Williams MD, Opar DA, Al Najjar A, Kerr GK, Shield AJ. Impact of exercise selection on hamstring muscle activation. Br J Sports Med. 2017;51(13):1021-1028. PMID 27467123.

Notes: Erectors 0.5 removed from Natural_Glute_Ham_Raise (spine is held neutral isometrically). Distribution harmonised across the three to .6/.4: the Nordic literature shows the short head hypertrophies strongly (22%) but ST (a hip_ext-region muscle) grows at least as much, so the split matches the seated-curl anchor rather than the current .5/.5 or .8/.2 guesses.

### Hip thrust and glute bridge

`hip_thrust_bridge` · 7 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Supine/upper-back-supported hip extension with the knees bent ~90 degrees: glute max produces the hip moment with peak load at full extension; hamstrings assist as hip extensors at a shortened knee angle; quads and erectors hold position.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| glutes | 1 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} | Hip thrust training produces gluteus hypertrophy equal to back squat training (Plotkin 2023); very high upper and lower glute max EMG (Contreras 2015; Neto 2020). Kept as current. |
| hamstrings | 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} | Biceps femoris mean EMG ~41% MVIC in the barbell hip thrust, higher than squat (Contreras 2015); knee is fixed so the short head is not loaded. Kept. |

Exercises: Barbell Glute Bridge, Barbell Hip Thrust, Butt Lift (Bridge), Hip Lift with Band, Kneeling Squat, Physioball Hip Bridge, Single Leg Glute Bridge.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Physioball Hip Bridge**: glutes 1 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; hamstrings 0.75 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} — Feet on a ball lengthen the lever and keep the knees closer to extension, so the hamstrings do more of the hip extension than in a bent-knee bridge (Bourne 2017 bridge ratios; anatomical).
- **Single Leg Glute Bridge**: glutes 1 {glute_max 0.8, glute_med 0.2}; hamstrings 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} — Unilateral: the stance-side glute med must prevent pelvic drop, shifting emphasis toward glute_med (single-limb tasks recruit glute med ~60% MVIC, Distefano 2009).
- **Kneeling Squat**: glutes 1 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; hamstrings 0.25 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} — Kneeling with a bar on the back the knee is fully flexed and fixed, so the movement is pure hip extension by glute max; hamstrings are maximally shortened at the knee (minor credit). Abs 0.5 and erectors 0.5 removed (isometric trunk hold).

Evidence:

- [hypertrophy, verified] Nine weeks of set-equated hip thrust vs back squat training in untrained adults produced similar gluteus maximus mCSA increases (upper, mid, lower) and similar glute med/min growth; hip thrust sEMG for glutes was higher. — Plotkin DL, Rodas MA, Vigotsky AD, et al. Hip thrust and back squat training elicit similar gluteus muscle hypertrophy and transfer similarly to the deadlift. Front Physiol. 2023;14:1279170. PMID 37877099.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Meta-analysis of 12 studies: hip-extension resistance training has a moderate effect on gluteus maximus hypertrophy (SMD 0.71), with hip thrust and squat variants both effective. — Krause Neto W, et al. The impact of resistance training on gluteus maximus hypertrophy: a systematic review and meta-analysis. Front Physiol. 2025;16:1542334. PMID 40276368.
- [emg, verified] Barbell hip thrust elicited greater mean upper (69.5 vs 29.4%) and lower (86.8 vs 45.4%) glute max and biceps femoris (40.8 vs 14.9%) EMG than the back squat at 10RM; vastus lateralis did not differ. — Contreras B, Vigotsky AD, Schoenfeld BJ, Beardsley C, Cronin J. A Comparison of Gluteus Maximus, Biceps Femoris, and Vastus Lateralis Electromyographic Activity in the Back Squat and Barbell Hip Thrust Exercises. J Appl Biomech. 2015;31(6):452-458. PMID 26214739.
- [hypertrophy, verified] In well-trained women, 12 weeks of back squat produced greater glute max thickness gains (9.4%) than hip thrust (3.7%); a conflicting result to Plotkin 2023. — Barbalho M, Coswig V, Souza D, Serrão JC, Hebling Campos M, Gentil P. Back Squat vs. Hip Thrust Resistance-training Programs in Well-trained Women. Int J Sports Med. 2020;41(5):306-310. PMID 31975359.
- [emg, verified] Barbell hip thrust glute max activity at 1RM exceeded the back squat and matched the RDL; hip thrust glute activation at 60 kg already equalled MVIC. — Delgado J, Drinkwater EJ, Banyard HG, Haff GG, Nosaka K. Comparison Between Back Squat, Romanian Deadlift, and Barbell Hip Thrust for Leg and Hip Muscle Activities During Hip Extension. J Strength Cond Res. 2019;33(10):2595-2601. PMID 31356511.
- [emg, verified] Bent-knee bridge had among the lowest BF:medial-hamstring EMG ratios, i.e. bridge-pattern hamstring work is medial-biased and modest. — Bourne MN, Williams MD, Opar DA, Al Najjar A, Kerr GK, Shield AJ. Impact of exercise selection on hamstring muscle activation. Br J Sports Med. 2017;51(13):1021-1028. PMID 27467123.

Notes: Calves 0.5 removed from Barbell_Hip_Thrust, Barbell_Glute_Bridge and Hip_Lift_with_Band: the heel-driven position produces no plantarflexion range (consistent with the squat anchor). Quads and erectors (position holders) are not credited under the stabiliser rule; Plotkin 2023 found squat-favoured leg growth, not hip-thrust quad growth. Adductor magnus is a large contributor to hip-thrust hip extension but sits outside the visible 'adductors' group, so it is not credited (see open question).

### Hip extension isolation (kickbacks, band/cable hip extension, prone leg raises)

`hip_extension_kickback` · 5 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Single-joint hip extension of one or both legs with the trunk fixed (quadruped, standing, prone). Glute max is the mover; hamstring contribution depends on knee angle (straight leg = biarticular hamstrings load, bent knee = glute isolation).

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| glutes | 1 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} | Isolated hip extension; hip extension exercises reach very high glute max EMG (Neto 2020; Macadam 2015). Kept as current. |
| hamstrings | 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} | Hip extension specifically targets biceps femoris over ST at 75-87% MVC (Zebis 2013) and the conic-pulley hip extension raised T2 in proximal BFlh and ST (Mendez-Villanueva 2016); secondary because the glute is the intended mover. Kept. |

Exercises: Downward Facing Balance, Flutter Kicks, Glute Kickback, Hip Extension with Bands, One-Legged Cable Kickback.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Glute Kickback**: glutes 1 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; hamstrings 0.25 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} — Quadruped with the knee held at 90 degrees: hamstrings are shortened at the knee and contribute little; glute max isolation (anatomical).
- **Hip Extension with Bands**: glutes 1 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; hamstrings 0.75 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} — Standing straight-knee band hip extension is the 'hip extension' condition in which BF reached 75-87% MVC (Zebis 2013) and the pattern that grew BFlh/SM (Carmichael 2022); strong-synergist hamstring credit.
- **Flutter Kicks**: glutes 1 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; hamstrings 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1}; erectors 0.25 — Prone on a bench with hips at the edge, alternating straight-leg raises: bodyweight hip extension with the lumbar extensors resisting the leg load (minor credit, cf. prone back extension hip-extensor/erector co-activation, Plamondon 2004).
- **Downward Facing Balance**: glutes 1 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; erectors 0.5; hamstrings 0.25 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} — Prone over a ball, legs and arms extended: a 'superman on a ball' whose only external load is the legs, so glute max and lumbar erectors hold hip and spine extension; low absolute stimulus. Abs 0.5 removed (no trunk flexion load).

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Hip extension (with knee near extension) specifically targeted biceps femoris over semitendinosus at very high normalised EMG (75-87% MVC). — Zebis MK, Skotte J, Andersen CH, et al. Kettlebell swing targets semitendinosus and supine leg curl targets biceps femoris: an EMG study with rehabilitation implications. Br J Sports Med. 2013;47(18):1192-1198. PMID 22736206.
- [emg, verified] Cable/conic-pulley standing hip extension raised T2 only in proximal and middle regions of BFlh and ST, i.e. moderate biarticular hamstring loading proximally. — Mendez-Villanueva A, Suarez-Arrones L, Rodas G, et al. MRI-Based Regional Muscle Use during Hamstring Strengthening Exercises in Elite Soccer Players. PLoS One. 2016;11(9):e0161356. PMID 27583444.
- [emg, verified] Systematic review of glute max activation in loaded hip-extension exercises; many single- and multi-joint hip extension variants exceed 60% MVIC. — Neto WK, Soares EG, Vieira TL, et al. Gluteus Maximus Activation during Common Strength and Hypertrophy Exercises: A Systematic Review. J Sports Sci Med. 2020;19(1):195-203. PMID 32132843.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Eccentric hip extension training (6 weeks) increased BFlh (+13%) and semimembranosus (+12.5%) volume, showing straight-leg hip extension is a hypertrophic stimulus for the biarticular hamstrings. — Carmichael DS, Hickey JT, Tofari PJ, Bourne MN, Ward MR, Timmins RG. Effect of an Isometric or Eccentric Hip Extension Exercise Intervention on Hamstring Strength, Architecture, and Morphology. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 2022;54(12):2196-2207. PMID 35941515.
- [anatomical, likely] Flexing the knee shortens the biarticular hamstrings and reduces their capacity to contribute to hip extension, making bent-knee kickbacks more glute-isolated. — Length-tension / biarticular muscle reasoning (standard functional anatomy).

Notes: One-Legged_Cable_Kickback (straight/slightly bent leg, cable at ankle) keeps the family mapping. There is no serratus/pec-minor-style proxy issue here; TFL is not modelled.

### Hip abduction (machine, band walks)

`hip_abduction` · 2 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Frontal-plane hip abduction against a machine pad or band, standing or seated. Gluteus medius/minimus and the upper glute max fibres are the movers; TFL assists (not modelled).

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| glutes | 1 {glute_max 0.3, glute_med 0.7} | Abduction is the primary action of glute med; standing band abduction and side-lying abduction reach 81-103% MVIC glute med (Macadam 2015; Distefano 2009). Upper glute max fibres abduct, and in the seated machine (hip flexed 90 degrees) the posterior glute max fibres also abduct, so glute_max keeps a real share. Changed from current .8/.2 for the machine. |

Exercises: Monster Walk, Thigh Abductor.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Monster Walk**: glutes 1 {glute_max 0.2, glute_med 0.8} — Banded lateral/diagonal walking in a partial squat: glute med dominant with low glute max activation (lateral band walk glute max 27% MVIC, Distefano 2009). Matches current.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Systematic review (23 studies): highest glute med activity in side bridge with abduction, standing hip abduction with elastic resistance at the ankle, and side-lying abduction (81-103% MVIC). — Macadam P, Cronin J, Contreras B. An examination of the gluteal muscle activity associated with dynamic hip abduction and hip external rotation exercise: a systematic review. Int J Sports Phys Ther. 2015;10(5):573-591. PMID 26491608.
- [emg, verified] Side-lying hip abduction produced the greatest glute med activation (81% MVIC); lateral band walk produced low glute max activation (27% MVIC). — Distefano LJ, Blackburn JT, Marshall SW, Padua DA. Gluteal muscle activation during common therapeutic exercises. J Orthop Sports Phys Ther. 2009;39(7):532-540. PMID 19574661.
- [emg, verified] Dynamic partner-resisted hip abduction evoked 88% nEMG in gluteus medius, high enough to count as strength training. — Krommes K, Bandholm T, Jakobsen MD, et al. Dynamic hip adduction, abduction and abdominal exercises from the Holmich groin-injury prevention program are intense enough to be considered strengthening exercises - a cross-sectional study. Int J Sports Phys Ther. 2017;12(3):371-380. PMID 28593090.
- [emg, verified] Hip rotation alters the glute med / TFL / glute max share during side-lying abduction; medial rotation increases glute med relative to glute max and TFL. — Lee JH, Cynn HS, Kwon OY, et al. Different hip rotations influence hip abductor muscles activity during isometric side-lying hip abduction in subjects with gluteus medius weakness. J Electromyogr Kinesiol. 2014;24(2):318-324. PMID 24560168.

Notes: Current Thigh_Abductor mapping (.8 max / .2 med) had the emphasis backwards; abduction machines are the user's main glute-med exercise and should colour that region. No hypertrophy study isolates the abduction machine; Plotkin 2023 shows glute med/min do grow with heavy hip work generally.

### Hip adduction (machine, band)

`hip_adduction` · 2 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Frontal-plane hip adduction against a pad or band: adductor longus/brevis/magnus and gracilis are the movers; nothing else is meaningfully loaded.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| adductors | 1 | Adduction with an elastic band is a dynamic high-intensity adductor longus exercise (up to ~108% nEMG, Serner 2014); gluteal and abdominal activity during adduction exercises is low (5-48%). Kept; glutes 0.5 and hamstrings 0.5 removed from Thigh_Adductor. |

Exercises: Band Hip Adductions, Thigh Adductor.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Across eight adduction exercises adductor longus peak nEMG ranged 14-108%; hip adduction with an elastic band and the Copenhagen adduction were dynamic high-intensity exercises; gluteal and abdominal activation was low (5-48% nEMG). — Serner A, Jakobsen MD, Andersen LL, Hölmich P, Sundstrup E, Thorborg K. EMG evaluation of hip adduction exercises for soccer players: implications for exercise selection in prevention and treatment of groin injuries. Br J Sports Med. 2014;48(14):1108-1114. PMID 23511698.
- [emg, verified] Lying leg lift (side-lying adduction) is tier 2 for adductor brevis and longus force; Copenhagen variants tier 1; hip-extension-oriented lifts load adductor magnus instead. — Collings TJ, et al. Hip Adductor Muscle Forces during Strength Training and Rehabilitation Exercises. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 2026 (epub Apr 3). PMID 41931009.
- [emg, verified] Isometric ball-squeeze adduction reached 84-118% nEMG in adductor longus and dynamic partner adduction 87%, i.e. adduction tasks load the adductors near maximum. — Krommes K, Bandholm T, Jakobsen MD, et al. Dynamic hip adduction, abduction and abdominal exercises from the Holmich groin-injury prevention program are intense enough to be considered strengthening exercises - a cross-sectional study. Int J Sports Phys Ther. 2017;12(3):371-380. PMID 28593090.

Notes: Seated adduction machines with the hip flexed bias adductor longus/brevis/gracilis (the visible inner thigh) over the posterior adductor magnus, which is fine for a single-region group.

`step_up_lunge_misfiled` · 2 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Unilateral knee-and-hip extension of the lead leg (step-up, forward lunge). Quadriceps and glute max are the movers, glute med stabilises the pelvis, hamstrings contribute little. These belong with the squat/lunge batch; mapped here so the batch is complete.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| quads | 1 {quads_rf 0.15, quads_vasti 0.85} | Lead-leg knee extension under bodyweight/load; standard for lunge-pattern movements. Changed: current lunge mapping had quads at 0.5 and hamstrings at 1.0, which the EMG does not support. |
| glutes | 0.75 {glute_max 0.75, glute_med 0.25} | Lunges are very-high (>60% MVIC) glute max exercises (Neto 2020) and unilateral stance loads glute med; strong synergist. Distribution shifted toward glute_med for the single-leg stance. |
| hamstrings | 0.25 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} | Lunge concentric phase has a high BF:MH ratio but low absolute hamstring EMG relative to hinge exercises (Bourne 2017); minor credit. |
| adductors | 0.25 | Step-up is tier 1 for adductor magnus (ischiocondylar) force (Collings 2026); minor visible-group credit consistent with the deadlift family. |

Exercises: Lunge Pass Through, Step-up with Knee Raise.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Step-up with Knee Raise**: quads 1 {quads_rf 0.15, quads_vasti 0.85}; glutes 1 {glute_max 0.7, glute_med 0.3}; hamstrings 0.25 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1}; adductors 0.25 — Step-ups show the highest glute max activation in the literature (Neto 2020; Macadam 2015) with strong glute med demand in single-leg stance (Simenz 2012), so glutes join quads as a primary mover; current mapping had quads at only 0.5.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Step-up and its variations produce the highest gluteus maximus activation of all reviewed exercises (>60% MVIC), followed by deadlifts, hip thrusts, lunges and squats. — Neto WK, Soares EG, Vieira TL, et al. Gluteus Maximus Activation during Common Strength and Hypertrophy Exercises: A Systematic Review. J Sports Sci Med. 2020;19(1):195-203. PMID 32132843.
- [emg, verified] At 6RM the standard step-up elicited the greatest glute max, biceps femoris and semitendinosus activation of four step-up variants and the greatest eccentric glute med activation; crossover step-up the greatest concentric glute med. — Simenz CJ, Garceau LR, Lutsch BN, Suchomel TJ, Ebben WP. Electromyographical analysis of lower extremity muscle activation during variations of the loaded step-up exercise. J Strength Cond Res. 2012;26(12):3398-3405. PMID 22237139.
- [emg, verified] Lateral step-up, crossover step-up and rotational single-leg squat produced the highest glute max activity (79-113% MVIC) across 23 studies. — Macadam P, Cronin J, Contreras B. An examination of the gluteal muscle activity associated with dynamic hip abduction and hip external rotation exercise: a systematic review. Int J Sports Phys Ther. 2015;10(5):573-591. PMID 26491608.
- [emg, verified] Step-up was tier 1 for adductor magnus (ischiocondylar) force and tier 2 for gracilis in an EMG-driven model. — Collings TJ, et al. Hip Adductor Muscle Forces during Strength Training and Rehabilitation Exercises. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 2026 (epub Apr 3). PMID 41931009.
- [emg, verified] Concentrically the lunge had one of the highest BF:medial-hamstring ratios of ten exercises, but hip-extension exercises, not lunges, are the ones shown to grow the hamstrings. — Bourne MN, Williams MD, Opar DA, Al Najjar A, Kerr GK, Shield AJ. Impact of exercise selection on hamstring muscle activation. Br J Sports Med. 2017;51(13):1021-1028. PMID 27467123.

Notes: Both exercises should be reconciled with whatever the squat/lunge batch decides for lunges and step-ups; the weights here are chosen to match the squat anchor's quads-primary / glutes-0.75 structure. Hip flexors for the knee-raise are not credited (unloaded bodyweight knee lift).

## quads

### Bilateral back / hands-at-side squat

`back_squat` · 17 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Bilateral squat with the load on the upper back, on the shoulders (Smith), in the hands at the sides (dumbbell, Jefferson) or bodyweight. Simultaneous knee extension (vasti, RF weakly), hip extension (glute max, adductor magnus), isometric trunk extension under a compressive load (erectors). Depth, stance width and bar position shift the knee/hip share but not the muscle list.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| quads | 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9} | Anchor row. Vasti grow 4-7% from squat training; RF volume does not change (Kubo 2019, Bloomquist 2013, Zabaleta-Korta 2021), and RF EMG is well below vasti in a parallel squat (Ema 2017). |
| glutes | 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} | Anchor row. Glute max volume +6.7% after full-squat training, similar to hip thrust; glute med+min showed little to no growth (Kubo 2019, Plotkin 2023). |
| adductors | 0.5 | Anchor row. Adductor volume +6.2% with full squats and grew more with squats than with hip thrusts (Kubo 2019, Plotkin 2023). |
| erectors | 0.5 | Anchor row. Erector spinae is the most active trunk muscle in a loaded back squat (Aspe & Swinton 2014); kept at half credit per brief. |

Exercises: Barbell Full Squat, Barbell Squat, Bodyweight Squat, Chair Squat, Dumbbell Squat, Jefferson Squats, Narrow Stance Squats, Plie Dumbbell Squat, Reverse Band Power Squat, Smith Machine Squat, Speed Squats, Squat with Bands, Squat with Chains, Squat with Plate Movers, Squats - With Bands, Weighted Squat, Wide Stance Barbell Squat.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Bodyweight Squat**: quads 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; glutes 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; adductors 0.5 — No external load on the spine; erector demand is trivial. Current mapping already omits erectors and is kept.
- **Chair Squat**: quads 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; glutes 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; adductors 0.5 — Sit-to-stand style squat with no bar on the back; keep current mapping (no erector credit).
- **Dumbbell Squat**: quads 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; glutes 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; adductors 0.5; erectors 0.5; forearms 0.25 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2} — Load is carried in the hands, so grip (finger flexors) is loaded for the whole set; minor credit.
- **Narrow Stance Squats**: quads 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; glutes 0.5 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; adductors 0.25; erectors 0.5 — Narrow stance lowers adductor and glute max activity with no change in quads (McCaw 1999, Paoli 2009, Larsen 2021).
- **Wide Stance Barbell Squat**: quads 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; glutes 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; adductors 0.75; erectors 0.5 — Wide stance raises adductor and glute max EMG and hip moment share (McCaw 1999, Paoli 2009, Larsen 2021); adductors promoted to strong synergist.
- **Plie Dumbbell Squat**: quads 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; glutes 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; adductors 0.75; erectors 0.25 — Very wide (sumo) stance: adductors up as for wide stance. Dumbbell hangs between the legs, so spinal loading is low (erectors 0.25). Abs credit removed: no evidence of meaningful stimulus.
- **Jefferson Squats**: quads 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; glutes 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; adductors 0.5; erectors 0.5; forearms 0.5 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2}; traps_upper 0.25 — Straddle-stance squat holding the bar between the legs: heavy grip load (forearms 0.5) and a deadlift-like arm hang; traps_upper is isometric, so reduced from the current 0.5 to 0.25.

Evidence:

- [hypertrophy, verified] 10 weeks of full or half squat training increased knee extensor volume ~5%, adductor volume 6.2% and glute max volume 6.7% (full squats) while rectus femoris and hamstring volumes did not change. — Kubo K, Ikebukuro T, Yata H. Effects of squat training with different depths on lower limb muscle volumes. Eur J Appl Physiol. 2019;119(9):1933-1942. PMID 31230110.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Squat training grew the quadriceps and adductors more than hip-thrust training; glute max growth was similar; glute med+min and hamstrings showed little to no growth in either group. — Plotkin DL, Rodas MA, Vigotsky AD, et al. Hip thrust and back squat training elicit similar gluteus muscle hypertrophy and transfer similarly to the deadlift. Front Physiol. 2023;14:1279170. PMID 37877099.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Deep-squat training produced greater front-thigh CSA gains (4-7%) than shallow-squat training over 12 weeks. — Bloomquist K, Langberg H, Karlsen S, et al. Effect of range of motion in heavy load squatting on muscle and tendon adaptations. Eur J Appl Physiol. 2013;113(8):2133-2142. PMID 23604798.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Smith-machine squat training grew only the central vastus lateralis region; leg-extension training grew all three rectus femoris regions. — Zabaleta-Korta A, Fernandez-Pena E, Torres-Unda J, et al. The role of exercise selection in regional muscle hypertrophy: a randomized controlled trial. J Sports Sci. 2021;39(20):2298-2304. PMID 34743671.
- [emg, verified] Rectus femoris activation during a parallel squat is significantly lower than vastus lateralis and medialis. — Ema R, Takayama H, Miyamoto N, Akagi R. Effect of prolonged vibration to synergistic and antagonistic muscles on the rectus femoris activation during multi-joint exercises. Eur J Appl Physiol. 2017;117(10):2109-2118. PMID 28849258.
- [emg, verified] Stance width does not change quadriceps EMG; a wide stance raises adductor longus and glute max activity, narrow stance lowers them. — McCaw ST, Melrose DR. Stance width and bar load effects on leg muscle activity during the parallel squat. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 1999;31(3):428-436. PMID 10188748.
- [emg, verified] Across three stance widths only glute max EMG differed, being higher at the widest stance; quads, hamstrings, glute med and adductor major did not differ. — Paoli A, Marcolin G, Petrone N. The effect of stance width on the electromyographical activity of eight superficial thigh muscles during back squat with different bar loads. J Strength Cond Res. 2009;23(1):246-250. PMID 19130646.
- [emg, verified] High-bar narrow-stance squats give higher vastus lateralis and lower glute max EMG with greater knee contribution; low-bar wide stance shifts moment share to the hip. — Larsen S, Kristiansen E, Helms E, van den Tillaar R. Effects of stance width and barbell placement on kinematics, kinetics, and myoelectric activity in back squats. Front Sports Act Living. 2021;3:719013. PMID 34541522.
- [emg, verified] In loaded back squats erector spinae shows the highest posterior-trunk activity; anterior trunk (RA/EO) activity is small. — Aspe RR, Swinton PA. Electromyographic and kinetic comparison of the back squat and overhead squat. J Strength Cond Res. 2014;28(10):2827-2836. PMID 24662228.
- [emg, verified] Review of 18 EMG studies: prime-mover activation rises with load; stance width, hip rotation and front vs back bar position do not materially change which muscles are activated. — Clark DR, Lambert MI, Hunter AM. Muscle activation in the loaded free barbell squat: a brief review. J Strength Cond Res. 2012;26(4):1169-1178. PMID 22373894.

Notes: Barbell_Squat kept exactly as the anchor. Barbell_Full_Squat uses the same row; Kubo 2019 shows full depth grows glutes and adductors more than half depth, but the app does not model depth so no override. Speed squats, band/chain/reverse-band and plate-mover variants change load profile only, not the muscle list. Smith_Machine_Squat kept at family values (Zabaleta-Korta 2021 shows Smith squats grow the vasti). Reverse_Band_Power_Squat: no forearm credit (bar on the back).

### Box squat and squat-to-bench

`box_squat` · 7 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Back squat with the descent stopped on a box or bench. Wide stance and vertical shins in the powerlifting version shift the moment share from knee to hip; squat-to-bench is simply a depth-limited squat.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| quads | 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9} | Knee extension still drives the movement; same RF/vasti split as the squat anchor. |
| glutes | 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} | Box squat is performed with a wide stance and posterior COM shift, giving hip moments at least equal to the traditional squat (Swinton 2012); raised from the current 0.5 to the squat anchor's 0.75. |
| adductors | 0.5 | Wide-stance hip extension loads adductor magnus (McCaw 1999); keep squat anchor value. |
| erectors | 0.5 | Loaded bar on the back; same as squat anchor. |

Exercises: Barbell Squat To A Bench, Box Squat, Box Squat with Bands, Box Squat with Chains, Dumbbell Squat To A Bench, Reverse Band Box Squat, Speed Box Squat.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Dumbbell Squat To A Bench**: quads 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; glutes 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; adductors 0.5; erectors 0.5; forearms 0.25 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2} — Dumbbells held at the sides load grip throughout the set.

Evidence:

- [anatomical, verified] Powerlifting and box squats are performed with a wide stance and vertical shin, shifting the system COM posteriorly and producing the largest hip moments in all three planes; spine and ankle moments are lower than in the traditional squat. — Swinton PA, Lloyd R, Keogh JW, Agouris I, Stewart AD. A biomechanical comparison of the traditional squat, powerlifting squat, and box squat. J Strength Cond Res. 2012;26(7):1805-1816. PMID 22505136.
- [emg, verified] Wide stance raises adductor longus and glute max activity without changing quadriceps EMG. — McCaw ST, Melrose DR. Stance width and bar load effects on leg muscle activity during the parallel squat. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 1999;31(3):428-436. PMID 10188748.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Shallow (60 degree) squat training still grew the front thigh but less than deep squats; depth-limited squats remain a quad exercise. — Bloomquist K, Langberg H, Karlsen S, et al. Effect of range of motion in heavy load squatting on muscle and tendon adaptations. Eur J Appl Physiol. 2013;113(8):2133-2142. PMID 23604798.

Notes: Main change vs current: glutes 0.5 -> 0.75 for all box variants, because the box squat is, if anything, more hip-dominant than the traditional squat. Reverse_Band_Box_Squat loses its current forearm credit (bar on the back, hands only steady it). Speed_Box_Squat gains adductors and erectors to match the family.

### Front-loaded squat (front, goblet, Zercher, kettlebell rack)

`front_loaded_squat` · 6 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Bilateral squat with the load in front of the body (front rack, goblet, Zercher, double kettlebell rack). More upright trunk, slightly more knee-dominant, same prime movers as the back squat. Arms and upper back hold the load isometrically.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| quads | 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9} | Vastus medialis EMG is equal or higher than in back squats (Yavuz 2015, Gullett 2009); RF remains low in any deep squat. |
| glutes | 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} | No glute max EMG difference between front, full and parallel squats (Contreras 2016). |
| adductors | 0.5 | Same deep hip flexion/extension as the back squat; adductors grow from squatting (Kubo 2019). |
| erectors | 0.5 | Erector activity is not lower in front squats (Gullett 2009; iliocostalis higher in McCormick 2023); keep squat anchor value. |

Exercises: Front Barbell Squat, Front Barbell Squat To A Bench, Front Squat (Clean Grip), Front Squats With Two Kettlebells, Goblet Squat, Zercher Squats.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Goblet Squat**: quads 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; glutes 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; adductors 0.5; erectors 0.25 — Load is light relative to a barbell and is held at the chest, so spinal load is modest (erectors 0.25). Current delt_front 0.5 removed: the arms only hold the bell isometrically.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Front and back squats produced the same muscle activation (quads, hamstrings, erector spinae) with lower knee compressive forces in the front squat. — Gullett JC, Tillman MD, Gutierrez GM, Chow JW. A biomechanical comparison of back and front squats in healthy trained individuals. J Strength Cond Res. 2009;23(1):284-292. PMID 19002072.
- [emg, verified] At maximal loads vastus medialis EMG was greater in the front squat while semitendinosus was greater in the back squat; the back squat had more trunk lean. — Yavuz HU, Erdag D, Amca AM, Aritan S. Kinematic and EMG activities during front and back squat variations in maximum loads. J Sports Sci. 2015;33(10):1058-1066. PMID 25630691.
- [emg, verified] No differences in glute max, biceps femoris or vastus lateralis EMG between front, full and parallel squats at 10RM in trained women. — Contreras B, Vigotsky AD, Schoenfeld BJ, Beardsley C, Cronin J. A comparison of gluteus maximus, biceps femoris, and vastus lateralis EMG amplitude in the parallel, full, and front squat variations in resistance-trained females. J Appl Biomech. 2016;32(1):16-22. PMID 26252837.
- [emg, verified] Trunk muscle activity (internal/external oblique, iliocostalis) is higher in front than back squats at matched relative load. — McCormick JB, Drusch AS, Lynch DJ, et al. The effect of volitional preemptive abdominal contraction on biomechanical measures during a front versus back loaded barbell squat. Int J Sports Phys Ther. 2023;18(4):831-844. PMID 37547830.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Squat training does not grow the hamstrings (no change in hamstring volume/CSA). — Kubo K, Ikebukuro T, Yata H. Effects of squat training with different depths on lower limb muscle volumes. Eur J Appl Physiol. 2019;119(9):1933-1942. PMID 31230110.

Notes: Front_Squat_Clean_Grip: current abs 0.5 and hamstrings 0.5 removed. Abdominal EMG rises only a few percent MVIC and is isometric; hamstrings do not hypertrophy from squats and the brief gives squats no hamstring credit. Zercher_Squats: biceps and upper back hold the bar isometrically, no credit per brief; erectors kept at 0.5. Front_Squats_With_Two_Kettlebells: rack hold is isometric for the shoulders, no delt credit.

### Overhead squat and squat jerk

`overhead_squat` · 2 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Squat with the load locked out overhead (kettlebell or barbell). Lower limb pattern is a narrow, upright squat at lighter loads; shoulders and trunk stabilise isometrically. The squat jerk adds a ballistic dip-and-drive with active shoulder/triceps lockout.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| quads | 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9} | Knee extension is the prime mover; overhead squats keep a very upright trunk (knee-dominant). |
| glutes | 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} | Deep hip extension as in any full squat; back squat showed higher lower-body EMG but the pattern is the same (Aspe & Swinton 2014). |
| adductors | 0.5 | Full-depth squat pattern; same as squat anchor. |
| erectors | 0.5 | Erector spinae is the dominant trunk muscle in both back and overhead squats (Aspe & Swinton 2014). |

Exercises: One-Arm Overhead Kettlebell Squats, Squat Jerk.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Squat Jerk**: quads 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; glutes 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; adductors 0.25; erectors 0.5; calves 0.25 {gastrocnemius 0.7, soleus 0.3}; delt_front 0.5; triceps 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7}; traps_upper 0.25 — Ballistic dip-drive with triple extension (calves 0.25, traps shrug 0.25) and an active punch to lockout (delt_front, triceps at half credit as in an overhead press). Hamstrings removed (no squat hamstring credit per brief); adductors reduced because the receiving squat is brief.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Overhead squat showed slightly (2-7%) greater rectus abdominis and external oblique EMG than the back squat; back squat had greater erector spinae and all lower-body EMG and higher peak force. — Aspe RR, Swinton PA. Electromyographic and kinetic comparison of the back squat and overhead squat. J Strength Cond Res. 2014;28(10):2827-2836. PMID 24662228.
- [emg, verified] In a clean-and-jerk pattern the erector spinae is most active in the lowest (deep squat) position while rectus abdominis and obliquus internus show little activation. — Eriksson Crommert M, Ekblom MM, Thorstensson A. Motor control of the trunk during a modified clean and jerk lift. Scand J Med Sci Sports. 2014;24(5):758-763. PMID 23489349.

Notes: One-Arm_Overhead_Kettlebell_Squats: current delt_front 0.5 removed because the overhead arm works isometrically; the brief excludes isometric stabiliser credit. Flagged as an open question since users may expect shoulder colour. Trunk anti-lateral-flexion (obliques) is also isometric and left uncredited.

### Leg press, hack squat and machine squat

`machine_leg_press_hack` · 7 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Back-supported or sled-guided bilateral knee/hip extension: 45-degree and horizontal leg presses, hack squat sled, lying machine squat, and the barbell hack squat (bar behind the legs). Trunk is supported so erectors are unloaded; hip range is shorter than a free squat so glute credit is lower.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| quads | 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9} | Closed-chain pressing recruits the vasti far more than RF (Escamilla 1998); leg press and squat produce comparable quad development (Rossi 2016). |
| glutes | 0.5 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} | Hip extension is present but hip ROM and hip moment are smaller than in a free squat with back support; half credit (current mapping kept). |
| adductors | 0.5 | Adductor magnus acts as a hip extensor in the flexed-hip bottom position; stance-dependent (McCaw 1999, Escamilla 2001). |

Exercises: Barbell Hack Squat, Hack Squat, Leg Press, Lying Machine Squat, Narrow Stance Hack Squats, Narrow Stance Leg Press, Smith Machine Leg Press.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Narrow Stance Leg Press**: quads 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; glutes 0.5 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; adductors 0.25 — Narrow stance reduces adductor involvement (McCaw 1999; Escamilla 2001 wide vs narrow leg press).
- **Narrow Stance Hack Squats**: quads 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; glutes 0.5 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; adductors 0.25 — Narrow stance reduces adductor involvement (McCaw 1999).
- **Barbell Hack Squat**: quads 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; glutes 0.5 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; adductors 0.5; forearms 0.5 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2}; erectors 0.25 — Free-weight version: bar is gripped behind the legs from the floor, so grip is loaded (forearms 0.5, split toward finger flexors) and the trunk carries the bar (erectors 0.25). Current forearm credit kept; erectors added.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Squat produced roughly twice the hamstring activity of the leg press; closed-chain exercises (squat, leg press) produced more vasti and less rectus femoris activity than open-chain knee extension. — Escamilla RF, Fleisig GS, Zheng N, Barrentine SW, Wilk KE, Andrews JR. Biomechanics of the knee during closed kinetic chain and open kinetic chain exercises. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 1998;30(4):556-569. PMID 9565938.
- [emg, verified] Squat generated more quadriceps and hamstring activity than high- or low-foot leg press; wide-stance high-foot leg press had more hamstring activity than narrow stance; foot angle made no difference. — Escamilla RF, Fleisig GS, Zheng N, et al. Effects of technique variations on knee biomechanics during the squat and leg press. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 2001;33(9):1552-1566. PMID 11528346.
- [emg, verified] During the leg press quadriceps EMG was ~39-52% MVIC while hamstring activity was minimal (~12% MVIC). — Wilk KE, Escamilla RF, Fleisig GS, Barrentine SW, Andrews JR, Boyd ML. A comparison of tibiofemoral joint forces and electromyographic activity during open and closed kinetic chain exercises. Am J Sports Med. 1996;24(4):518-527. PMID 8827313.
- [hypertrophy, verified] 10 weeks of squat-only, leg-press-only or combined training produced similar body-composition and lower-body outcomes; transfer was task specific. — Rossi FE, Schoenfeld BJ, Ocetnik S, et al. Strength, body composition, and functional outcomes in the squat versus leg press exercises. J Sports Med Phys Fitness. 2018;58(3):263-270. PMID 27735888.
- [emg, verified] Narrow stance lowers adductor and glute max activity; quads unchanged. — McCaw ST, Melrose DR. Stance width and bar load effects on leg muscle activity during the parallel squat. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 1999;31(3):428-436. PMID 10188748.

Notes: Current family values (quads 1.0, glutes 0.5, adductors 0.5, no erectors) are already right and are kept. Lying_Machine_Squat is a horizontal sled squat with the shoulders under pads; its current glutes 0.75 is lowered to the family 0.5 because the back is supported. Hack/leg-press place the hip in less flexion than a free squat, which lengthens RF slightly, but no data justify moving the RF share off 0.10.

### Leg extension (bilateral and single-leg)

`leg_extension` · 2 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Open-chain seated knee extension against a pad. Isolates the quadriceps; the only knee-dominant exercise where rectus femoris is a strong contributor because the hip is flexed and no hip extension torque suppresses it.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| quads | 1 {quads_rf 0.3, quads_vasti 0.7} | RF grows proportionally more than the vasti with knee-extension training (17% vs 10-11%, Lundberg 2019; Ema 2013) and is the region that separates leg extensions from squats (Zabaleta-Korta 2021). RF is only ~15% of quad volume, so 0.30 already over-weights it to express the emphasis. Current value kept. |

Exercises: Leg Extensions, Single-Leg Leg Extension.

Evidence:

- [hypertrophy, verified] 12 weeks of knee-extension training increased rectus femoris CSA, thickness and pennation relatively more than the vasti. — Ema R, Wakahara T, Miyamoto N, Kanehisa H, Kawakami Y. Inhomogeneous architectural changes of the quadriceps femoris induced by resistance training. Eur J Appl Physiol. 2013;113(11):2691-2703. PMID 23949789.
- [hypertrophy, verified] 8 weeks of knee-extension training (flywheel or weight stack) grew RF 17% versus VL 10-11%, VM 6-8%, VI 5%. — Lundberg TR, Garcia-Gutierrez MT, Mandic M, Lilja M, Fernandez-Gonzalo R. Regional and muscle-specific adaptations in knee extensor hypertrophy using flywheel versus conventional weight-stack resistance exercise. Appl Physiol Nutr Metab. 2019;44(8):827-833. PMID 30620623.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Leg-extension training grew all three RF regions whereas Smith squat training grew only central VL. — Zabaleta-Korta A, Fernandez-Pena E, Torres-Unda J, et al. The role of exercise selection in regional muscle hypertrophy: a randomized controlled trial. J Sports Sci. 2021;39(20):2298-2304. PMID 34743671.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Leg extension with the hip at 40 degrees (more reclined) produced clearly greater RF hypertrophy than at 90 degrees, with no difference for VL. — Larsen S, Sandvik Kristiansen B, Swinton PA, et al. The effects of hip flexion angle on quadriceps femoris muscle hypertrophy in the leg extension exercise. J Sports Sci. 2024;42(24):2400-2408. PMID 39699974.
- [emg, verified] Open-chain knee extension produces more rectus femoris activity than squat or leg press. — Escamilla RF, Fleisig GS, Zheng N, Barrentine SW, Wilk KE, Andrews JR. Biomechanics of the knee during closed kinetic chain and open kinetic chain exercises. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 1998;30(4):556-569. PMID 9565938.

Notes: Current mapping (quads 1.0 {rf .3, vasti .7}) is kept. Single-leg version is identical; unilateral work does not change the muscle list.

### Sissy squat

`sissy_squat` · 1 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Knee flexion/extension with the hips held extended and the torso leaning back, heels raised. Knee-only movement that puts rectus femoris at long length (hip extended + knee flexed) with essentially no hip extension torque.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| quads | 1 {quads_rf 0.35, quads_vasti 0.65} | Quad isolation like a leg extension, but with the hip extended, which lengthens RF further than any seated leg extension; Larsen 2024 shows less hip flexion produces more RF growth, so RF share is set slightly above the leg-extension row. |

Exercises: Weighted Sissy Squat.

Evidence:

- [hypertrophy, verified] Reducing hip flexion during knee extension (40 vs 90 degrees) markedly increases RF hypertrophy with no change in VL; sissy squats take this to ~0 degrees of hip flexion. — Larsen S, Sandvik Kristiansen B, Swinton PA, et al. The effects of hip flexion angle on quadriceps femoris muscle hypertrophy in the leg extension exercise. J Sports Sci. 2024;42(24):2400-2408. PMID 39699974.
- [anatomical, likely] RF is biarticular (hip flexor + knee extensor); with the hip held extended and the knee flexed it is at maximal length under load, while glute max and adductors produce no shortening work because the hip does not move. — Anatomical reasoning (moment arms and length-tension); no sissy-squat-specific study indexed in PubMed as of 2026-09 (query 'sissy squat' returned 0 records).
- [consensus, likely] Practitioners classify the sissy squat as a quadriceps isolation movement with negligible glute contribution. — Practitioner consensus (strength-coaching texts and exercise libraries).

Notes: Current glutes 0.75 and adductors 0.5 removed: the hip stays extended through the rep, so glute max and adductor magnus do no dynamic work. Only quads are credited.

### Jump squat (bodyweight and loaded)

`jump_squat` · 2 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Ballistic countermovement squat into a vertical jump. Shallow-to-parallel squat pattern plus explosive triple extension (hip, knee, ankle), so the plantar flexors do concentric work that ordinary squats do not require.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| quads | 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9} | Knee extension drives take-off; RF stays minor as in all squats. |
| glutes | 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} | Hip extension is a major contributor to jump power; glute max force rises with external load in squats (Kipp 2022). Raised from current 0.5 to the squat anchor value. |
| calves | 0.5 {gastrocnemius 0.7, soleus 0.3} | Take-off and landing require concentric/eccentric plantar flexion under body-plus-load; gastrocnemius and soleus forces scale with load in squat-type movements (Kipp 2022). Gastrocnemius-biased because the knee is near extension at toe-off. |
| adductors | 0.25 | Shallower squat depth than a full squat, so adductor magnus works over a shorter range; minor credit. |

Exercises: Freehand Jump Squat, Weighted Jump Squat.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Weighted Jump Squat**: quads 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; glutes 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; calves 0.5 {gastrocnemius 0.7, soleus 0.3}; adductors 0.25; erectors 0.25 — Barbell on the back adds spinal load and a landing impulse; current erectors 0.5 halved because loads are light (typically <= 30% 1RM).

Evidence:

- [anatomical, verified] Soleus, gastrocnemius medialis/lateralis, glute max and vasti forces all increase with external load during squat-type exercises (musculoskeletal modelling). — Kipp K, Kim H, Wolf WI. Muscle forces during the squat, split squat, and step-up across a range of external loads in college-aged men. J Strength Cond Res. 2022;36(2):314-323. PMID 32569122.
- [emg, verified] Lateral gastrocnemius is one of the lower-limb muscles whose EMG rises after squat conditioning during countermovement and squat jumps, consistent with plantar-flexor involvement in the jump. — Gao Z. Load-specific post-activation performance enhancement from back squats in male collegiate basketball players. Front Physiol. 2026;17:1847856. PMID 42620624.
- [anatomical, likely] Jump squats are a ballistic triple-extension movement; the ankle plantar flexors perform positive work at take-off that a standing squat does not require. — Anatomical / biomechanical reasoning; no longitudinal hypertrophy study of jump squats found.

Notes: Jump squats are trained for power at low loads and high velocity; the credit table describes which muscles are loaded, while the engine's set factors (reps, RIR) should account for the lower hypertrophic stimulus per set. Calves added because plantar flexion is dynamic here, unlike in a regular squat.

### Trap-bar, machine, cable and suitcase deadlift

`quad_dominant_deadlift` · 4 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Floor pulls where the handles sit beside the body and the trunk stays more upright than a straight-bar deadlift: knee moment is higher and hip/lumbar moments lower, so the quads are a prime mover alongside the hip extensors. Grip and upper traps carry the load.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| quads | 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9} | Hex bar raises knee moment and vastus lateralis EMG above the straight-bar deadlift (Swinton 2011, Camara 2016). |
| glutes | 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} | Hex-bar deadlift is a very-high glute max activation exercise (Neto 2020); hip extension is a primary joint action, kept at the RDL anchor's 0.75. |
| hamstrings | 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} | Biceps femoris EMG is 28-39% lower with the hex bar than a straight bar (Andersen 2018, Camara 2016); hip-extension hamstrings only, half credit versus the RDL anchor's 1.0. |
| erectors | 0.75 | Lumbar moment is lower than a straight-bar pull but still substantial; erector EMG did not differ between bars (Andersen 2018). Between the squat anchor (0.5) and a conventional deadlift. |
| forearms | 0.5 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2} | Heavy grip-limited hold; finger flexors dominate. |
| traps_upper | 0.25 | Upper traps resist scapular depression isometrically under heavy load; minor credit to keep the expected colour (consensus), reduced from the current 0.5. |
| adductors | 0.25 | Adductor magnus assists hip extension from the flexed start position; less range than a squat. |

Exercises: Cable Deadlifts, Leverage Deadlift, One-Arm Side Deadlift, Trap Bar Deadlift.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Cable Deadlifts**: quads 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; glutes 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; hamstrings 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1}; erectors 0.5; forearms 0.25 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2}; adductors 0.25 — Low-pulley loads are much lighter than a bar, so grip and trap demand are minor; erectors at the squat level. No traps_upper credit.
- **One-Arm Side Deadlift**: quads 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; glutes 0.75 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1}; hamstrings 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1}; erectors 0.75; forearms 0.5 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2}; traps_upper 0.25; obliques 0.25 — Suitcase-style pull with the bar at one side: obliques resist lateral flexion (isometric, so only 0.25, flagged in open questions). Current abs 0.5 and calves 0.5 removed: no dynamic work for either.

Evidence:

- [anatomical, verified] Hexagonal-bar deadlift lowered peak lumbar, hip and ankle moments and increased peak knee moment versus the straight bar; lifters also pulled a heavier 1RM. — Swinton PA, Stewart A, Agouris I, Keogh JW, Lloyd R. A biomechanical analysis of straight and hexagonal barbell deadlifts using submaximal loads. J Strength Cond Res. 2011;25(7):2000-2009. PMID 21659894.
- [emg, verified] Hex-bar deadlift produced greater vastus lateralis EMG (concentric and eccentric) while the straight bar produced greater biceps femoris (concentric) and erector spinae (eccentric) EMG. — Camara KD, Coburn JW, Dunnick DD, Brown LE, Galpin AJ, Costa PB. An examination of muscle activation and power characteristics while performing the deadlift exercise with straight and hexagonal barbells. J Strength Cond Res. 2016;30(5):1183-1188. PMID 26840440.
- [emg, verified] Barbell deadlift activated biceps femoris 28% more than the hex-bar deadlift; erector spinae activation did not differ between hex bar, barbell deadlift and hip thrust. — Andersen V, Fimland MS, Mo DA, et al. Electromyographic comparison of barbell deadlift, hex bar deadlift, and hip thrust exercises: a cross-over study. J Strength Cond Res. 2018;32(3):587-593. PMID 28151780.
- [emg, verified] Hex-bar deadlift is classed as a very-high (>60% MVIC) glute max activation exercise. — Neto WK, Soares EG, Vieira TL, et al. Gluteus maximus activation during common strength and hypertrophy exercises: a systematic review. J Sports Sci Med. 2020;19(1):195-203. PMID 32132843.

Notes: These four are hinge-pattern exercises that were placed in the quads batch; the mapping deliberately keeps quads at 1.0 because the evidence (higher knee moment and VL EMG than a straight-bar pull) supports it, while hamstrings are held at half the RDL anchor. Leverage_Deadlift (plate-loaded machine with side handles) uses the family row: same geometry as the trap bar; forearms and erectors added versus current.

### Lunges and split squats (incl. Bulgarian)

`lunge_split_squat` · 11 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Unilateral knee/hip extension of the front leg in a split stance: forward, walking, reverse and deficit lunges, static split squats and rear-foot-elevated (Bulgarian) split squats. Hip-dominant relative to a back squat, with frontal-plane glute med demand and a small but real hamstring contribution from the forward trunk lean.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| quads | 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9} | Vasti show the highest EMG of all muscles in lunges (Muyor 2020, Khaiyat 2018); RF remains minor because the front hip is flexed. |
| glutes | 0.75 {glute_max 0.7, glute_med 0.3} | Split squat and lunges are very-high glute max exercises (Neto 2020); glute max and glute med forces rise more steeply with load than in a back squat (Kipp 2022). Glute med share raised because single-leg stance loads the abductors (Muyor 2020). |
| hamstrings | 0.25 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} | Hamstring forces are highest in the front leg of a split squat (Jaeggi 2024), BF long head force scales more with load than in a squat (Kipp 2022), and walking-lunge training improved hamstring strength (Jonhagen 2009). Minor credit only; no direct hypertrophy data. |
| adductors | 0.25 | Hip adductor EMG rises with a longer step (Escamilla 2025); less range than a bilateral deep squat, so kept at the current 0.25. |

Exercises: Barbell Lunge, Barbell Walking Lunge, Bodyweight Walking Lunge, Dumbbell Lunges, Dumbbell Rear Lunge, Elevated Back Lunge, Lunge Sprint, One Leg Barbell Squat, Smith Single-Leg Split Squat, Split Squat with Dumbbells, Suspended Split Squat.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Split squat, in-line lunge and traditional lunge are classed as very-high (>60% MVIC) glute max activation exercises. — Neto WK, Soares EG, Vieira TL, et al. Gluteus maximus activation during common strength and hypertrophy exercises: a systematic review. J Sports Sci Med. 2020;19(1):195-203. PMID 32132843.
- [anatomical, verified] Load-dependent increases in glute max, vasti and biceps femoris long head forces were more pronounced in the split squat and step-up than in the back squat. — Kipp K, Kim H, Wolf WI. Muscle forces during the squat, split squat, and step-up across a range of external loads in college-aged men. J Strength Cond Res. 2022;36(2):314-323. PMID 32569122.
- [anatomical, verified] Bulgarian split squat and back squat are both hip-dominant; knee moment impulse and knee displacement are lower in the Bulgarian split squat. — Mackey ER, Riemann BL. Biomechanical differences between the Bulgarian split-squat and back squat. Int J Exerc Sci. 2021;14(1):533-543. PMID 34055144.
- [anatomical, verified] In musculoskeletal models the gluteal muscles were most active in the front leg of the split squat and the hamstrings (esp. semimembranosus) generated their highest forces there. — Jaeggi JS, Achermann B, Lorenzetti SR. Female lower body muscle forces: a musculoskeletal modeling comparison of back squats, split squats and good mornings. J Funct Morphol Kinesiol. 2024;9(2):68. PMID 38651426.
- [emg, verified] Quadriceps, hamstrings, gastrocnemius, hip adductor and glute max EMG were all greater in long-step than short-step forward lunges. — Escamilla RF, Thompson IS, Asuncion R, et al. Effects of step length and stride variation during forward lunges on lower-extremity muscle activity. J Funct Morphol Kinesiol. 2025;10(1):42. PMID 39982282.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Six weeks of walking forward lunges improved hamstring strength in soccer players. — Jonhagen S, Ackermann P, Saartok T. Forward lunge: a training study of eccentric exercises of the lower limbs. J Strength Cond Res. 2009;23(3):972-978. PMID 19387378.
- [emg, verified] In forward lunge, monopodal squat and lateral step-up the vasti showed the highest EMG followed by glute med and glute max. — Muyor JM, Martin-Fuentes I, Rodriguez-Ridao D, Antequera-Vique JA. Electromyographic activity in the gluteus medius, gluteus maximus, biceps femoris, vastus lateralis, vastus medialis and rectus femoris during the Monopodal Squat, Forward Lunge and Lateral Step-Up exercises. PLoS One. 2020;15(4):e0230841. PMID 32236133.
- [emg, verified] Trunk flexion during the Bulgarian split squat increases glute max, biceps femoris and RF activation; suspension-based rear foot increases erector spinae activation. — Aygun-Polat E, Guzel NA, Guruhan S, Polat Y, Karatas N. Targeted muscle activation in Bulgarian split squat variations: effects of trunk position and suspension-based execution. BMC Sports Sci Med Rehabil. 2025;17(1):251. PMID 40867012.

Notes: Only change vs current: hamstrings 0.25 added across the family and the glute med share confirmed at 0.3. One_Leg_Barbell_Squat is the free-exercise-db name for a barbell Bulgarian split squat and belongs here rather than with pistols; its current glute split {.9/.1} and adductors 0.5 are replaced by the family values. Suspended_Split_Squat adductors lowered from 0.5 to the family 0.25. Bar-on-back variants get no erector credit because the unilateral load is roughly half a back-squat load.

### Step-up

`step_up` · 2 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Unilateral concentric-dominant hip and knee extension onto a box. The lead-leg hip extensors do the largest share of work of any squat-pattern exercise, with substantial glute med demand for pelvic control.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| quads | 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9} | Knee extension of the lead leg; vasti dominant. |
| glutes | 1 {glute_max 0.7, glute_med 0.3} | Step-ups and their variations show the highest glute max activation of all reviewed exercises (Neto 2020) and glute max/glute med forces scale more with load than in the squat (Kipp 2022); treated as a co-primary mover. Raised from current 0.75. |
| hamstrings | 0.25 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} | Biceps femoris long head force rises with load more in the step-up than the squat (Kipp 2022); minor credit. |
| adductors | 0.25 | Adductor activation is higher in the step-up than the step-down position (Park 2019); minor credit. |

Exercises: Barbell Step Ups, Dumbbell Step Ups.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Step-up and its variations (lateral, diagonal, cross-over) present the highest glute max activation levels of all exercises reviewed, above deadlifts, hip thrusts, lunges and squats. — Neto WK, Soares EG, Vieira TL, et al. Gluteus maximus activation during common strength and hypertrophy exercises: a systematic review. J Sports Sci Med. 2020;19(1):195-203. PMID 32132843.
- [anatomical, verified] Load-dependent increases in glute max, glute med, vasti and BF long head forces were more pronounced in the step-up and split squat than in the back squat. — Kipp K, Kim H, Wolf WI. Muscle forces during the squat, split squat, and step-up across a range of external loads in college-aged men. J Strength Cond Res. 2022;36(2):314-323. PMID 32569122.
- [emg, verified] Adductor activation relative to glute med, VL and BF is greater in the step-up than the step-down position. — Park SH, Lee YS, Cheon SH. Analysis of the ratios of medial-lateral and proximal-distal muscle activities surrounding the hip joint in the step-up and step-down positions. J Back Musculoskelet Rehabil. 2019;32(4):589-595. PMID 30347585.
- [emg, verified] Step-up tasks are performed with high glute med and glute max activity in healthy women; weakness reduces it. — Glaviano NR, Saliba S. Differences in gluteal and quadriceps muscle activation during weight-bearing exercises between female subjects with and without patellofemoral pain. J Strength Cond Res. 2022;36(1):55-62. PMID 31725556.

Notes: Glutes promoted to 1.0 on tier-2 evidence (EMG systematic review plus modelled forces). No longitudinal hypertrophy study of step-ups exists, so this is listed as an open question. Trailing-leg push-off, if used, reduces lead-leg load; the table assumes a strict step-up.

### Pistol / single-leg squat

`single_leg_squat` · 3 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Bilateral-depth squat on one leg with the free leg held forward (pistol) or a partial single-leg squat to a high box. Very high vasti, glute max and glute med demand for the stance leg; the free-leg hip flexors work isometrically.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| quads | 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9} | Monopodal squat produced higher EMG than lunge and lateral step-up in VL and VM (Muyor 2020); RF minor with the hip deeply flexed. |
| glutes | 0.75 {glute_max 0.65, glute_med 0.35} | Modified single-leg squat is a very-high glute max exercise (Neto 2020); glute med EMG is high in the monopodal squat (Muyor 2020) and the free-leg-forward variant carries the largest hip extensor moment (Khuu 2016). |
| adductors | 0.25 | Deep unilateral hip flexion loads adductor magnus as a hip extensor, but over one leg with bodyweight-scale loads; minor credit. |

Exercises: Kettlebell Pistol Squat, Single-Leg High Box Squat, Smith Machine Pistol Squat.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Single-Leg High Box Squat**: quads 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9}; glutes 0.5 {glute_max 0.65, glute_med 0.35} — Partial range to a high box: hip flexion is limited, so glute and adductor work is reduced (glutes 0.5, no adductors). Matches the current mapping apart from the glute split.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] The monopodal (single-leg) squat produced higher EMG than the forward lunge and lateral step-up in glute med, glute max, BF, VL and VM. — Muyor JM, Martin-Fuentes I, Rodriguez-Ridao D, Antequera-Vique JA. Electromyographic activity in the gluteus medius, gluteus maximus, biceps femoris, vastus lateralis, vastus medialis and rectus femoris during the Monopodal Squat, Forward Lunge and Lateral Step-Up exercises. PLoS One. 2020;15(4):e0230841. PMID 32236133.
- [anatomical, verified] Holding the non-stance leg in front (pistol style) produces greater hip extensor moment and less knee extensor moment than holding it behind. — Khuu A, Foch E, Lewis CL. Not all single leg squats are equal: a biomechanical comparison of three variations. Int J Sports Phys Ther. 2016;11(2):201-211. PMID 27104053.
- [emg, verified] Modified single-leg squat is classed as a very-high glute max activation exercise. — Neto WK, Soares EG, Vieira TL, et al. Gluteus maximus activation during common strength and hypertrophy exercises: a systematic review. J Sports Sci Med. 2020;19(1):195-203. PMID 32132843.

Notes: Kettlebell_Pistol_Squat: current delt_front 0.5 removed (the bell is a counterbalance held isometrically). Smith_Machine_Pistol_Squat: current erectors 0.5 removed because the fixed bar removes the balance and trunk demand. Free-leg hip flexors/RF work hard isometrically in a pistol but are not credited per brief; see open questions.

### Lateral (side) split squat / Cossack squat

`lateral_squat` · 1 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Frontal-plane lunge: one leg squats while the other stays straight and abducted. The working leg's quads and glutes extend the knee and hip; the adductors of both legs are loaded (stretched on the straight leg, hip-extending on the bent leg) and glute med controls the pelvis.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| quads | 1 {quads_rf 0.1, quads_vasti 0.9} | Knee extension of the bent leg is the prime mover. |
| adductors | 0.75 | Adductors are the distinguishing feature of the lateral lunge: stretched under load on the trailing leg and acting as hip extensors on the working leg; strong synergist (raised from current 0.25). |
| glutes | 0.75 {glute_max 0.6, glute_med 0.4} | Hip extension plus frontal-plane control; glute med is high in side-step lunges (Bouillon 2012). |
| erectors | 0.25 | Barbell on the back with a forward-inclined trunk at moderate loads; minor credit (lowered from current 0.5). |

Exercises: Barbell Side Split Squat.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] In the side-step lunge glute max and glute med produced higher activity than trunk, hip flexor or hamstring muscles. — Bouillon LE, Wilhelm J, Eisel P, Wiesner J, Rachow M, Hatteberg L. Electromyographic assessment of muscle activity between genders during unilateral weight-bearing tasks using adjusted distances. Int J Sports Phys Ther. 2012;7(6):595-605. PMID 23316423.
- [emg, verified] Wide-stance hip extension raises adductor activity (adductor longus/magnus) relative to a shoulder-width stance; the lateral lunge is the extreme case of a wide stance. — McCaw ST, Melrose DR. Stance width and bar load effects on leg muscle activity during the parallel squat. Med Sci Sports Exerc. 1999;31(3):428-436. PMID 10188748.
- [anatomical, likely] During a lateral lunge the trailing-leg adductors are lengthened under load and the lead-leg adductor magnus acts as a hip extensor from deep hip flexion, so both limbs' adductors are loaded eccentrically or concentrically. — Anatomical reasoning (adductor magnus hip-extension moment arm at high flexion angles).

Notes: Adductors raised to 0.75 because this is the only knee-dominant exercise in the batch that loads them through length. No direct EMG of adductors in loaded lateral lunges was found in PubMed; tier-3 reasoning fills the gap.

### Cable hip adduction

`hip_adduction` · 1 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Standing single-leg hip adduction against a low cable. Isolates the adductor group (longus, brevis, magnus, gracilis); no knee action.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| adductors | 1 | Single-joint adduction; adductors are the only prime mover. Current mapping to quads was a placement error. |

Exercises: Cable Hip Adduction.

Evidence:

- [anatomical, likely] Hip adduction is produced by adductor longus, brevis, magnus, pectineus and gracilis; the quadriceps have no adduction moment arm. — Anatomical reasoning (standard functional anatomy of the hip).
- [hypertrophy, verified] The adductors are a trainable, hypertrophy-responsive group (adductor volume +6.2% from squat training), so an isolation movement warrants full credit. — Kubo K, Ikebukuro T, Yata H. Effects of squat training with different depths on lower limb muscle volumes. Eur J Appl Physiol. 2019;119(9):1933-1942. PMID 31230110.

Notes: Misfiled in the quads batch; current quads 1.0 replaced with adductors 1.0. The stance-leg glute med works isometrically and is not credited.

### Banded hip flexion

`hip_flexion` · 1 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Standing or supine hip flexion against a band with the knee extended or bent. Prime movers are iliopsoas and the biarticular rectus femoris; no knee extension torque is required.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| hip_flexors | 1 | Iliopsoas (and TFL/sartorius) are the primary hip flexors; single-joint hip flexion. |
| quads | 0.5 {quads_rf 1} | Rectus femoris is a major hip flexor and is highly responsive to hip-angle manipulation (Larsen 2024); the vasti do not cross the hip and get no credit. Half credit, all to the RF region. |

Exercises: Hip Flexion with Band.

Evidence:

- [hypertrophy, verified] Rectus femoris hypertrophy depends strongly on hip position (greater growth with the hip less flexed), showing RF is trained through its hip-flexion function, not only knee extension. — Larsen S, Sandvik Kristiansen B, Swinton PA, et al. The effects of hip flexion angle on quadriceps femoris muscle hypertrophy in the leg extension exercise. J Sports Sci. 2024;42(24):2400-2408. PMID 39699974.
- [hypertrophy, verified] High-volume loaded hip flexor stretching increased rectus femoris thickness proximally and distally, with inconsistent VL effects, confirming RF responds to hip-flexor-directed loading. — Siegel SD, Sproll M, Lohmann LH, et al. Stretch-mediated hypertrophy and strength increases and their impact on dynamic balance performance - a randomized controlled intervention study. Sci Rep. 2026;16(1). PMID 41796242.
- [emg, verified] Rectus femoris produced its highest EMG in hip-flexion tasks (double leg raise, sit-up) among common rehabilitation exercises. — Khaiyat OA, Norris J. Electromyographic activity of selected trunk, core, and thigh muscles in commonly used exercises for ACL rehabilitation. J Phys Ther Sci. 2018;30(4):642-648. PMID 29706723.

Notes: Misfiled in the quads batch. hip_flexors is an opt-in group (off by default), so for most users the visible effect is the RF half-credit. The vasti get an explicit 0 share so the region check does not flag them.

## shoulder isolation
