| forearms | 1 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2} | Gripping and pinching are finger/thumb flexion; extensors co-contract to hold the wrist in slight extension (the position of strongest grip). Plate_Pinch's current 0.7/0.3 nudged to 0.8/0.2 to match the hand squeeze; the squeeze's current 0.5/0.5 changed. |

Exercises: Plate Pinch, Standing Olympic Plate Hand Squeeze, Wrist Roller.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Standing Olympic Plate Hand Squeeze**: forearms 1 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2} — Arm hangs straight and the plate is squeezed by the fingers: no elbow flexion occurs, so the current biceps 0.5 credit is removed.
- **Wrist Roller**: forearms 1 {forearm_flexors 0.6, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.4}; delt_front 0.25 — Rolling the cord up with palms down is repeated wrist extension, rolling it back down (or palms-up) is wrist flexion, and the grip is continuous: both compartments work, flexor-biased. Arms held horizontally for 30-90 s is a real anterior-deltoid isometric, but per the brief isometric stabilisers get minor credit only, so delt_front drops from 0.5 to 0.25.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Power-grip efforts strongly activate flexor carpi radialis, palmaris longus, flexor digitorum and other anterior-compartment muscles, with the extensors active as wrist stabilisers. — Bader J, Boland MR, Greybe D, Nitz A, Uhl T, Pienkowski D. Muscle activity during maximal isometric forearm rotation using a power grip. J Biomech. 2018;68:24-32. PMID 29305049.
- [emg, verified] Forearm flexor and extensor EMG both rise as grip becomes harder (fat grips) in loaded holds/pulls, and grip becomes the limiting factor. — Krings BM, Shepherd BD, Swain JC, et al. Impact of fat grip attachments on muscular strength and neuromuscular activation during resistance exercise. J Strength Cond Res. 2021;35(2S):S152-S157. PMID 30694963.
- [anatomical, likely] Pinch grip is produced by flexor pollicis longus, adductor pollicis and the finger flexors; support grip by FDS/FDP with the wrist held in extension by ECRL/ECRB for optimal flexor length. — Neumann DA. Kinesiology of the Musculoskeletal System. 3rd ed. Elsevier; 2017 (hand chapter).

Notes: Farmer carries are not in this batch but belong here (forearms 1.0 {0.8/0.2}, traps_upper 0.5, upper_back 0.25) if encountered. No hypertrophy study of grip-only training was found; PubMed searches returned only correlational forearm-thickness/grip-strength work.

### Bottoms-up kettlebell clean (hang)

`kettlebell_bottoms_up_clean` · 1 exercises · confidence low, unverified

Hip-hinge hang clean into an inverted (bottoms-up) rack: explosive hip extension and shrug propel the bell, then a maximal crush grip and forearm/shoulder stabilisation keep it balanced upside down. The grip, not the legs, limits the load.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| forearms | 1 {forearm_flexors 0.8, forearm_extensors_brachioradialis 0.2} | Holding the bell inverted requires near-maximal crush grip on every rep (McGill & Marshall 2012 chose the bottoms-up carry specifically for its grip/stiffening demand); this is the exercise's limiting muscle. Split changed from 0.7/0.3 to 0.8/0.2: crush grip is finger flexion, extensors stabilise the wrist. |
| glutes | 0.5 {glute_max 0.9, glute_med 0.1} | Each rep is driven by a hang-position hip snap; kettlebell hinge patterns show high hip-extensor activity (Zebis 2013; McGill 2012). |
| hamstrings | 0.5 {hamstrings_hip_ext 1} | Hip-extension half of the hinge; knee stays nearly straight so no short-head involvement. |
| traps_upper | 0.5 | The pull finishes with a shrug (upper trapezius shrug activity is characteristic of the clean pull, Fukuda 2021); half credit because the bell is light. |
| delt_front | 0.25 | Anterior deltoid and rotator cuff stabilise the inverted bell in the rack; minor isometric credit. Rotator-cuff work has no group and is folded here rather than delt_rear because the rack position is shoulder flexion, not external rotation. |
| biceps | 0.25 {biceps 0.4, brachialis 0.6} | A small arm pull/curl guides the bell into the rack; neutral grip biases brachialis. Reduced from current 0.5 and rebalanced. |

Exercises: Bottoms-Up Clean From The Hang Position.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] The bottoms-up kettlebell carry was studied as a grip- and stiffness-demanding variant; kettlebell swings/cleans create a rapid hip-hinge pattern with high gluteal and spinal-extensor activation. — McGill SM, Marshall LW. Kettlebell swing, snatch, and bottoms-up carry: back and hip muscle activation, motion, and low back loads. J Strength Cond Res. 2012;26(1):16-27. PMID 21997449.
- [emg, verified] Kettlebell hinge/swing patterns load the hamstrings (semitendinosus 73-115% MVC) at very high levels. — Zebis MK, Skotte J, Andersen CH, et al. Kettlebell swing targets semitendinosus and supine leg curl targets biceps femoris: an EMG study with rehabilitation implications. Br J Sports Med. 2013;47(18):1192-1198. PMID 22736206.
- [emg, verified] Shrug motion and upper-trapezius activity are integral to the second pull of the power clean. — Fukuda DH, et al. Characteristics of the shrug motion and trapezius muscle activity during the power clean. J Strength Cond Res. 2021;35(11):3288-3295. PMID 31453932.
- [emg, unverified] Bottoms-up kettlebell positions increase shoulder-stabiliser (deltoid/rotator cuff) activation relative to conventional holds. — Shoulder muscular activation during common kettlebell exercises: a randomized cross-sectional surface EMG study. Physiother Theory Pract. 2026 (online ahead of print). PMID 41944385.

Notes: Versus current (forearms 1.0, biceps 0.5, delt_front 0.5): hip-hinge credit added because a clean from the hang is a hip-driven lift; biceps and delt_front reduced. The 2026 kettlebell shoulder-EMG paper's abstract did not state per-muscle results, so it is marked unverified.

## triceps

### Cable / machine pushdowns (arm at side)

`cable_pushdown` · 6 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Elbow extension with the upper arm pinned at or slightly in front of the torso (shoulder ~0-45 deg flexion). Includes bar, rope, V-bar, reverse-grip and one-arm pushdowns and the seated machine extension with arm pads. Isolation; no other group receives meaningful hypertrophic stimulus.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| triceps | 1 {triceps_long 0.4, triceps_lateral_medial 0.6} | Sole mover. Long head is moderately shortened at the shoulder but still contributes substantially at 0 deg shoulder elevation (Kholinne 2018) and grew +19.6% vs +10.5% for the monoarticular heads in the neutral-arm cable condition of Maeo 2023, so the long-head share should sit above the compound-press anchor (.3) but below the overhead anchor (.7). Changed from current .3/.7. |

Exercises: Cable One Arm Tricep Extension, Machine Triceps Extension, Reverse Grip Triceps Pushdown, Triceps Pushdown, Triceps Pushdown - Rope Attachment, Triceps Pushdown - V-Bar Attachment.

Evidence:

- [hypertrophy, verified] 12 wk cable elbow extension in the neutral (arm-at-side, pushdown-like) position grew the long head +19.6% and lateral+medial heads +10.5%; overhead position grew them +28.5% and +14.6% (1.45x and 1.39x more). Arm position scaled total stimulus almost uniformly across heads rather than redistributing it. — Maeo S, Wu Y, Huang M, et al. Triceps brachii hypertrophy is substantially greater after elbow extension training performed in the overhead versus neutral arm position. Eur J Sport Sci. 2023;23(7):1240-1250. PMID 35819335.
- [emg, verified] At 0 deg shoulder elevation the long head produced significantly higher force and sEMG than lateral and medial heads; at >=90 deg elevation the medial head dominated. — Kholinne E, Zulkarnain RF, Sun YC, Lim S, Chun JM, Jeon IH. The different role of each head of the triceps brachii muscle in elbow extension. Acta Orthop Traumatol Turc. 2018;52(3):201-205. PMID 29503079.
- [emg, verified] Triceps EMG during pressing was not influenced by forearm supination/pronation (triceps has no forearm-rotation function), supporting identical head mapping for reverse-grip, rope and bar handles. — Lehman GJ. The influence of grip width and forearm pronation/supination on upper-body myoelectric activity during the flat bench press. J Strength Cond Res. 2005;19(3):587-591. PMID 16095407.
- [consensus, likely] Bench dips, triangle push-ups and kickbacks produced the highest triceps EMG among 8 exercises; rope pushdowns and bar pushdowns were mid-ranked (~74% and ~67% of triangle push-up). Non-peer-reviewed ACE report. — Boehler B, Porcari JP, Kline D, Hendrix R, Foster C. ACE-sponsored research: Best triceps exercises. ACE Certified News, 2011 (University of Wisconsin-La Crosse).

Notes: Current mapping already isolates triceps correctly; only the region split changes (.3/.7 -> .4/.6). Reverse-grip pushdown is popularly said to hit the medial head; there is no evidence for that, so it shares the family mapping. Machine_Triceps_Extension (arm pads, shoulder ~30-60 deg flexed) already had .4/.6 and stays there. Cable_One_Arm_Tricep_Extension is a standing supinated one-arm pushdown per its description.

### Overhead / incline triceps extensions (French press)

`overhead_extension` · 16 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Elbow extension with the shoulder flexed >=120 deg (upper arm beside the head, or lying/incline with the bar travelling behind the head). Long head is at long length across the whole rep. Covers dumbbell, barbell, EZ, kettlebell, rope/cable, band, sled, towel, kneeling-cable and incline-bench versions, uni- and bilateral.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| triceps | 1 {triceps_long 0.7, triceps_lateral_medial 0.3} | Fixed anchor row (Standing_Dumbbell_Triceps_Extension). Overhead position produced the largest long-head growth of any studied position (Maeo 2023). No credit to delt_front, abs, chest or forearms: holding the humerus overhead is isometric shoulder flexion with no demonstrated hypertrophic stimulus. |

Exercises: Cable Incline Triceps Extension, Cable Rope Overhead Triceps Extension, Dumbbell One-Arm Triceps Extension, Incline Barbell Triceps Extension, Kettlebell Overhead Triceps Extension, Kneeling Cable Triceps Extension, Lying Close-Grip Barbell Triceps Extension Behind The Head, Seated Triceps Press, Sled Overhead Triceps Extension, Speed Band Overhead Triceps, Standing Dumbbell Triceps Extension, Standing Low-Pulley One-Arm Triceps Extension, Standing One-Arm Dumbbell Triceps Extension, Standing Overhead Barbell Triceps Extension, Standing Towel Triceps Extension, Triceps Overhead Extension with Rope.

Evidence:

- [hypertrophy, verified] Overhead cable extension produced 1.5x greater long-head and 1.4x greater whole-triceps volume gain than the neutral-arm position over 12 weeks despite 34-39% lower absolute loads. — Maeo S, Wu Y, Huang M, et al. Triceps brachii hypertrophy is substantially greater after elbow extension training performed in the overhead versus neutral arm position. Eur J Sport Sci. 2023;23(7):1240-1250. PMID 35819335.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Single-joint lying barbell triceps extension increased long-head CSA significantly more than bench press training, confirming that shoulder-flexed extension work biases the biarticular head. — Brandao L, de Salles Painelli V, Lasevicius T, et al. Varying the order of combinations of single- and multi-joint exercises differentially affects resistance training adaptations. J Strength Cond Res. 2020;34(5):1254-1263. PMID 32149887.
- [emg, verified] Overhead and lying dumbbell extensions showed the same long/lateral head activation pattern across ROM thirds in trained men (concentric > eccentric; initial and middle ROM > final), i.e. handle/implement does not change the pattern. — Alves D, Matta T, Oliveira L. Effect of shoulder position on triceps brachii heads activity in dumbbell elbow extension exercises. J Sports Med Phys Fitness. 2018;58(9):1247-1252. PMID 28677940.
- [anatomical, verified] The triceps long head is a shoulder extensor whose moment arm and shoulder-extension moment increase as the shoulder flexes toward 80 deg and beyond; overhead positions therefore load it at long length. — Landin D, Thompson M. The shoulder extension function of the triceps brachii. J Electromyogr Kinesiol. 2011;21(1):161-165. PMID 20934352.

Notes: Current mapping is already .7/.3 for most members; overrides below only strip unjustified secondary credits (delt_front, abs, chest, forearms) and lift the one-arm/kneeling versions that were mis-set to .4/.6. Incline (45-75 deg bench, upper arm ~25 deg from floor) and lying behind-the-head versions have the shoulder at 120-180 deg flexion, so they share the overhead split. Speed_Band_Overhead_Triceps is normally done for speed with light bands; the set-hardness rule handles that, not the mapping.

### Lying / skullcrusher extensions (upper arm vertical)

`lying_extension_skullcrusher` · 16 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Elbow extension lying supine (flat or decline) with the humerus roughly perpendicular to the torso (shoulder ~90 deg flexion), bar/dumbbell/cable/band/chain lowered to forehead, chin or beside the ears. Also the bodyweight lever versions (Body Tricep Press on a racked bar, Body-Up from forearm plank) and the elbows-out Tate press.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| triceps | 1 {triceps_long 0.5, triceps_lateral_medial 0.5} | Shoulder at ~90 deg puts the long head at intermediate length: between the pushdown (.4) and overhead (.7) splits. Brandao 2020 showed the lying barbell extension grows the long head more than bench press and the lateral head less, so it must sit well above the .3 compound anchor. Current .5/.5 kept. No forearm, chest or delt credit (grip and shoulder are static). |

Exercises: Band Skull Crusher, Body Tricep Press, Body-Up, Cable Lying Triceps Extension, Chain Handle Extension, Decline Dumbbell Triceps Extension, Decline EZ Bar Triceps Extension, Dumbbell Tricep Extension -Pronated Grip, EZ-Bar Skullcrusher, Low Cable Triceps Extension, Lying Close-Grip Barbell Triceps Press To Chin, Lying Dumbbell Tricep Extension, Lying Triceps Press, One Arm Pronated Dumbbell Triceps Extension, One Arm Supinated Dumbbell Triceps Extension, Tate Press.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Body-Up**: triceps 1 {triceps_long 0.4, triceps_lateral_medial 0.6}; abs 0.25 — Forearm-plank to straight-arm press: shoulder stays near 0-45 deg flexion (pushdown-like split) and the whole set is a loaded plank, so a minor abs stabiliser credit (0.25, down from 0.5) is kept; forearms 0.5 removed (grip is passive).
- **Tate Press**: triceps 1 {triceps_long 0.35, triceps_lateral_medial 0.65} — Elbows flared, dumbbells lowered to the chest with the humerus abducted rather than flexed: the long head is not lengthened, so the split moves toward the monoarticular heads. Chest 0.5 and delt_front 0.5 removed; the pecs only assist the final few degrees (tier 3/4).

Evidence:

- [hypertrophy, verified] Lying barbell triceps press (SJ) produced greater long-head CSA gains than bench press (MJ), while bench press produced greater lateral-head gains; medial head similar. — Brandao L, de Salles Painelli V, Lasevicius T, et al. Varying the order of combinations of single- and multi-joint exercises differentially affects resistance training adaptations. J Strength Cond Res. 2020;34(5):1254-1263. PMID 32149887.
- [hypertrophy, verified] 12 wk of lying triceps extension produced non-uniform triceps hypertrophy whose regional pattern matched T2-MRI activation in the first session (less activation and growth distally), showing per-exercise activation maps onto growth. — Wakahara T, Miyamoto N, Sugisaki N, et al. Association between regional differences in muscle activation in one session of resistance exercise and in muscle hypertrophy after resistance training. Eur J Appl Physiol. 2012;112(4):1569-1576. PMID 21858666.
- [emg, verified] Overhead and lying dumbbell extensions produced the same long- and lateral-head activation pattern across the ROM, so implement (bar vs dumbbell vs cable) and grip do not change the family mapping. — Alves D, Matta T, Oliveira L. Effect of shoulder position on triceps brachii heads activity in dumbbell elbow extension exercises. J Sports Med Phys Fitness. 2018;58(9):1247-1252. PMID 28677940.
- [anatomical, verified] Long-head shoulder-extension moment rises steeply between 0 and 80 deg shoulder flexion, so at ~90 deg the long head is meaningfully lengthened but less than overhead. — Landin D, Thompson M. The shoulder extension function of the triceps brachii. J Electromyogr Kinesiol. 2011;21(1):161-165. PMID 20934352.

Notes: Forearm credit on EZ-Bar_Skullcrusher removed (no evidence the static grip is a hypertrophic stimulus). Chest/delt_front credit on Lying_Dumbbell_Tricep_Extension removed (upper arm is stationary). One_Arm_Pronated/Supinated lying extensions and the pronated-grip dumbbell version share the family mapping because forearm rotation does not alter triceps head recruitment. Decline versions keep .5/.5: on a decline the humerus is a few degrees less flexed relative to the torso, too small to change emphasis. Chain_Handle_Extension and Low_Cable_Triceps_Extension are lying extensions per their descriptions. Body_Tricep_Press is a bodyweight skullcrusher on a racked bar and shares the default.

### Triceps kickbacks (bent-over, shoulder extended)

`triceps_kickback` · 5 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Elbow extension with the torso bent forward and the humerus held in extension (behind the trunk line). The long head is at its shortest shoulder length and is also working isometrically as a shoulder extensor. One- and two-arm, standing and seated.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| triceps | 1 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} | Whole-muscle EMG is high (ACE 2011) but the long head is shortened at the shoulder, and short-length training produced the least growth in Maeo 2023, so the long-head share is set to the low end of the scale. No delt_rear/delt_front credit: holding the arm extended is an isometric posterior-deltoid task with no hypertrophy evidence. |

Exercises: Seated Bent-Over One-Arm Dumbbell Triceps Extension, Seated Bent-Over Two-Arm Dumbbell Triceps Extension, Standing Bent-Over One-Arm Dumbbell Triceps Extension, Standing Bent-Over Two-Arm Dumbbell Triceps Extension, Tricep Dumbbell Kickback.

Evidence:

- [consensus, likely] Kickbacks were among the three highest-EMG triceps exercises (~87-88% of the triangle push-up value) in 15 women; non-peer-reviewed ACE report. — Boehler B, Porcari JP, Kline D, Hendrix R, Foster C. ACE-sponsored research: Best triceps exercises. ACE Certified News, 2011 (University of Wisconsin-La Crosse).
- [hypertrophy, verified] Triceps training at the neutral/short long-head length produced ~30% less hypertrophy than at long length; kickbacks place the long head even shorter than the neutral position. — Maeo S, Wu Y, Huang M, et al. Triceps brachii hypertrophy is substantially greater after elbow extension training performed in the overhead versus neutral arm position. Eur J Sport Sci. 2023;23(7):1240-1250. PMID 35819335.
- [anatomical, verified] Long-head shoulder-extension moment is smallest at 0 deg shoulder angle and rises with flexion, so in the extended kickback position the long head is at a length-tension disadvantage. — Landin D, Thompson M. The shoulder extension function of the triceps brachii. J Electromyogr Kinesiol. 2011;21(1):161-165. PMID 20934352.
- [emg, verified] Kinetic/EMG analysis of the rest-pause kickback in women: triceps is the sole significant contributor; heavier dumbbells compromised form rather than raising triceps contraction proportionally. — Serbest K, Eroglu M, Dereshgi HA. Effects of dumbbell weight on the rest-pause triceps kickback exercise in women: kinetic, finite element and EMG analyses. Phys Eng Sci Med. 2025. PMID 41222890.

Notes: Current triceps split (.3/.7 or .4/.6) is broadly right; standardised on .3/.7. The delt_front 0.5 on Standing_Bent-Over_One-Arm was a guess and is removed (the shoulder is extended, not flexed).

### Close-grip and reverse-grip bench presses

`close_grip_bench_press` · 5 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Full-ROM supine press with hands at or inside shoulder width and elbows tucked (barbell, EZ, dumbbells, Smith). Triceps become the limiting mover but pectoralis major activation is largely preserved.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| triceps | 1 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} | Narrow grip raises triceps EMG (Lehman 2005, Barnett 1995) and bench pressing grows the lateral head preferentially (Brandao 2020); same split as the bench anchor. |
| chest | 0.75 {chest_upper 0.35, chest_mid 0.45, chest_lower 0.2} | Pec EMG and the PM/TB ratio are essentially unchanged by grip width (Saeterbakken 2017, Tanimoto 2023) and 6RM loads are only 6-11% lower, so the chest is a strong synergist, not half-credit. Narrow, tucked grip shifts emphasis toward the clavicular head (Barnett 1995) and away from the sternocostal (Lehman 2005). Raised from current 0.5. |
| delt_front | 0.5 | Same anterior-deltoid role as the bench anchor. |

Exercises: Close-Grip Barbell Bench Press, Close-Grip Dumbbell Press, Close-Grip EZ-Bar Press, Reverse Triceps Bench Press, Smith Machine Close-Grip Bench Press.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Reverse Triceps Bench Press**: triceps 1 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7}; chest 0.75 {chest_upper 0.5, chest_mid 0.35, chest_lower 0.15}; delt_front 0.5 — Supinated grip increases clavicular pec activity and removes the narrow-grip drop in sternocostal activity (Lehman 2005); biceps activity rises but as a stabiliser, so no biceps credit.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Moving from wide to narrow grip increased triceps activity and decreased sternocostal pec activity; a supinated grip increased clavicular pec and biceps activity; triceps increase with narrow grip was unaffected by supination. — Lehman GJ. The influence of grip width and forearm pronation/supination on upper-body myoelectric activity during the flat bench press. J Strength Cond Res. 2005;19(3):587-591. PMID 16095407.
- [emg, verified] In competitive bench pressers, narrow vs medium/wide grip produced no significant difference in pectoralis, anterior deltoid or triceps EMG (only 26-31% lower biceps), with 6RM loads 5.8-11.1% lower. — Saeterbakken AH, Mo DA, Scott S, Andersen V. The effects of bench press variations in competitive athletes on muscle activity and performance. J Hum Kinet. 2017;57:61-71. PMID 28713459.
- [emg, verified] The pectoralis major / triceps brachii EMG contribution ratio did not differ between 40 cm and 81 cm grips in trained or untrained men across concentric/eccentric and fatigue conditions. — Tanimoto M, Arakawa H, Sato M, Nagano A. Lateral force and EMG activity in wide- and narrow-grip bench press in various conditions. Sports (Basel). 2023;11(8):154. PMID 37624134.
- [emg, likely] Narrow hand spacing increased triceps (and clavicular pec) EMG relative to wide spacing across bench inclinations in six trained men. — Barnett C, Kippers V, Turner P. Effects of variations of the bench press exercise on the EMG activity of five shoulder muscles. J Strength Cond Res. 1995;9(4):222-227.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Bench press training grew the triceps lateral head significantly more than single-joint extension, supporting the .3/.7 split for pressing. — Brandao L, de Salles Painelli V, Lasevicius T, et al. Varying the order of combinations of single- and multi-joint exercises differentially affects resistance training adaptations. J Strength Cond Res. 2020;34(5):1254-1263. PMID 32149887.
- [emg, verified] Close-grip bench press elicited clavicular pec, anterior deltoid and long-head triceps EMG comparable to the standard bench press across barbell and Smith machine modes. — Bezerra ES, Schoenfeld BJ, Paz G, et al. Effect of exercise order with barbell and machine modalities on upper body volume load and myoelectric activity. Sports Biomech. 2019;19(6):778-791. PMID 30274549.

Notes: Chest raised from 0.5 to 0.75 for the whole family; distribution shifted toward the upper/clavicular fibres for the tucked-elbow grip. Smith and dumbbell versions share the barbell mapping (Bezerra 2019 found near-identical activation for barbell vs Smith).

### Close-grip / triangle push-ups

`close_grip_pushup` · 3 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Push-ups with hands inside shoulder width (on a dumbbell, in a triangle, or elevated on a bench). Closed-chain equivalent of the close-grip bench press; narrow hand base raises both triceps and pec EMG.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| triceps | 1 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} | Narrow base gives the highest triceps EMG of any hand position (Cogley 2005; triangle push-up was the ACE top exercise). Pressing pattern uses the same .3/.7 split as the bench anchor. |
| chest | 0.75 {chest_upper 0.3, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.2} | Pec EMG also rises with the narrow base (Cogley 2005) and push-up prime-mover activation matches bench press at matched load (Gottschall 2018), so chest keeps strong-synergist credit like the close-grip bench. |
| delt_front | 0.5 | Anterior deltoid is a consistent prime mover in push-up EMG reviews (Kowalski 2021). |

Exercises: Close-Grip Push-Up off of a Dumbbell, Incline Push-Up Close-Grip, Push-Ups - Close Triceps Position.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Incline Push-Up Close-Grip**: triceps 1 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7}; chest 0.5 {chest_upper 0.15, chest_mid 0.45, chest_lower 0.4}; delt_front 0.5 — Hands elevated: the press direction relative to the torso is decline-like (lower/costal pec bias) and the load is reduced, so chest credit drops to 0.5.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Narrow-base push-ups produced greater pectoralis major and triceps brachii EMG than wide-base push-ups in 40 subjects. — Cogley RM, Archambault TA, Fibeger JF, Koverman MM, Youdas JW, Hollman JH. Comparison of muscle activation using various hand positions during the push-up exercise. J Strength Cond Res. 2005;19(3):628-633. PMID 16095413.
- [emg, verified] Prime-mover activation did not differ between push-up and bench press at normalised load; narrow hand positions yielded greater activation; only core activation differed (higher in push-up). — Gottschall JS, Hastings B, Becker Z. Muscle activity patterns do not differ between push-up and bench press exercises. J Appl Biomech. 2018;34(6):442-447. PMID 29809073.
- [emg, verified] Across 30 studies, triceps and pectoralis major had the highest EMG amplitudes in standard and incline push-ups; anterior deltoid is a consistent secondary mover. — Kowalski KL, Connelly DM, Jakobi JM, Sadi J. Shoulder electromyography activity during push-up variations: a scoping review. Shoulder Elbow. 2022;14(3):326-340. PMID 35599715.
- [emg, verified] Floor push-ups activate rectus abdominis and obliques only at stabiliser levels compared with suspended variants; abdominal stimulus is a stability effect, not a hypertrophic dose. — Calatayud J, Borreani S, Colado JC, Martin F, Rogers ME. Muscle activity levels in upper-body push exercises with different loads and stability conditions. Phys Sportsmed. 2014;42(4):106-119. PMID 25419894.

Notes: Abs 0.5 on Close-Grip_Push-Up_off_of_a_Dumbbell removed: rectus abdominis works as an isometric anti-extension stabiliser (Calatayud 2014, Gottschall 2018) and crediting it would inflate abs coverage for every pressing set. Chest raised from 0.5 to 0.75 for floor versions to match the close-grip bench family.

### Dips (bar, ring, machine, bench)

`dips` · 6 exercises · confidence medium, unverified

Closed-chain press from shoulder extension back to neutral with elbow extension. Upright-torso, elbows-in versions are triceps-dominant; forward-lean bar and ring dips load the pecs heavily; bench dips are low-load and triceps-dominant with large shoulder extension.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| triceps | 1 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} | Triceps is the primary mover in upright/tucked dips (McKenzie 2022: 'triceps dip' label appropriate). The shoulder is extended behind the trunk so the long head is shortened and works as an antagonist to the concentric shoulder flexion, hence the low long-head share. |
| chest | 0.5 {chest_upper 0.05, chest_mid 0.35, chest_lower 0.6} | Pectoralis major is an agonist in every dip variant (McKenzie 2022a, 2022b) and, from the extended-shoulder start, the costal/lower sternal fibres do the shoulder flexion/adduction. Upright triceps versions hold pec credit at half. |
| delt_front | 0.5 | Anterior deltoid is a prime shoulder flexor in bar and ring dips (McKenzie 2022a). |

Exercises: Bench Dips, Dip Machine, Dips - Triceps Version, Parallel Bar Dip, Ring Dips, Weighted Bench Dip.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Parallel Bar Dip**: triceps 1 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7}; chest 0.75 {chest_upper 0.05, chest_mid 0.35, chest_lower 0.6}; delt_front 0.5 — Generic bar dip with self-selected forward lean: pec activation is significantly higher than the bench dip and pec is a co-agonist with triceps (McKenzie 2022a/b), so chest is a strong synergist.
- **Ring Dips**: triceps 1 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7}; chest 0.75 {chest_upper 0.05, chest_mid 0.35, chest_lower 0.6}; delt_front 0.5 — Ring dip produced the greatest pectoralis major (and latissimus) activation of the three variants (McKenzie 2022a) because the arms must also adduct to stabilise the rings; lats not credited (stabiliser).
- **Bench Dips**: triceps 1 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7}; chest 0.25 {chest_upper 0.05, chest_mid 0.35, chest_lower 0.6}; delt_front 0.25 — Feet on the floor remove much of the load; PM and AD activation were low relative to triceps in the bench dip (McKenzie 2022a), so pec and anterior deltoid drop to minor credit.
- **Weighted Bench Dip**: triceps 1 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7}; chest 0.25 {chest_upper 0.05, chest_mid 0.35, chest_lower 0.6}; delt_front 0.25 — Same kinematics as the bench dip (feet supported, upright torso, large shoulder extension); added plate raises triceps load but does not change the pec/deltoid share.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Bar and ring dips produced significantly greater peak pectoralis major, anterior deltoid and triceps activation than the bench dip; ring dip had the highest pec activation; bench dip was relatively triceps-dominant with low PM/AD activation but used ~101% of maximal shoulder extension ROM. — McKenzie A, Crowley-McHattan Z, Meir R, Whitting J, Volschenk W. Bench, bar, and ring dips: do kinematics and muscle activity differ? Int J Environ Res Public Health. 2022;19(20):13211. PMID 36293792.
- [emg, verified] During bar dips to exhaustion, pectoralis major and triceps brachii were the agonists whose peak activation increased with fatigue; the exercise 'appears to target pectoralis major and triceps brachii effectively'. — McKenzie A, Crowley-McHattan Z, Meir R, Whitting J, Volschenk W. Fatigue increases muscle activations but does not change maximal joint angles during the bar dip. Int J Environ Res Public Health. 2022;19(21):14390. PMID 36361276.
- [consensus, likely] Dips (bench) were among the top three triceps exercises by EMG in the ACE comparison. — Boehler B, Porcari JP, Kline D, Hendrix R, Foster C. ACE-sponsored research: Best triceps exercises. ACE Certified News, 2011 (University of Wisconsin-La Crosse).
- [anatomical, verified] Long head shoulder-extension moment is small near 0 deg and negative shoulder angles, so in the extended-shoulder dip position the long head is shortened and contributes less to elbow extension than the monoarticular heads. — Landin D, Thompson M. The shoulder extension function of the triceps brachii. J Electromyogr Kinesiol. 2011;21(1):161-165. PMID 20934352.

Notes: Current mappings were close; changes are chest 0.75 for bar/ring dips, chest and delt_front 0.25 for bench dips, and a slightly stronger lower-chest bias (.05/.35/.60 vs .1/.4/.5). Dip_Machine (seated, elbows in) keeps the family default. Users who lean far forward on bar dips get more pec than the mapping shows; that is a per-user technique variable we cannot capture.

### Partial-range / lockout presses (board, pin, floor)

`partial_rom_lockout_press` · 6 exercises · confidence low, unverified

Bench-press pattern with the bottom of the ROM removed: board press, pin press, floor press (barbell, dumbbell, one-arm, with chains). The stretched pec portion is absent and the elbow extensors dominate the remaining upper-half range.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| triceps | 1 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} | Upper-half bench ROM gives the highest mean and peak triceps excitation of any ROM condition (Fischer 2025) and elbow extensors become dominant near lockout (Evangelista 2025). Pressing split as per bench anchor. |
| chest | 0.75 {chest_upper 0.25, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.25} | Pec excitation in the upper-half ROM is still at least as high as full ROM (Fischer 2025), but the long-length portion that drives most pec growth is removed (Kassiano 2023), so chest drops from primary to strong synergist. Anchor bench distribution retained (medium grip). |
| delt_front | 0.5 | Anterior deltoid excitation in partial-ROM bench matched or exceeded full ROM (Fischer 2025). |

Exercises: Board Press, Dumbbell Floor Press, Floor Press, Floor Press with Chains, One Arm Floor Press, Pin Presses.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Upper-half ROM bench press elicited the highest mean triceps excitation, and the triceps long and lateral heads showed significantly higher peak EMG in the upper-half ROM than full or lower-half ROM; pec and anterior deltoid mean excitation were also higher in both partials than full ROM. — Fischer J, Burger C, Manieu Seguel JA, Rodoplu C, Paternoster FK, Tilp M, Konrad A. Acute muscle excitation response across various bench press ranges of motion. Sci Rep. 2025;15(1):14105. PMID 40269203.
- [anatomical, verified] Link-chain model of the bench press: elbow extensors contribute minimally in early lift phases and become dominant near full extension; grip width and bar constraint shape the sticking region. — Evangelista P, Rum L, Picerno P, Biscarini A. Decoding the contribution of shoulder and elbow mechanics to barbell kinematics and the sticking region in bench and overhead press exercises. J Funct Morphol Kinesiol. 2025;10(3):322. PMID 40843853.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Systematic review: training at long muscle lengths (full ROM or initial-range partials) favours hypertrophy of most muscles; a mid-range partial elicited greater triceps growth than full ROM in one study (ES 1.21), so removing the stretched pec range costs the pecs more than the triceps. — Kassiano W, Costa B, Nunes JP, Ribeiro AS, Schoenfeld BJ, Cyrino ES. Which ROMs lead to Rome? A systematic review of the effects of range of motion on muscle hypertrophy. J Strength Cond Res. 2023;37(5):1135-1144. PMID 36662126.
- [consensus, likely] Westside-style board, pin and floor presses are programmed specifically as triceps/lockout accessories to the bench press. — Practitioner consensus (Simmons L, Westside Barbell; Tate D, EliteFTS training articles).

Notes: Forearms 0.5, lats 0.5 and upper_back 0.5 on Board_Press and Pin_Presses removed: bar grip, lat 'tightness' and scapular retraction are isometric setup tasks with no hypertrophy evidence. Pin_Presses set 'an inch off the chest' are effectively full ROM and would deserve the bench anchor mapping; the description allows any pin height so the lockout mapping is used. One_Arm_Floor_Press: no oblique credit for the anti-rotation demand (isometric).

### Full-ROM bench press variants (powerlifting style, chains, reverse bands)

`full_rom_bench_variants` · 3 exercises · confidence high, unverified

Complete bench press ROM with a medium-to-wide grip: competition-style arched bench, bench with chains, reverse-band bench. Accommodating resistance changes the load curve but not the muscles that move it.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| chest | 1 {chest_upper 0.25, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.25} | Identical to the fixed Barbell_Bench_Press_-_Medium_Grip anchor: these are bench presses, not triceps exercises, and must colour the chest first. Current mapping (triceps 1.0 / chest 0.5) inherited the seed database's 'triceps primary' label and is wrong. |
| delt_front | 0.5 | Anchor value. |
| triceps | 0.5 {triceps_long 0.3, triceps_lateral_medial 0.7} | Anchor value. Chains and reverse bands overload the lockout, where the triceps dominate (Evangelista 2025), which argues for 0.75, but no study shows a shift in triceps activation or growth with accommodating resistance, so the anchor is kept. |

Exercises: Bench Press - Powerlifting, Bench Press with Chains, Reverse Band Bench Press.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Competition-style wide-grip flat bench press activates pectoralis major, anterior deltoid and triceps with no significant difference from medium grip; lat and posterior-deltoid EMG were recorded but were not affected by variation and reflect stabilisation. — Saeterbakken AH, Mo DA, Scott S, Andersen V. The effects of bench press variations in competitive athletes on muscle activity and performance. J Hum Kinet. 2017;57:61-71. PMID 28713459.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Bench press training produced significant pectoralis major CSA growth and lateral-head triceps growth, but no significant whole-triceps CSA increase when performed alone, supporting chest 1.0 / triceps 0.5. — Brandao L, de Salles Painelli V, Lasevicius T, et al. Varying the order of combinations of single- and multi-joint exercises differentially affects resistance training adaptations. J Strength Cond Res. 2020;34(5):1254-1263. PMID 32149887.
- [anatomical, verified] Elbow extensor torque becomes dominant only near full extension, which is where chains/reverse bands add load; the early and mid lift remain shoulder-torque (pec/deltoid) dominated. — Evangelista P, Rum L, Picerno P, Biscarini A. Decoding the contribution of shoulder and elbow mechanics to barbell kinematics and the sticking region in bench and overhead press exercises. J Funct Morphol Kinesiol. 2025;10(3):322. PMID 40843853.

Notes: These three ids sit in the triceps batch only because free-exercise-db tags them triceps-primary; the anchor row applies verbatim. Forearms 0.5, lats 0.5 and upper_back 0.5 removed (isometric grip/setup). The powerlifting arch turns the press slightly decline-like, which would nudge the chest split toward lower fibres (~.20/.50/.30); the anchor split is kept to avoid an untested deviation.

### JM press and press-to-skullcrusher hybrids

`jm_press_hybrid` · 2 exercises · confidence low, unverified

Barbell lowered toward the chin/throat with elbows driven forward, then pressed: a hybrid between a close-grip bench press and a skullcrusher. Shoulder flexes to ~45-70 deg at the bottom, so the long head is more lengthened than in a close-grip press but less than in a skullcrusher.

| Group | Credit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| triceps | 1 {triceps_long 0.4, triceps_lateral_medial 0.6} | Primary mover; the forward-elbow bar path adds long-head length relative to the close-grip press (.3) without reaching the skullcrusher position (.5). |
| chest | 0.25 {chest_upper 0.3, chest_mid 0.5, chest_lower 0.2} | The press-out portion recruits the pecs, but the bar never reaches the chest and loads used are far below close-grip bench, so only minor credit. Down from current 0.5. |
| delt_front | 0.25 | Anterior deltoid assists the short press-out; minor credit (down from 0.5). |

Exercises: Decline Close-Grip Bench To Skull Crusher, JM Press.

Per-exercise differences:

- **Decline Close-Grip Bench To Skull Crusher**: triceps 1 {triceps_long 0.4, triceps_lateral_medial 0.6}; chest 0.5 {chest_upper 0.1, chest_mid 0.4, chest_lower 0.5}; delt_front 0.5 — Each rep includes a full decline close-grip press to the lower chest plus a skullcrusher, so the pec (lower/costal bias on the decline) and anterior deltoid keep half credit; triceps split averages the two halves.

Evidence:

- [emg, verified] Narrow grip and elbows-forward pressing increase triceps EMG and reduce sternocostal pec activity relative to a standard bench press. — Lehman GJ. The influence of grip width and forearm pronation/supination on upper-body myoelectric activity during the flat bench press. J Strength Cond Res. 2005;19(3):587-591. PMID 16095407.
- [hypertrophy, verified] Bench press grows the lateral head and lying extension grows the long head; a hybrid between the two is expected to sit between the .3/.7 and .5/.5 splits. — Brandao L, de Salles Painelli V, Lasevicius T, et al. Varying the order of combinations of single- and multi-joint exercises differentially affects resistance training adaptations. J Strength Cond Res. 2020;34(5):1254-1263. PMID 32149887.
- [consensus, likely] The JM press was designed by JM Blakley as a triceps lockout builder combining close-grip bench and skullcrusher mechanics; no peer-reviewed EMG or hypertrophy data exist for it. — Practitioner consensus (Blakley JM; Westside Barbell / EliteFTS articles; Onnit Academy 'How to properly do the JM press').

Notes: Lying_Close-Grip_Barbell_Triceps_Press_To_Chin was considered for this family but its description keeps the upper arm stationary (bar to chin by elbow flexion only), so it is a skullcrusher variant and sits in lying_extension_skullcrusher.

## Open questions from the researchers

- (back) London_Bridges: the batch gives no description and I could not confirm the movement from a source. It is kept with the pronated pull-up family (as the current mapping implies). If it is actually an inverted-row-style bar pull, move it to inverted_row.
- (back) Seated_Cable_Rows is logged with any attachment (V-handle, wide bar, rope). The default here assumes the close neutral V-handle. If the product can capture attachment, wide-pronated should use biceps 0.5/0.5 and keep upper_back 1.0, while close-neutral could be promoted to lats 1.0 / upper_back 0.75 like the T-bar (Di Fonza 2026 narrower grip / lower abduction effect).
- (back) One-Arm_Dumbbell_Row and One-Arm_Kettlebell_Row are assumed bench/knee-supported (no erector credit). If the app treats them as unsupported hinge rows, add erectors 0.25 as for the Meadows rows.
- (back) No longitudinal study measuring latissimus or trapezius thickness after pulldown vs row training was found in PubMed (a registered trial, NCT07360236, compares pulldown vs row for lat and forearm-flexor thickness and is not yet reported). All lat/upper-back weights therefore rest on EMG plus the brief's anchors; revisit when that trial publishes.
- (back) Chest 0.25 on chin-ups/close-grip pulldowns and on straight-arm pulldowns is evidence-based (pec 44-57% MVIC in chin-ups) but will colour the chest after a back day. Confirm this is acceptable product behaviour; if not, drop the chest rows in vertical_pull_supinated_neutral and straight_arm_pulldown.
- (back) Forearm credit policy: I gave forearms 0.25 to hanging bodyweight pulls and 0.5 to grip-limited climbs, and none to machine pulldowns or rows. The brief has no anchor for forearms in pulls; confirm whether hanging grip work should colour the forearms at all.
- (back) Renegade row and sled row have no direct EMG studies; their mappings are by analogy (one-arm row plus plank; row plus rope grip).
- (biceps) Forearm credit policy: I gave every curl a forearm credit (0.25 supinated, 0.5 neutral, 0.75 pronated) biased to the brachioradialis region, because brachioradialis is a real elbow flexor with meaningful stimulus, while wrist flexors only stabilise. The current table is inconsistent (some rows 0.5 at 50/50, some none). Confirm the product wants forearms coloured by ordinary supinated curls at all; if not, drop the 0.25 rows and keep only the hammer/reverse/Zottman credits.
- (biceps) Machine_Bicep_Curl was placed in the braced (preacher-style) family assuming an arm-pad machine. If the seed's machine is a free-arm cable-stack curl, move it to the standing family (only the brachialis share changes, 0.3 vs 0.25).
- (biceps) Grip width (close/wide/'inner-biceps') has no verified EMG or hypertrophy evidence for short- vs long-head selectivity, so no overrides were created. If the product later adds a biceps short/long-head region split, revisit with Jarrett 2012 anatomy.
- (biceps) Reverse curls: the 0.75 forearm credit is a judgement call between 'strong synergist' and 'primary mover'; there is no longitudinal brachioradialis hypertrophy study for any curl variant. Coratella 2023 also reports slightly higher brachioradialis excitation with the supinated than pronated grip in bodybuilders, which conflicts with Kleiber 2015 and with practice; I followed Kleiber plus the wrist-extensor load argument.
- (biceps) Drag curl: adds active shoulder extension against load; I left it in the standing family without a delt_rear/lats credit because the extension moment is small relative to the elbow moment. Add delt_rear 0.25 if the product wants that visible.
- (biceps) The ACE 2014 concentration-curl EMG report (Young et al.) is non-peer-reviewed and could not be opened; it is cited only as consensus support and does not drive any number.
- (chest) Product decision on stabiliser credit for push-ups: this batch removes the inconsistent abs 0.5 from Push-Up_Wide and the incline push-up variants (rectus ~13-19% MVC, isometric; Lehman 2006) and keeps abs/obliques only at 0.25 for suspension, unilateral and alternating variants. If the team wants planks and push-ups to colour the abs at all, apply one rule across all closed-chain pressing rather than per exercise.
- (chest) Anterior deltoid credit for the flat press anchor: Lanza 2024 (MRI, 10 weeks bench-only) found AD CSA +15% vs pec +20%, and incline EMG at >=45 deg shows AD >= clavicular pec. The anchors fix delt_front at 0.5; consider whether steep inclines (>=45 deg) or a 0.75 value belong in a future brief revision.
- (chest) Guillotine / Neck Press: the practitioner claim of extra upper-chest stimulus has no EMG or hypertrophy support; both are mapped as wide-grip flat presses (triceps 0.25). A dedicated study would settle whether they deserve an upper tilt.
- (chest) Floor press: no EMG or hypertrophy study on PubMed; the bottom-range cut plausibly reduces the stretched-pec stimulus, but the brief has no ROM modifier, so full chest credit is kept.
- (chest) Pullover lats share: EMG consistently shows pec-dominant activation, but Teixeira 2022 shows strong joint-position dependence; a straight-arm pullover performed only in the top half (arms behind the head) may be more lat-biased than the 0.5 given here. No pullover hypertrophy data exist.
- (chest) Triceps region split in push-ups and dips: the anchors' lateral/medial bias comes from bench data (Brandao 2020). Dips and floor presses work the long head from a shoulder-extended position (Kholinne 2018), so the dip row uses {long .4}; there is no hypertrophy evidence for that shift.
- (chest) Two citations are pre-1999 JSCR papers that PubMed does not index (Barnett 1995; Glass & Armstrong 1997) and one is from the non-indexed Int J Strength Cond (Roy/Arseneault/Sercia 2021); all three were read via publisher or journal pages and are marked 'likely'. The ACE/Schanke 2012 thesis and Campos & Silva 2014 are marked 'unverified' and used only as consensus support.
- (core) Should straight-leg raise variants (hanging leg raise, lying leg raise, seated tucks) credit hip_flexors at 1.0 alongside abs? EMG says hip flexors are the movers; the brief's secondary cap kept them at 0.75.
- (core) Should plain crunches carry the 0.25 oblique credit at all? It is EMG-defensible but adds low-value colour; dropping it to 0 is equally defensible.
- (core) Balance_Board: should pattern 'other' balance/proprioception drills be excluded from volume scoring entirely rather than mapped to calves 1.0?
- (core) Forearm 0.25 on bar-hanging exercises is an isometric grip credit the brief discourages; keep or drop?
- (core) Time-based sets (plank, side bridge, Pallof holds): the engine's repFactor treats <3 reps as 0.5; confirm how held exercises are logged so a 45 s plank is not scored as a 1-rep set.
- (core) Gorilla Chin/Crunch has two 1.0 groups (lats and abs); confirm the engine allows multiple primaries for genuine two-movement compounds.
- (core) No longitudinal regional-hypertrophy studies exist for any core movement; every mapping in this batch is EMG/anatomy-based (tier 2-3).
- (hinge_posterior) Adductor magnus is a major hip extensor in deadlifts, hip thrusts and step-ups (Collings 2026 tier 1) but the only adductor group is the visible inner-thigh 'adductors'. I gave deadlift/step-up 0.25 and sumo 0.75 and left hip thrust/RDL/good morning at 0; decide whether hip-extension work should colour the adductors region at all, and apply one rule across batches (the squat anchor's adductors 0.5 suggests yes).
- (hinge_posterior) Isometric credit policy: deadlifts and cleans credit forearms 0.5, upper_back 0.5, traps_upper 0.5-0.75 and lats 0.25 even though this work is near-isometric; the brief's stabiliser rule would zero it. Confirm the exception or drop these rows batch-wide.
- (hinge_posterior) Olympic-lift derivatives (11 exercises) are power work usually done for 1-5 non-failure reps; the repFactor discount applies but the credit table still counts a set of kettlebell cleans like a set of deadlifts. Consider an implement/pattern-level discount for kettlebell and Smith 'olympic' entries or accept the repFactor as sufficient.
- (hinge_posterior) Lunge_Pass_Through and Step-up_with_Knee_Raise belong to the squat/lunge batch; whatever that batch decides for lunge and step-up should override the mapping here to avoid two different lunge rules.
- (hinge_posterior) Prone/standing leg curl distribution (.5/.5) vs the seated anchor (.6/.4) is based on Maeo 2021 (seated grows biarticular heads more) plus anatomical reasoning; no study measures short-head vs biarticular growth for prone vs seated directly. Keep or collapse to the anchor.
- (hinge_posterior) Hip abduction machine: I set glute_max .3 / glute_med .7 (was .8/.2). Confirm the product wants the abduction machine to colour glute_med strongly; there is no per-region hypertrophy study for it.
- (hinge_posterior) Barbalho 2020 (squat > hip thrust for glute growth) conflicts with Plotkin 2023 (equal); I followed Plotkin (larger, MRI, preregistered) but the disagreement is worth noting in the ADR.
- (quads) Hamstrings 0.25 for lunges, split squats and step-ups: supported by modelled forces (Kipp 2022, Jaeggi 2024) and a walking-lunge strength study (Jonhagen 2009), but no study has measured hamstring hypertrophy from lunges. If the product wants strict parity with the 'no hamstring credit for squats' anchor, drop it to 0.
- (quads) Step-up glutes at 1.0 rests on EMG (Neto 2020) and modelling, not longitudinal hypertrophy. A step-up performed with a trailing-leg push-off would justify 0.75 instead.
- (quads) Isometric stabiliser credit was removed from goblet/overhead/pistol (delt_front), Zercher (biceps), front squat (abs) and Smith pistol (erectors) per the brief. Users may expect shoulder colour on overhead squats; obliques 0.25 was kept on the suitcase deadlift and traps_upper 0.25 on heavy pulls as the only isometric exceptions, flagged for product review.
- (quads) No PubMed record exists for the sissy squat; RF 0.35 is anatomical reasoning extrapolated from Larsen 2024. A regional-hypertrophy study of sissy squats vs leg extensions would settle it.
- (quads) Box squat glutes raised from 0.5 to 0.75 on kinetics (Swinton 2012). If the app treats 'squat to a bench' as a beginner depth cue rather than a Westside box squat, the two 'To_A_Bench' rows could stay on the plain back-squat row (identical numbers except the Dumbbell forearm credit).
- (quads) Trap-bar/leverage deadlift quads 1.0 assumes a high-handle, upright pull. A low-handle trap-bar pull behaves closer to a conventional deadlift (erectors and hamstrings higher); the app has one row per exercise, so the high-handle interpretation was used.
- (quads) Cable_Hip_Adduction and Hip_Flexion_with_Band are not quad exercises and should move to an adductor/hip-flexor batch; they were mapped here so no id is left unassigned.
- (quads) Traps_upper and forearm credit on deadlift variants are consensus, not literature: no study measured trap or forearm hypertrophy from deadlifts.
- (shoulder_isolation) Six seed rows are mislabeled relative to their instructions and should probably be renamed/aliased in the catalogue, not only remapped: Dumbbell_Raise (a dumbbell upright row), Cable_Seated_Lateral_Raise (a seated bent-over cable rear-delt raise), Lying_One-Arm_Lateral_Raise (prone rear-delt raise), Barbell/Dumbbell/Smith_Incline_Shoulder_Raise (scapular protraction), Straight_Raises_on_Incline_Bench (prone front raise), Low_Pulley_Row_To_Neck (seated face pull).
- (shoulder_isolation) Serratus anterior and the rotator cuff have no group. Three families (scapular_protraction, external_rotation, internal_rotation) are mapped entirely to proxies (delt_front/chest, delt_rear, delt_front) as the brief instructs; if a 'shoulder stabilisers' or serratus group is ever added these should move first. Copy for these rows should say the colour reflects cuff/serratus work, not deltoid volume.
- (shoulder_isolation) rear_delt_fly credits delt_side 0.5 on the strength of middle-deltoid EMG in horizontal abduction (63-87% MVIC in Reinold 2004/2007). This is the largest deviation from the current table and from common practice; the reviewer may prefer 0.25. No longitudinal study separates middle- vs posterior-deltoid growth after reverse flyes.
- (shoulder_isolation) The only longitudinal per-exercise deltoid hypertrophy trial found (Larsen 2025, dumbbell vs cable lateral raise) covers only the lateral raise; front raise, upright row, rear-delt fly and face pull mappings rest on EMG plus anatomy. No regional-hypertrophy evidence exists to weight upper-trapezius credit in upright rows or lateral raises.
- (shoulder_isolation) Iron_Cross has no instructions in the seed data; the mapping assumes the common 'squat while sweeping straight arms between front and T positions' description (FitnessVolt/Fitbod). If the catalogue's intended movement differs, remap.
- (shoulder_isolation) Overhead-press triceps distribution: this batch uses {long .3, lateral_medial .7} for jerks/thruster/Cuban press lockouts (long head shortened at the shoulder), the opposite of the overhead-extension anchor {long .7}. The press batch should adopt one convention so kettlebell jerks and barbell presses agree.
- (shoulder_isolation) Kettlebell snatch/clean rows are in this 'shoulder isolation' batch by name only; they were remapped as hip-hinge ballistics (glutes 1.0). If a hinge/olympic batch also covers them, reconcile so each id has one owner.
- (shoulder_isolation) Isometric-hold credit: Car_Drivers (front hold), Sled_Overhead_Backward_Walk and the Turkish get-ups credit the deltoid at 1.0 although the shoulder work is largely isometric. The brief allows this only where the isometric is the exercise's loaded element; confirm the product is comfortable with these counting as full anterior-deltoid sets, or cap them at 0.75.
- (shoulder_press) delt_side weight for front overhead presses: Campos 2020 measured medial deltoid at 27.9% MVIC in the shoulder press vs 30.3% in the lateral raise (anchor delt_side 1.0). A strict EMG reading argues for 0.75; I kept 0.5 on practitioner consensus that OHP is a secondary side-delt builder. Product should decide whether EMG parity with the anchor should win.
- (shoulder_press) traps_upper 0.5 on every press family: each OHP set then contributes half a set to a group with a 0.5 target multiplier, so heavy pressers will show traps_upper covered without any shrug work. If that reads wrong on the map, 0.25 is the conservative alternative; the EMG (Coratella 2022, Isiklar 2026, Kochanowicz 2018) supports meaningful but not primary involvement.
- (shoulder_press) chest 0.25 {upper .8, mid .2, lower 0} for front overhead presses is inferred from Coratella 2022 (front > back pec excitation) and anatomy; no study reports pec % MVIC in a strict OHP relative to a bench press. If this makes chest colour in on shoulder days in a way users dislike, drop it to 0 for the bilateral/unilateral families and keep it only for the landmine family.
- (shoulder_press) Triceps split .4/.6 for all overhead pressing is derived from Kholinne 2018 (n=10, submaximal elbow extension at fixed shoulder angles), not from a pressing study. Note this makes overhead pressing a lateral/medial-head exercise in the table while the overhead-extension anchor stays long-head-biased; confirm this is the intended contrast.
- (shoulder_press) Push press leg credits (quads 0.5, glutes 0.25, calves 0.25) rest on kinetics (Lake 2014) plus anatomy; no EMG or hypertrophy data exist for the push press. If the app wants leg colour only from dedicated leg work, reduce all three to 0.25.
- (shoulder_press) Arnold press: the only direct EMG comparison (Raizada & Bagchi 2017, Indian J Public Health Res Dev) is not indexed in PubMed and could not be opened; the family mapping equals the plain front press and could be merged.
- (shoulder_press) Region distributions containing an explicit 0 share (chest_lower 0, hamstrings_bf_short 0) follow the Romanian_Deadlift anchor's precedent; confirm the validator accepts zeros.
- (small_groups) Seated calf raise anchor: Kinoshita 2023 found essentially no gastrocnemius growth from seated raises; should the anchor 0.2/0.8 move to 0.1/0.9? (Reported under conflictsWithBrief, anchor left unchanged.)
- (small_groups) Forearm credit for heavy holds (shrugs 0.25, rack pulls 0.5): no longitudinal study shows forearm hypertrophy from isometric gripping alone; the weights are a product call between 'grip is the limiting muscle' and the brief's rule against crediting isometric stabilisers. Consistency matters more than the exact value: today Leverage_Shrug and Barbell_Shrug_Behind_The_Back credit forearms while Barbell_Shrug and Dumbbell_Shrug do not.
- (small_groups) Wrist_Roller delt_front: proposed 0.25 (isometric front hold). If the product prefers zero credit for isometric work, drop it; the forearm mapping is unaffected.
- (small_groups) Seated_Head_Harness_Neck_Resistance is mapped as extension (plate hanging in front). Some users wear the harness backwards for flexion; the app cannot tell, so a note in the exercise copy may be warranted.
- (small_groups) No PubMed study exists for rack pulls specifically; the mapping interpolates from deadlift position studies (Moreira 2023, Escamilla 2002). A user-visible note that the erectors' loaded range is short could help set expectations.
- (small_groups) Quads on Rack_Pull_with_Bands: currently 0.5 while plain Rack_Pulls has none; proposal removes both. If the band version is meant as a from-the-floor pull, restore quads 0.25 for it.
- (small_groups) Wrist curls: no hypertrophy trial isolates wrist-flexor training; all distributions are anatomical/EMG. Low priority to research further because the group is 0.5x-multiplied in TARGETS.
- (triceps) Maeo 2023 implies overhead extensions deliver ~1.4x the per-set triceps stimulus of pushdowns in every head. The weight cap of 1.0 cannot express this; should the engine allow a >1.0 'stimulus multiplier' for long-length isolation work, or should the region split (.7/.3 vs .4/.6) remain the only lever?
- (triceps) The head split for arm-at-side pushdowns is genuinely uncertain: Kholinne 2018 (EMG/modelling) and Maeo 2023 (relative growth) both suggest the long head is well recruited at 0 deg shoulder elevation, contrary to bodybuilding consensus. .4/.6 is a compromise; a tier-1 study comparing head-specific growth from pushdowns vs skullcrushers would settle it.
- (triceps) Close-grip bench: Saeterbakken 2017 and Tanimoto 2023 show pec activation essentially unchanged by grip width. Should close-grip presses be chest 1.0 / triceps 0.75 (closer to the anchor) rather than triceps 1.0 / chest 0.75? The current recommendation follows load-limiting muscle and user expectation.
- (triceps) Partial-ROM presses (board/pin/floor) have no longitudinal data; the triceps 1.0 / chest 0.75 mapping is inferred from Fischer 2025 (acute EMG) and Kassiano 2023 (length-dependent growth). Pin height in Pin_Presses is user-defined and can range from lockout-only to near-full ROM.
- (triceps) Bench with chains / reverse bands: should lockout-overload variants bump triceps from 0.5 to 0.75? No activation or hypertrophy study supports a shift, so the anchor was kept.
- (triceps) Stabiliser credits removed across the batch (forearms on skullcrushers/presses, lats and upper_back on bench variants, abs on push-ups, delt_front on overhead and kickback holds). If the product wants stabilisers to receive colour, a uniform 0.25 rule should be decided centrally rather than per exercise.
- (triceps) Barnett 1995 and the ACE/Boehler 2011 report could not be opened as PubMed records (JSCR 1995 is not indexed; ACE is not peer reviewed) and are marked 'likely'.
